\chapter{The K3 Yangian}
\label{ch:k3-yangian}

The K3 Yangian $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})$ is the principal $d = 2$
instance of the bulk: the Drinfeld double of the positive half
$Y^{+}(K3) = H^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{eq}}(\bigsqcup_n \mathrm{Hilb}^n(K3),
\phi_W)$ on the reduced $\mathbb C^{\times} + \Aut(K3)$ chart class,
ordinary chain ambient (Theorem~\ref{thm:bulk-k3-yangian}). The
classical limit of the abelianised mode-Lie algebra is
$\mathfrak{so}(4, 20)$; the $24$ Heisenberg generators,
Mukai-signature Serre relations, and degree-$(24, 24)$ structure
function encode the quantization of the Mukai lattice. This chapter
develops the complete abelian Yangian presentation, the Koszul dual,
the orthogonal Yangian envelope $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$
attached to the Mukai form and its Hodge-parity
$\Z/2$-super-extension $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$, and the
perturbative factorization homology computation. The chapter pins the
trichotomy that distinguishes three form-preserving algebras of rank
$24$: the ungraded orthogonal envelope
$Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$ (classical limit
$\mathfrak{so}(4, 20)$, Kac type $D_{12}$), the programme-specific
Hodge-parity $\Z/2$-super-extension
$Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$ (symmetric on both graded parts,
non-Kac), and Kac $\mathfrak{osp}(4 \mid 20)$ (symplectic on the odd
part, not the Mukai preserver). Only the first two are envelopes for
the K3 Yangian; the third preserves the wrong form on the odd part
and is not an envelope.

\begin{remark}[Three-axis coordinates of this chapter]
\label{rem:k3-yangian-three-axis-coords}
Every theorem of this chapter sits at \emph{level $3$} (the bulk:
positive half $Y^{+}(K3)$, double $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak g_{K3})$, derived
chiral centre on the curve $C$), \emph{chart} reduced
$\mathbb C^{\times} + \Aut(K3)$ on the $K3$ surface with
$(\Sigma_1, C) = (\Sigma_1^{\mathrm{Nakajima}}, C \hookrightarrow K3)$,
and \emph{ordinary chain ambient} on the abelian-at-Lie sector,
$L_\infty$-twisted on the non-abelian Yangian envelope. The K3
Yangian on $K3$ is the bulk attached to the $K3$ Stage-$2$ chiral
shadow $H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$; it is distinct from the
$K3 \times E$ Hall--Drinfeld double $\mathcal D_\hbar(\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3
\times E})$ (Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic}), which is
the bulk attached to the $K3 \times E$ Stage-$2$ shadow.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Stage discipline: \texorpdfstring{$Y(\mathfrak g_{K3})$}{Y(g-K3)} as stage-\texorpdfstring{$2$}{2}
shadow of the \texorpdfstring{$E_2$}{E-2}-factorisation algebra on K3]
\label{rem:k3-yangian-stage-discipline}
Under the two-stage factorisation
$\Phi_d = \mathrm{Sp}^{\mathrm{ch}}_{\Sigma_{d-1},C}\circ\mathrm{Phi}^{\mathrm{FA}}_d$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation}), the native object
attached to K3 is the $E_2$-holomorphic factorisation algebra
$\mathrm{Phi}^{\mathrm{FA}}_2(D^b\!\Coh(K3))$ on the surface, built
from the Gerstenhaber bracket of degree $-1$ on
$\HH^\bullet(D^b\!\Coh(K3))$. The K3 abelian Yangian
$Y(\mathfrak g_{K3})$ of this chapter is the stage-$2$ specialisation
of that $E_2$-factorisation algebra along the normal cycle of a
reference curve $C\hookrightarrow K3$: an $E_1$-chiral algebra on $C$
quantised by $\hbar$, with Mukai-Cartan rank $24$ and structure function
$g_{K3}(z)$ of degree $(24,24)$. Natively $Y(\mathfrak g_{K3})$ is
$E_1$; the $E_2$-braided enhancement lives on the Drinfeld centre
$Z(\mathrm{Rep}^{E_1}(Y(\mathfrak g_{K3})))$ rather than on the algebra
itself (Remark~\ref{rem:e2-on-drinfeld-centre}), and it is the
braided monoidal structure on the representation category that
realises the K3 $R$-matrix face of $r_{\mathrm{CY}}$ at $d = 2$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Nomenclature scope: ``K3 Yangian'' versus Hall--Drinfeld double]
\label{rem:k3-yangian-nomenclature-scope}
The label \emph{K3 Yangian} used throughout this chapter refers to the integrable
quantisation of $\mathfrak{g}_{K3}$ via its $24$ Heisenberg generators, Mukai-signature
Serre relations, and degree-$(24,24)$ structure function. The object underlying the
BKM superalgebra $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ (Chapter~\ref{ch:k3e-bkm}) is a
different object. It is \emph{not} a Drinfeld Yangian in the sense of
Drinfeld 1985: there is no strict $J$-presentation, no finite or
Kac--Moody Cartan matrix, and no Weyl action on imaginary simple roots.
The proved automorphic object is the Borcherds denominator algebra
$\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ with denominator
$D_5(2Z)=64^{-1}\Delta_5(2Z)$; the compact $K3\times E$ quantum-group
target is the still conditional Hall--Drinfeld double
\[
 D^\wedge_\hbar\!\bigl(Y^+_{\mathrm{Hall}}(K3\times E)\bigr),
 \qquad
 Y^+_{\mathrm{Hall}}(K3\times E)\rightsquigarrow
 U(\mathfrak n_+(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})),
\]
after an oriented compact CoHA, negative half, nondegenerate Hopf
pairing, coproduct, centre, completion, and associator have been
constructed. Schiffmann--Vasserot supplies the local shuffle
positive-half grammar; it does not by itself construct the compact
double. The Yangian facet of the K3 construction continues here; the
BKM / Siegel-modular-form facet occupies
Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic}. The two facets meet on
rank-$24$ Mukai data: $\mathfrak{g}_{K3}$ presents the self-mirror
Yangian branch, while $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ presents the
generalised-Borcherds imaginary-root extension.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Abelian-at-Lie / non-abelian-at-vertex discipline]
\label{rem:k3-yangian-abelian-at-lie}
The Lie-primary Heisenberg inputs underlying the K3 and \(K3\times E\)
branches are abelian modulo their centres. The BKM algebra
\(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}\) itself is not abelian: its non-abelian
root-space brackets are produced only after the Frenkel--Kac/Borcherds
vertex closure on the K3 Fock module
$\mathcal{F}_{K3}=\mathrm{Sym}(\bigoplus_n H^*(K3)_{t^n})$ via the Frenkel--Kac
pattern (Frenkel--Kac 1980). Every occurrence of ``non-abelian K3 chiral bialgebra''
in these two chapters should be read as vertex-operator-level, not Lie-bracket-level;
the discipline is made structural by the Frenkel--Kac closure
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-yangian-frenkel-kac-closure} below, and proved as
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-abelian-at-lie} in Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic}.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Abelian at Lie level; non-abelian emerges at vertex-operator closure]
\label{thm:k3-yangian-frenkel-kac-closure}
Let $\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ denote the Mukai--Heisenberg lattice vertex algebra
$V_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}}$ attached to the Mukai lattice
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}} = U^{\oplus 3}\oplus E_8(-1)^{\oplus 2}$ of signature $(4,20)$
(Section~\ref{sec:k3-mukai-heisenberg-voa}). As a Lie algebra, the mode algebra of
$\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ restricted to its rank-$24$ Cartan current subalgebra
is the affine Heisenberg
\[
 \widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{Muk}}
 \;=\;
 \bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}\otimes t^n\;\oplus\;\mathbb{C}\mathbf{c},
 \qquad
 [\alpha_m,\beta_n] = m\,\langle\alpha,\beta\rangle_{\mathrm{Muk}}\,\delta_{m+n,0}\,\mathbf{c},
\]
which is \emph{abelian modulo centre} in the following precise sense: the derived
subalgebra $[\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{Muk}},\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{Muk}}]
= \mathbb{C}\mathbf{c}$ is one-dimensional, and the abelianisation
$\widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{Muk}}^{\mathrm{ab}} =
\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}\otimes t^n$ has dimension $24$ per
mode. The full non-abelian BKM structure $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$
(Chapter~\ref{ch:k3e-bkm}) arises only after closing under the vertex-operator
exponentials furnished by Theorem~\ref{thm:mukai-frenkel-kac-map},
\[
 V(\alpha, z)
 \;=\;
 {:}\exp\!\Bigl(\alpha\cdot\phi(z)\Bigr){:}
 \;=\;
 \exp\!\Bigl(\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{\alpha_{-n}}{n}z^n\Bigr)\,
 \exp\!\Bigl(-\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{\alpha_n}{n}z^{-n}\Bigr)\,
 e^{\alpha}\,z^{\alpha_0},
\]
for $\alpha\in\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ acting on the K3 Fock module $\mathcal{F}_{K3}
= \mathrm{Sym}\!\bigl(\bigoplus_{n\geq 1}\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}\otimes t^{-n}\bigr)
\otimes\mathbb{C}[\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}]$. The commutator
\[
 [V(\alpha,z),\, V(\beta,w)]
 \;=\;
 \epsilon(\alpha,\beta)\,(z-w)^{\langle\alpha,\beta\rangle}\,
 V(\alpha+\beta,w)\;+\;\text{regular},
\]
with $\epsilon\colon \Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}\times\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}\to\{\pm 1\}$
the Frenkel--Kac sign cocycle, produces non-abelian brackets $[e_\alpha,e_\beta]
= \epsilon(\alpha,\beta)e_{\alpha+\beta}$ whenever $\langle\alpha,\beta\rangle = -1$;
these are the non-abelian BKM simple-root brackets of $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$.
Thus ``abelian at Lie level'' refers to the Heisenberg input algebra, not to
the resulting BKM root Lie algebra.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
The Lie-algebra statement is a direct consequence of the mode expansion of a
free-boson vertex algebra: the Heisenberg commutator $[\alpha_m,\beta_n] =
m\langle\alpha,\beta\rangle\delta_{m+n,0}\mathbf{c}$ is central
(Kac--Frenkel 1980; Frenkel--Lepowsky--Meurman 1988, Chapter~1). The
vertex-operator commutator formula is the OPE consequence of
Theorem~\ref{thm:mukai-frenkel-kac-map}, applied to the Mukai lattice with its
Borcherds sign cocycle $\epsilon$ determined by the bimultiplicative solution of
$\epsilon(\alpha,\beta)\epsilon(\beta,\alpha)
= (-1)^{\langle\alpha,\beta\rangle+\langle\alpha,\alpha\rangle\langle\beta,\beta\rangle}$
(Borcherds 1986, §5). The non-abelianity at discriminant $D < 0$ (real simple roots)
is the content of Frenkel--Kac's original ADE construction; the extension to
imaginary simple roots of $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ uses Borcherds's 1986
generalised-Kac--Moody lattice-VOA-to-BKM functor
(Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-lift-universal} of Chapter~\ref{ch:k3e-bkm}).
Both steps are vertex-operator constructions: the underlying Heisenberg Lie
algebra remains abelian-modulo-centre throughout; the non-abelian brackets
live entirely on the Fock module.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Frenkel--Kac dictionary: abelian \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} non-abelian]
\label{rem:k3-yangian-frenkel-kac-dictionary}
The Frenkel--Kac pattern is three-column:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabular}{@{}llll@{}}
\textbf{Heisenberg} & \textbf{Lattice} & \textbf{Vertex closure} & \textbf{Non-abelian output} \\ \hline
$\mathcal{H}_r$ rank-$r$ & root lattice of ADE $\mathfrak{g}$ & $V(\alpha,z)$ for $\alpha$ root & affine $\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}$ at level $1$ \\
$\mathcal{H}_{25,1}$ & $\mathrm{II}_{25,1}$ & $V^{\mathrm{lattice}}_{\mathrm{II}_{25,1}}$ & Fake Monster BKM \\
$\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ rank-$24$ & $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}} = \mathrm{II}_{4,20}$ & $V_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}}$ & $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ BKM super
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Row~$1$ is Frenkel--Kac 1980; row~$2$ is Borcherds 1992; row~$3$ is the K3 case, synthesis
of Borcherds 1998 (singular theta lift) with Gritsenko--Nikulin 1997/1998
(denominator-product equality). In every row the input is an abelian Heisenberg,
the output is a non-abelian Lie algebra, and the passage is vertex-operator closure
on the Fock module. The K3 chiral bialgebra
$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ of Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic} is the
$\hbar$-quantisation of the rank-$24$ Heisenberg input of row~$3$, not of the
non-abelian BKM output.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Hall--Drinfeld double discipline]
\label{rem:k3-yangian-hall-drinfeld-scope}
Combining Remark~\ref{rem:k3-yangian-nomenclature-scope} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-yangian-frenkel-kac-closure}, the BKM-side
comparison is not a second Drinfeld Yangian. The finite data visible in
this chapter are the $24$ abelian-Heisenberg Mukai currents, their
Frenkel--Kac vertex closure, and the finite Rees/positive-half maps that
land in bounded pieces of
$U(\mathfrak n_+(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}))$. The compact
Hall--Drinfeld double is a further object: it requires the compact
critical CoHA of $K3\times E$, an orientation, a negative half, a Hopf
pairing, a coproduct, centre control, and a completion compatible with
the Siegel--Borcherds associator. Thus $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$, when
constructed, is a Hall--Drinfeld/Borcherds comparison target, not a
Drinfeld Yangian presentation of $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$. The
non-abelian brackets of $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ are supplied by the
vertex-operator closure on $\mathcal{F}_{K3}$
(Remark~\ref{rem:k3-not-yangian-strict} of
Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic}); the compact Hall
brackets remain part of the comparison problem.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Chiral-Yangian disambiguation: three variants of ``Yangian'']
\label{rmk:k3-yangian-chiral-disambiguation}
\index{Yangian!three variants (classical, affine, chiral)}
The label \emph{Yangian} as used across the Costello--Gaiotto--Witten
programme carries three structurally distinct meanings. The distinction
matters for the K3 Yangian $Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})$ of the present chapter.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item \emph{Classical $Y(\mathfrak g)$ (Costello--Yagi~$2018$).} The
 boundary algebra of $4$D Chern--Simons theory on
 $\mathbb R^2\times\Sigma$ with defect along $\Sigma$ is the Drinfeld
 Yangian $Y(\mathfrak g)$ of the gauge Lie algebra $\mathfrak g$
 (Costello--Witten--Yamazaki~$2018$ \emph{ICCM Not.}~$6$; Costello--Yagi~$2018$
 Theorem~$1$). This is the \emph{classical} Yangian in the sense that
 no affine/conformal weight enters.
\item \emph{Affine $Y(\widehat{\mathfrak g})$ (Costello--Gaiotto~$2018$).}
 The boundary algebra of $5$D holomorphic Chern--Simons on
 $\mathbb C^2\times\Sigma$ (equivalently, a $6$D hCS theory
 on $\mathbb C^3$ restricted to $\mathbb C^2\times\Sigma$) is the
 affine Yangian $Y_{\epsilon_1,\epsilon_2,\epsilon_3}(\widehat{\mathfrak g})$
 with conformal weight, proved to all orders in $\hbar$ via the chain
 Costello~$2013$ + Costello--Gwilliam~$2017$ + Costello--Yagi~$2018$ +
 Costello--Gaiotto~$2018$ + Costello--Dimofte--Paquette~$2020$.
\item \emph{Chiral $\mathcal Y^{\mathrm{chir}}(\mathfrak g)$.} The
 locally-finite zero-mode sector of the affine Yangian
 $Y(\widehat{\mathfrak g})$, extracted for
 $\Omega$-background partition functions on
 $\mathbb C^2_{\epsilon_1,\epsilon_2}\times E_\tau$. Unlike~(ii), this is
 a vertex algebra on a curve; the conformal weight is fixed by the
 $\Omega$-deformation and the zero modes pick out the locally-finite
 part of $Y(\widehat{\mathfrak g})$ in the Harish-Chandra sense.
\end{enumerate}
The K3 Yangian of the present chapter is the
classical~(i) variant on the Mukai-lattice data
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}=\mathrm{II}_{4,20}$: $Y(\mathfrak g_{K3})$ is
the Drinfeld Yangian of the abelian-at-Lie rank-$24$ Heisenberg
$\mathfrak g_{K3}$, with structure function of degree $(24,24)$ and no
conformal weight. The chiral zero-mode sector
$\mathcal Y^{\mathrm{chir}}(\mathfrak g_{K3})$ sits at level~(iii): it
is the locally-finite part of the $\Omega$-partition function on
$\mathbb C^2\times E_\tau$ restricted to the K3 Mukai data, carried as
a vertex algebra on $E_\tau$ and recovering, in the rational limit
$\hbar\to 0$, the classical $Y(\mathfrak g_{K3})$ discussed in this
chapter. Cross-reference: the chiral zero-mode construction is
elaborated in \cite{VolI}
Remark~\ref{rem:tor-ell-k3-lift-opener}; the affine~(ii) variant is the
all-orders non-abelian compound theorem of
a working note. Primary-lit: Costello--Yagi
$2018$; Costello--Gaiotto $2018$; Costello--Dimofte--Paquette $2020$;
Drinfeld $1985$ \emph{Soviet Math.\ Dokl.}~$32$.
\end{remark}



\section{The Frenkel--Kac map and vertex-operator closure}
\label{sec:k3-frenkel-kac-map-closure}

The Yangian presentation of $Y(\fg_{K3})$ is rank-$24$ and abelian at the Lie level.
The passage from that abelian presentation to the non-abelian BKM side is the
Frenkel--Kac lattice-VOA map on the Mukai lattice. The detailed construction is
expanded in Section~\ref{sec:k3-mukai-heisenberg-voa}; the present section places the
map next to the Yangian presentation so that the Heisenberg generators, the lattice
states, and the vertex-operator closure are visible on one page.

\begin{theorem}[Presentation-level Frenkel--Kac map on the Mukai lattice]
\label{thm:k3-presentation-frenkel-kac-map}
Let
\[
 \Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}
 \;=\;
 \widetilde{H}(K3,\mathbb{Z})
 \;\simeq\;
 \mathrm{II}_{4,20},
 \qquad
 \widehat{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{Muk}}
 \;=\;
 \bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}\otimes t^n
 \oplus \mathbb{C}\mathbf{c}.
\]
Fix a bimultiplicative cocycle
\[
 \epsilon\colon
 \Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}\times\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}
 \longrightarrow
 \{\pm 1\},
 \qquad
 \epsilon(\alpha,\beta)\epsilon(\beta,\alpha)
 =
 (-1)^{\langle\alpha,\beta\rangle_{\mathrm{Muk}}}
\]
as in Frenkel--Kac 1980 and Borcherds 1986, and write normal ordering
$:{-}:$ by placing all negative Heisenberg modes to the left of the non-negative
ones. Then the assignment
\[
 \iota_{\mathrm{FK}}^{\mathrm{pres}}\colon
 \mathrm{Sym}\!\Bigl(\bigoplus_{n\geq 1}\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}\otimes t^{-n}\Bigr)
 \otimes \mathbb{C}_\epsilon[\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}]
 \longrightarrow
 V_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}}
\]
defined by
\[
 (\alpha_1\otimes t^{-n_1})\cdots(\alpha_r\otimes t^{-n_r})\otimes e^\lambda
 \longmapsto
 \alpha_{1,-n_1}\cdots\alpha_{r,-n_r}(1\otimes e^\lambda)
\]
is a linear isomorphism, and for each $\alpha\in\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ the
corresponding field is the vertex operator
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:k3-presentation-level-vertex-operator}
 V_\alpha(z)
 \;:=\;
 Y(1\otimes e^\alpha,z)
 \;=\;
 {:}\exp\!\bigl(i\,\alpha\cdot\phi(z)\bigr){:}\,\otimes e^\alpha
 \;=\;
 \exp\!\Bigl(\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{\alpha_{-n}}{n}z^n\Bigr)
 \exp\!\Bigl(-\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{\alpha_n}{n}z^{-n}\Bigr)
 e^\alpha z^{\alpha_0}.
\end{equation}
In the algebraic normalisation used elsewhere in this chapter, the factor of $i$ is
absorbed into the boson field, so \eqref{eq:k3-presentation-level-vertex-operator}
matches Theorem~\ref{thm:mukai-frenkel-kac-map}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
This is the lattice-VOA construction of Frenkel--Kac 1980, written for the Mukai
lattice $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}=\mathrm{II}_{4,20}$. The bosonic Fock space of the
negative Heisenberg modes identifies with the symmetric algebra on
$\bigoplus_{n\geq 1}\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}\otimes t^{-n}$, while the momentum sectors
are recorded by the cocycle-twisted group algebra
$\mathbb{C}_\epsilon[\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}]$. Normal ordering together with the
Baker--Campbell--Hausdorff formula for the Heisenberg commutator
$[\alpha_m,\beta_n]
=m\langle\alpha,\beta\rangle_{\mathrm{Muk}}\delta_{m+n,0}\mathbf{c}$
gives the exponential expression for $V_\alpha(z)$. The full Mukai construction is
spelled out in Theorem~\ref{thm:mukai-frenkel-kac-map}; the present statement is its
presentation-level form.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Twenty-four Heisenberg generators lift to the Mukai lattice VOA]
\label{rem:k3-24-heisenberg-lattice-voa}
The $24$ Heisenberg generators of $Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})$ are the modes of a rank-$24$
free boson system on the Mukai lattice
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}=\mathrm{II}_{4,20}$. Concretely, if
$\{e_i\}_{i=1}^{24}$ is any integral basis of $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}$, then the
Yangian Cartan currents are the fields
\[
 J_i(z)
 \;=\;
 \sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}} (e_i)_n\, z^{-n-1},
 \qquad
 J_i(z)J_j(w)
 \sim
 \frac{\langle e_i,e_j\rangle_{\mathrm{Muk}}}{(z-w)^2}.
\]
Their common state space is the lattice VOA
\[
 \mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{Muk}}
 \;=\;
 V_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}}
 \;=\;
 \mathrm{Sym}\!\Bigl(\bigoplus_{n\geq 1}\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}\otimes t^{-n}\Bigr)
 \otimes \mathbb{C}_\epsilon[\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}],
\]
whose central charge is
$c\bigl(\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{Muk}}\bigr)=\mathrm{rk}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}})=24$.
This is the precise sense in which the Yangian side is free-field and rank-$24$ at
presentation level: the Heisenberg part is abelian modulo centre, and all subsequent
non-abelianity is deferred to the vertex-operator closure.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Borcherds 1986 closure on the hyperbolic K3 sublattice]
\label{rem:k3-borcherds-hyperbolic-closure}
Frenkel--Kac 1980 constructs affine Kac--Moody algebras from positive-definite root
lattices. Borcherds 1986 extends the same vertex-operator mechanism to even lattices
of Lorentzian type by allowing lattice vectors of non-positive norm to serve as simple
roots. For $\fg_{K3}$ the relevant hyperbolic lattice is the rank-$3$ Borcherds
sublattice
\[
 \Lambda_{II}^{2,1}
 \hookrightarrow
 \Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}=\mathrm{II}_{4,20},
\]
and the corresponding fields
\[
 V_\alpha(z)
 \;=\;
 {:}\exp\!\bigl(i\,\alpha\cdot\phi(z)\bigr){:}\,\otimes e^\alpha,
 \qquad
 \alpha\in\Lambda_{II}^{2,1},
 \qquad
 \alpha^2\leq 0,
\]
are the vertex operators attached to imaginary simple roots. Their OPE is still
governed by the same cocycle $\epsilon$ and the same normal ordering as in
\eqref{eq:k3-presentation-level-vertex-operator}; what changes is the signature of the
lattice and hence the presence of negative-norm and isotropic simple roots. This is
the vertex-operator input for the generalised Borcherds--Kac--Moody superalgebra
$\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ of Chapter~\ref{ch:k3e-bkm}: the non-abelian brackets on the
imaginary sector are generated by these hyperbolic-lattice vertex operators, not by a
separate Yangian presentation.
\end{remark}

\section{Symplectic duality and the K3 Yangian}
\label{sec:k3e-symplectic-duality}

Symplectic duality (Braden--Licata--Proudfoot--Webster 2014; physically, 3d $\cN = 4$ mirror symmetry) exchanges Coulomb and Higgs branches of 3d $\cN = 4$ theories:
\[
 \cM_C(T) \;\longleftrightarrow\; \cM_H(T^!),
 \qquad
 Y(\frakg)\text{ acts on }H^*(\cM_C)
 \;\longleftrightarrow\;
 Y(\frakg^L)\text{ acts on }H^*(\cM_H).
\]
For K3 the self-mirror property forces a self-identification: $\cM_C \simeq \cM_H$ and $Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})^L = Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})$.

\subsection{Coulomb and Higgs branches for K3}
\label{subsec:k3e-coulomb-higgs}

\begin{conjecture}[K3 Coulomb branch]
\label{conj:k3e-coulomb-branch}
The Coulomb branch of the 3d $\cN = 4$ theory associated to K3 at charge~$n$ is $\cM_C = T^*\!\operatorname{Hilb}^n(K3)$, a holomorphic symplectic manifold of complex dimension~$4n$. The $T = \C^*$-fixed locus is the zero section $\operatorname{Hilb}^n(K3)$, so
\[
 \chi(\cM_C^T) \;=\; \chi(\operatorname{Hilb}^n(K3)) \;=\; p_{24}(n).
\]
The Hilbert--Chow morphism $\operatorname{Hilb}^n(K3) \to \Sym^n(K3)$ lifts to a symplectic resolution $T^*\!\operatorname{Hilb}^n \to T^*\!\Sym^n$ for $n \geq 2$.
\end{conjecture}

On the Higgs side, for a primitive Mukai vector $v$ with $\langle v, v \rangle_{\mathrm{Muk}} = 2n-2$, the moduli space $M_H(v)$ of $H$-stable sheaves is deformation-equivalent to $\operatorname{Hilb}^n(K3)$ by the Beauville theorem (Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-beauville}). Since K3 is self-mirror under the Dolgachev--Nikulin involution, the Coulomb and Higgs branches have the same Euler characteristics:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:k3e-ch-euler-match}
 \chi(\cM_C^T, n) \;=\; \chi(M_H(v)) \;=\; p_{24}(n)
 \qquad\text{for all } n \geq 0.
\end{equation}

\subsection{Self-duality under symplectic duality}
\label{subsec:k3e-symplectic-selfduality}

\begin{conjecture}[K3 Yangian self-duality]
\label{conj:k3e-yangian-selfdual}
The K3 Yangian is self-dual under symplectic duality:
\[
 Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})^L \;=\; Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3}).
\]
At the level of the structure function: $g_{K3}^L(z) = g_{K3}(z)$. The Langlands dual parameters satisfy $h_i^L = h_i$ (for the abelian case $\frakg = \mathfrak{gl}_1$, since $\mathfrak{gl}_1^L = \mathfrak{gl}_1$; for simply-laced ADE, by Langlands self-duality of ADE Dynkin diagrams).
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Three involutions on \texorpdfstring{$Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})$}{Y(g-K3)}]
\label{rem:k3e-three-involutions}
The K3 Yangian admits three structurally distinct involutions:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Koszul duality}: $h_i \mapsto -h_i$, $g(z) \mapsto 1/g(z)$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \mapsto -\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$. This exchanges algebra and coalgebra (ordered bar/cobar). The Koszul conductor on the free-field branch is $K = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^! = 2 + (-2) = 0$.
 \item \emph{Symplectic duality (Langlands)}: $h_i \mapsto h_i^L = h_i$ (for $\mathfrak{gl}_1$), $g(z) \mapsto g(z)$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \mapsto \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$. This exchanges Coulomb and Higgs branches; for K3 (self-mirror) it is a self-identification.
 \item \emph{Algebraic unitarity}: $g(z) \cdot g(-z) = 1$, an algebraic identity holding for any CY structure function (proved, Section~\ref{sec:k3e-mo-rmatrix}).
\end{enumerate}
These are \emph{distinct operations}: Koszul reverses $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$, symplectic preserves it, and unitarity is a constraint, not an involution. In particular, symplectic duality $\neq$ Koszul duality: the former preserves $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 2$ while the latter sends it to $-2$. Mirror symmetry (HMS) also preserves $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ but is distinct from both: HMS $\neq$ Koszul was established in Proposition~\ref{prop:koszul-dual-k3} (Chapter~\ref{ch:derived-cy}).
\end{remark}

\subsection{The BFN quantized Coulomb branch}
\label{subsec:k3e-bfn}

The Braverman--Finkelberg--Nakajima (BFN) construction produces quantized Coulomb branches as convolution algebras on the affine Grassmannian:
\[
 A_C(T) \;=\; H^G_*(\mathrm{Gr}_G,\, \mathrm{IC}_R).
\]
For Nakajima quiver varieties, this is unconditional and produces the Yangian.

\begin{conjecture}[BFN = K3 Yangian, Kummer-orbifold form]
\label{conj:bfn-k3-yangian-kummer}
At the Kummer orbifold point $K3 = T^4/\Z_2$ (resolved), the McKay correspondence provides an affine $A_1$ quiver description with $4$ bifundamental pairs. The BFN quantized Coulomb branch at charge~$n$ is:
\[
 A_C(\text{Kummer}, n) \;=\; Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})\big|_{\text{charge } n},
\]
a filtered quantization of $\C[T^*\!\operatorname{Hilb}^n(T^4/\Z_2)]$. The identification with $Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})$ requires deformation invariance of the Yangian under blowup of the $16$ orbifold singularities.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Two routes to the K3 Yangian]
\label{rem:k3e-two-routes-yangian}
The K3 Yangian $Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})$ is the conjectural target of two independent constructions:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\Alph*)}]
 \item \emph{CY-A route}: $D^b(\Coh(K3)) \xrightarrow{\Phi} A_{K3} \xrightarrow{\text{bar}} B(A_{K3}) \xrightarrow{\text{Koszul}} Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})$. CY-A$_2$ identifies the bar complex with $\eta^{24}$; the Yangian quantisation step remains the proof obligation.
 \item \emph{BFN route}: $T[K3] \xrightarrow{\text{BFN}} A_C(T[K3]) \stackrel{?}{=} Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})$. Braverman--Finkelberg--Nakajima prove the quiver-variety cases; the K3 identification requires a quiver description, available only at orbifold points.
\end{enumerate}
The routes are complementary: Route~(A) constructs the algebra from the CY category, Route~(B) constructs it from the 3d $\cN = 4$ gauge theory. Where both are defined (Kummer/orbifold K3), they should agree. The structure function $g(z) = \prod_{i=1}^{24} (z - h_i)/(z + h_i)$ is the same in both routes (it depends only on the Mukai lattice, which is deformation-invariant).

The MO stable envelope route of Proposition~\ref{prop:mo-bypass} (\S\ref{subsec:mo-bypass-k3e}) provides a third access to the $R$-matrix \emph{at the ADE/Kummer locus of K3 moduli}, where a local toric torus $T_S = (\C^*)^2$ acts on the analytic chart by the McKay correspondence (Lemma~\ref{lem:mo-bypass-local-to-global}: for generic K3, $\dim \mathrm{Aut}(S \times E)^\circ = 1 < 2$ and the MO machine fails; further sharpened by Lemma~\ref{lem:no-Gm-on-E}: the elliptic curve $E$ provides no $\mathbb{G}_m$-axis since $\mathrm{Aut}(E)^\circ \cong E$ as algebraic groups, an abelian variety with no $\mathbb{G}_m$-subgroup). At those scope-permitted points it yields the evaluation $R$-matrix on the Fock space $\bigoplus_n K_T(\operatorname{Hilb}^n(K3 \times E))$, not the full Yangian algebra.
\end{remark}

\subsection{ADE specialisation: the proved sub-case}
\label{subsec:k3e-bfn-ade-proved}

The two conjectures above (Conjecture~\ref{conj:bfn-k3-yangian-kummer} at the Kummer orbifold point and Conjecture~\ref{conj:bfn-k3-yangian-mukai} in Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-quantum-toroidal}) predict an identification $\Phi_2(T^*K3) \simeq Y(\frakg_{K3})$ via the BFN route. For the \emph{resolved ADE surface singularities} $\widetilde{S}_\fg \to \C^2/\Gamma$, the analogous identification is a theorem: it is a composition of Kronheimer's hyperk\"ahler resolution, the McKay correspondence, the Braverman--Finkelberg--Nakajima Coulomb-branch theorem, and the Nakajima--Takayama truncation. The theorem below records this assembly; it is the proved sub-case on which the two K3 conjectures deform.

\begin{remark}[Stage-\texorpdfstring{$2$}{2} attribution for resolved ADE surfaces]
\label{rem:k3y-stage-bfn-ade}
For a resolved ADE surface $\widetilde{S}_\fg$, the stage-$1$
functor produces the toric $\Etwo$-factorisation algebra
$\PhiFA_2(D^b\Coh(\widetilde{S}_\fg))$ on the resolved surface; the
BFN Coulomb branch is the stage-$2$ $\SpCh$-specialisation along
the exceptional divisor. Specifically,
\[
 \cA_\hbar(Q_\fg, \mathbf{v}, \mathbf{w})
 \;=\;
 \SpCh_{\Sigma_1^{\mathrm{exc}}, C}\!\bigl(
 \PhiFA_2(D^b\Coh(\widetilde{S}_\fg))\bigr),
\]
with $\Sigma_1^{\mathrm{exc}} \hookrightarrow \widetilde{S}_\fg$
the union of $(-2)$-curves carrying unit Kronheimer flux, and the
reference curve $C$ pushed forward to the quiver base through the
affine Grassmannian. The level-one evaluation is fixed by the
Kronheimer hyperk\"ahler moment-map normalisation on
$\Sigma_1^{\mathrm{exc}}$; the identification with
$Y^\mu(\widehat{\fg})_{k=1}$ is a $d = 2$ instance of the
$\SpCh$-specialisation for toric $\PhiFA_2$, and is precisely the
sub-case on which the two K3 conjectures
(Conjecture~\ref{conj:bfn-k3-yangian-kummer} and its Mukai-form
counterpart) deform.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[\texorpdfstring{$\Phi$}{Phi}-BFN identification for resolved ADE surfaces]
\label{thm:bfn-phi-ade-identification}
Let $\fg$ be a simple Lie algebra of ADE type and let $\Gamma \subset \mathrm{SU}(2)$ be the binary McKay subgroup corresponding to $\fg$ under the McKay correspondence. Let $(Q_\fg, \mathbf{v}, \mathbf{w})$ be the framed affine Dynkin quiver of type $\widehat{\fg}$ with dimension vectors $\mathbf{v} = \delta$ (minimal imaginary root) and $\mathbf{w} = \mathbf{e}_0$ (framing at the affine node), and let $\widetilde{S}_\fg \to \C^2/\Gamma$ denote the Kronheimer minimal crepant resolution. Then the CY-to-chiral correspondence $\Phi_2$ applied to the resolved ADE surface is canonically isomorphic, as an $\Eone$-chiral algebra, to the BFN quantised Coulomb branch of the framed affine quiver gauge theory, which in turn is the level-one truncated shifted affine Yangian:
\[
 \Phi\bigl(T^*\widetilde{S}_\fg\bigr)
 \;\simeq\;
 \cA_\hbar\bigl(Q_\fg, \mathbf{v}, \mathbf{w}\bigr)
 \;\simeq\;
 Y^\mu\bigl(\widehat{\fg}\bigr)_{k=1},
\]
where $\mu \in \mathbf{P}^+$ is the dominant coweight determined by the framing $\mathbf{w}$ via Nakajima--Takayama truncation, and the level-one evaluation is fixed by the Kronheimer hyperk\"ahler moment map at the exceptional divisor (unit flux through each $(-2)$-curve).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The theorem is a composition of four named inputs; Vol~III supplies the $\Phi$-compatibility bridge in Step~4.

\emph{Step 1 (McKay + Kronheimer).} By Kronheimer (J.~Diff.~Geom.\ 29, 1989) and McKay (Proc.~Symp.\ Pure Math.\ 37, 1980), the correspondence $\Gamma \leftrightarrow \fg$ identifies $\widetilde{S}_\fg$ as the minimal crepant resolution of $\C^2/\Gamma$, with exceptional divisor a disjoint union of $(-2)$-curves matching the finite Dynkin diagram of $\fg$.

\emph{Step 2 (Derived McKay equivalence).} Bridgeland--King--Reid (arXiv:math/9908027) give $D^b(\Coh \widetilde{S}_\fg) \simeq D^b(\Coh_\Gamma \C^2)$, and Kapranov--Vasserot (Math.\ Ann.\ 316, 2000) identifies $\Gamma$-equivariant sheaves on $\C^2$ with modules over the preprojective algebra $\Pi_{Q_\fg}$ of the affine Dynkin quiver.

\emph{Step 3 (BFN identification).} By Braverman--Finkelberg--Nakajima (arXiv:1604.03625, Thm~1.1), the quantised Coulomb branch $\cA_\hbar(Q_\fg, \mathbf{v}, \mathbf{w})$ of the 3d $\cN = 4$ quiver gauge theory is canonically isomorphic to the $\mathbf{w}$-truncated shifted Yangian $Y^\mu(\widehat{\fg})$; Nakajima--Takayama (arXiv:1606.02002, Thm~A) gives an explicit GKLO presentation. Webster (arXiv:1905.11473) extends to non-simply-laced types by folding (not needed here because we are ADE).

\emph{Step 4 ($\Phi$-compatibility).} $\Phi$ applied to the cotangent bundle of a hyperk\"ahler resolution produces the factorisation quantisation of the Higgs branch (CY-A$_2$ output for $d = 2$ local surfaces, proved at publication standard in this volume). Symplectic duality (Braden--Licata--Proudfoot--Webster, arXiv:1407.0964) identifies Higgs-side factorisation quantisation with Coulomb-side BFN convolution as $\Eone$-chiral algebras on the formal disk; combining Steps~2--3 gives the stated triple isomorphism. Level one is fixed by the Kronheimer moment-map normalisation at the exceptional divisor.
\end{proof}

\noindent\textit{Independent verification paths for Theorem~\ref{thm:bfn-phi-ade-identification} (two genuinely disjoint at the input layer; three disjoint at the verification-check layer):}

\begin{remark}[Shared Kronheimer input between Paths V1 and V3]
\label{rem:bfn-ade-input-dependency}
Paths V1 and V3 both take the Kronheimer minimal crepant resolution
$\widetilde{S}_\fg \to \C^2/\Gamma$ as geometric input. Path V1
then invokes the Bridgeland--King--Reid derived equivalence
$D^b(\Coh \widetilde{S}_\fg) \simeq D^b(\Pi_{Q_\fg})$, a
representation-theoretic statement. Path V3 invokes the Kronheimer
hyperk\"ahler moment-map normalisation at the exceptional divisor,
a symplectic/differential-geometric statement. The two downstream
verifications are algebraically disjoint (sheaves of $\Coh$-modules
versus moment-map flux integrals) and thus check different features
of the identification; but the \emph{input} Kronheimer construction
$\widetilde{S}_\fg$ is common. Path V2 (BFN affine-Grassmannian
homology) is genuinely input-disjoint: it touches no hyperk\"ahler
geometry and no sheaves on~$\widetilde{S}_\fg$. The correct
accounting is therefore: \emph{two input-disjoint paths}
(V2 versus V1+V3 as a shared-input block), with three
verification-layer disjoint checks (RTT / sheaf-equivalence /
moment-map flux) stratifying the V1+V3 block into two sub-checks.
A reader seeking genuine logical independence of the ADE
identification should treat V2 (BFN) and the V1+V3 block
(Kronheimer-conditional) as the two primary witnesses.
\end{remark}


\begin{description}
\item[Path V1 (Derived McKay on the Higgs side).]
Bridgeland--King--Reid and Kapranov--Vasserot give
$D^b(\Coh \widetilde{S}_\fg) \simeq D^b(\Pi_{Q_\fg})$.
The Hilbert-series character computation matches rank and character
across ADE types $A_n, D_n, E_6, E_7, E_8$ in
\texttt{compute/lib/fh\_mckay\_correspondence.py}. This path is
sheaf-theoretic on $\widetilde{S}_\fg$ and does not use affine
Grassmannian convolution.

\item[Path V2 (BFN convolution on the Coulomb side).]
Braverman--Finkelberg--Nakajima identify the quantised Coulomb branch
with equivariant homology of the triple variety $\cR_{G,N}$ under
convolution. The GKLO generators of Nakajima--Takayama match the RTT
presentation in \texttt{compute/lib/ade\_yangian\_level1.py}: Yang
$R$-matrix, Lax presentation, level-one evaluation, and ADE
type-by-type $R_{12}L_1L_2=L_2L_1R_{12}$. This path uses affine
Grassmannian homology and no sheaves on $\widetilde{S}_\fg$.

\item[Path V3 ($\Phi$-functor and level-one matching).]
CY-A$_2$ and the definition of $\Phi$ through factorisation
quantisation of $T^*\widetilde{S}_\fg$ fix the chiral side.
Kronheimer's unit-flux moment-map normalisation across the exceptional
$(-2)$-curves reproduces the level-one evaluation; the Kummer-point
$A_1$ specialisation is computed in
\texttt{compute/lib/bfn\_coulomb\_k3\_yangian.py}. This path uses the
hyperk\"ahler moment map and neither the sheaf equivalence of Path~V1
nor the affine-Grassmannian homology of Path~V2.
\end{description}

\begin{remark}[Reduction of Conjecture~\ref{conj:bfn-k3-yangian-kummer} to the proved \texorpdfstring{$A_1$}{A-1} case]
\label{rem:bfn-kummer-reduces-to-a1}
At the Kummer orbifold point $K3 = T^4/\Z_2$ (resolved), Conjecture~\ref{conj:bfn-k3-yangian-kummer} reduces to the proved $\fg = \mathfrak{sl}_2$ instance of Theorem~\ref{thm:bfn-phi-ade-identification} plus deformation invariance of the BFN Coulomb branch under blowup of the $16$ orbifold singularities. The Mukai-form Conjecture~\ref{conj:bfn-k3-yangian-mukai} requires the non-quiver BFN extension for generic K3 moduli and does not reduce to an ADE instance; it remains genuinely open.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Generating functions and self-duality check}
\label{subsec:k3e-symplectic-genfunction}

\begin{proposition}[Self-duality of the Fock space character]
\label{prop:k3e-selfdual-fock}
The generating functions for Coulomb and Higgs branch Euler characteristics coincide:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:k3e-fock-selfdual}
 Z_C(q) \;=\; Z_H(q)
 \;=\; \sum_{n \geq 0} p_{24}(n)\,q^n
 \;=\; \prod_{k \geq 1} \frac{1}{(1-q^k)^{24}}
 \;=\; \frac{1}{\eta(q)^{24}}.
\end{equation}
This is simultaneously the Fock space character of $Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})$, the inverse bar Euler product of $A_{K3}$, and the generating function of root multiplicities of the Fake Monster Lie algebra at lightlike level. The agreement $Z_C = Z_H$ is a necessary consequence of K3 self-mirror symmetry and is verified coefficient by coefficient through $n = 6$ in $125$ tests (\texttt{symplectic\_duality\_k3.py}).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The Coulomb branch Euler characteristics are $\chi(\cM_C^T, n) = \chi(\operatorname{Hilb}^n(K3)) = p_{24}(n)$ by the G\"ottsche formula. The Higgs branch Euler characteristics are $\chi(M_H(v)) = \chi(\operatorname{Hilb}^n(K3)) = p_{24}(n)$ by the Beauville deformation equivalence (Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-beauville}). Both equal $p_{24}(n)$, so $Z_C = Z_H$. The identification with $1/\eta^{24}$ is Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-dt-bar-rank0}.
\end{proof}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 125 tests in \texttt{symplectic\_duality\_k3.py} covering Coulomb branch geometry (charge $0$--$5$, dimensions, Hilbert--Chow resolution), Higgs branch Beauville data (Mukai vectors, deformation equivalence), K3 self-duality (Euler characteristic matching, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ preservation), three involutions (Koszul $h_i \mapsto -h_i$, Langlands trivial for $\mathfrak{gl}_1$, unitarity $g(z)g(-z) = 1$), BFN construction (Kummer affine $A_1$ quiver, ADE orbifold points), $\kappa_\bullet$-diagnostic (symplectic preserves $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 2$, Koszul reverses to $-2$, conductor $K = 0$), generating functions ($1/\eta^{24}$, bar Euler exponents $-24$, Fake Monster match), Dolgachev--Nikulin lattice (signatures $(2,\rho) + (2,20-\rho) = (4,20)$ for all $\rho$), and 16 cross-engine checks against \texttt{k3\_double\_current\_algebra.py}, \texttt{k3\_yangian.py}, \texttt{k3\_mirror\_koszul.py}, \texttt{k3\_yangian\_quantization.py}, and \texttt{drinfeld\_center\_k3\_heisenberg.py}.

\begin{remark}[Three involutions on \texorpdfstring{$Y(\frakg_{K3})$}{Y(frakg-K3)}: symplectic, Koszul, unitarity]
\label{rem:k3-yangian-three-involutions}
The conjectural K3 Yangian $Y(\frakg_{K3})$ admits three distinct involutions,
each with a sharp $\kappa_\bullet$-diagnostic that distinguishes it from the others
:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Koszul involution} $\iota_K \colon h_i \mapsto -h_i$.
 Sends $g_{K3}(z) \mapsto 1/g_{K3}(z)$ and reverses the modular
 characteristic: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A^!) = -\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A)$.
 The Koszul conductor vanishes on the free-field (class~$G$
 Heisenberg/KM) branch because the two Heisenberg exponents are opposite;
 the Virasoro-decorated
 branch carries $K = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^! = 13$
 (Vol.~I Theorem~C complementarity).
 This is the chiral Koszul duality of Vol~I, distinct from bar-cobar inversion
 $\Omega(B(A)) \simeq A$ and from the chiral derived center
 $\cZ^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A)$.

 \item \emph{Symplectic duality involution} $\iota_S \colon h_i \mapsto h_i^L$.
 For K3, the Dolgachev--Nikulin self-mirror property forces
 $h_i^L = h_i$ (since $\fgl_1^L = \fgl_1$), so $\iota_S = \id$.
 Symplectic duality \emph{preserves} $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ (Coulomb
 and Higgs branches have the same Euler characteristics by
 the Beauville deformation equivalence). The self-duality
 $Y(\frakg_{K3})^L \simeq Y(\frakg_{K3})$ is a prediction of
 K3 self-mirror symmetry, distinct from Koszul self-duality.

 \item \emph{Unitarity involution} $\iota_U \colon z \mapsto -z$.
 The CY$_2$ unitarity $g_{K3}(z) \cdot g_{K3}(-z) = 1$
 implies $R(z) R(-z) = \id$ for the diagonal $R$-matrix,
 a structural constraint on the representation category.
 This involution acts on the spectral parameter, not the
 parameters $h_i$.
\end{enumerate}
The three involutions generate a $(\bZ/2\bZ)^3$ action on the parameter
and spectral data. Koszul acts on the algebra/coalgebra axis (exchanging
$B^{\mathrm{ord}}$ and $\Omega^{\mathrm{ord}}$), symplectic duality acts on the
Coulomb/Higgs axis (exchanging $\cM_C$ and $\cM_H$), and unitarity acts
on the spectral axis (exchanging $z$ and $-z$). For K3, the symplectic
involution is trivial; for non-self-mirror CY surfaces (e.g., abelian
surfaces with non-self-dual polarisation), it would be nontrivial.

The BFN quantised Coulomb branch $A_{\mathrm{BFN}}$ provides an independent
construction of $Y(\frakg_{K3})$ at orbifold points (Kummer $T^4/\bZ_2$
via the affine $A_1$ quiver, ADE orbifold limits via the McKay
correspondence), bypassing CY-A$_3$ but not the quiver obstruction
(Proposition~\ref{prop:k3e-hall-yangian}(b): $K3 \times E$ is not a
Nakajima quiver variety at generic moduli).
\end{remark}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $125$ tests in
\texttt{symplectic\_duality\_k3.py}; see the verification note above
for the full breakdown.
