\textbf{Sector} & \textbf{Data fixed here} & \textbf{Current/Hall form} & \textbf{New input} \\
\midrule
Real ($D = 2$) & $3$ triples & Standard Cartan triples & No \\
$D = -1$ & $c(-1)=1$ & Timelike Borcherds current & No \\
$D = 0$ & $c(0)=10$ & No known analogue & Yes \\
$D > 0$ & signed $c(D)$ & Unknown (obstruction) & Yes \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The $D = 0$ sector has no counterpart in known quantum group theory: the quantum toroidal algebra $U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\gl}}_1)$ has two central extensions (not~$10$), and the passage from the protected coefficient $c(0)=10$ to ten lightlike generators with a Hall coproduct is one of the load-bearing open problems. For $D>0$, the signed coefficient $c(D)$ fixes the protected root character; the generator count is part of the missing parity-refined Hall--Borcherds presentation.
\end{remark}


\subsection{The Serre ideal from the quantum determinant}
\label{subsec:k3-serre-from-qdet}

\noindent The abelian K3 Yangian of Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-abelian-yangian-presentation}
has trivial Serre relations: its $24$ copies of $\gl_1$ commute, so
the quantum Serre element $\sum_{k=0}^{1-a_{ij}} (-1)^k
\binom{1-a_{ij}}{k}_q E_i^{1-a_{ij}-k} E_j E_i^k$ is vacuous when
$a_{ij} = 0$ for all $i \neq j$.
At an enhanced symmetry point (Proposition~\ref{prop:k3-ade-enhancement}),
a subset of the Mukai directions merges into a non-abelian subalgebra,
and the quantum determinant controls the Serre ideal.

\begin{conjecture}[K3 Serre relations at enhanced symmetry]
\label{conj:k3-serre-enhanced}
Let $S$ be a K3 surface acquiring an ADE singularity of type~$\fg$ with
rank~$r$ and Cartan matrix $(a_{ij})$.
The quantum determinant of the conjectural K3 Yangian decomposes as
\begin{equation}\label{eq:k3-qdet-decomposition}
 \mathrm{qdet}_{K3}(u) \;=\;
 \mathrm{qdet}_{\fg}(u) \cdot \prod_{k=r+1}^{24} (u - h_k),
\end{equation}
where $\mathrm{qdet}_{\fg}(u)$ is the degree-$(r+1)$ quantum determinant
of the enhanced $\fg$-block and the remaining $24 - r - 1$ factors are
linear (abelian complement). The Serre ideal decomposes:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:k3-serre-decomposition}
 I_{\mathrm{Serre}} \;=\; I_{\fg} \;\oplus\; I_{\mathrm{mix}}
 \;\oplus\; I_{\mathrm{ab}},
\end{equation}
where:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item $I_{\fg}$ contains the standard quantum Serre relations of~$\fg$,
 determined by the $r-1$ edges of the finite Dynkin diagram: for each
 pair $(i,j)$ with $a_{ij} = -1$,
 \[
 E_i^2 E_j - [2]_q E_i E_j E_i + E_j E_i^2 = 0
 \qquad \textup{(simply-laced quantum Serre)};
 \]

\item $I_{\mathrm{mix}} = 0$: the mixing Serre relations between the
 enhanced subalgebra and the abelian complement are \emph{trivial},
 because the ADE root lattice $\Lambda_\fg$ is orthogonal to its
 complement in the Mukai lattice $\widetilde{\Lambda}$:
 $\omega^{ab} = 0$ for $a \in \Lambda_\fg$, $b \in \Lambda_\fg^\perp$,
 so the cross-sector structure function $g_{\mathrm{mix}}(z) = 1$;

\item $I_{\mathrm{ab}}$: the abelian commutativity relations
 $[E_i, E_j] = 0$ for $i, j$ in the abelian complement.
\end{enumerate}
The total number of nontrivial Serre relations equals the number of
edges in the finite Dynkin diagram of~$\fg$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Enhanced symmetry examples]
\label{rem:k3-serre-examples}
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
\item \emph{$A_1$ enhancement} ($\fsl_2$, rank~$1$):
 $\mathrm{qdet}_{\fsl_2}(u) = (u + j)(u - j - 1)$, degree~$2$.
 Zeros at $u = -j$ and $u = j + 1$ give the Serre null vectors,
 matching \texttt{sl2\_matrix\_lax\_engine.py}
 (Proposition~\ref{prop:matrix-lax-coproduct}).
 No inter-node Serre (single node in finite $A_1$).
 The abelian complement has $22$ directions.

\item \emph{$A_2$ enhancement} ($\fsl_3$, rank~$2$):
 $\mathrm{qdet}_{\fsl_3}(u)$ has degree~$3$.
 One nontrivial Serre relation from the single edge $0$--$1$.
 Abelian complement: $21$ directions.

\item \emph{$D_4$ enhancement} ($\fso_8$, rank~$4$, triality):
 $\mathrm{qdet}_{\fso_8}(u)$ has degree~$4$.
 Three nontrivial Serre from the tristar Dynkin diagram
 ($3$~edges meeting at the central node).
 Abelian complement: $20$ directions.
\end{enumerate}
In all cases the degree of $\mathrm{qdet}_{K3}(u)$ is~$24$ (the Mukai rank),
and the mixing Serre $I_{\mathrm{mix}} = 0$ by the Mukai lattice
orthogonality.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Mechanism: orthogonality from the Mukai lattice]
\label{rem:k3-serre-mixing-mechanism}
The vanishing of the mixing Serre relations $I_{\mathrm{mix}} = 0$
is a consequence of the lattice-theoretic structure:
the ADE root lattice embeds as a direct summand of the Mukai lattice
$\widetilde{\Lambda} = U^3 \oplus E_8(-1)^2$,
and the complement inherits an orthogonal Mukai pairing
$\omega^{ab} = 0$ for cross-sector pairs.
The mixing structure function is therefore
\[
 g_{\mathrm{mix}}(z) \;=\;
 \prod_{\substack{a \in \fg \\ b \in \fg^\perp}}
 \frac{z - \omega^{ab} \epsilon}{z + \omega^{ab} \epsilon}
 \;=\; 1,
\]
and $[E_a(z), E_b(w)] = 0$ for $a$ in the enhanced subalgebra and
$b$ in the abelian complement.

This orthogonality is the reason the K3 Serre ideal decomposes as
a direct sum rather than a semidirect product: the non-abelian and
abelian sectors are dynamically decoupled. For a hypothetical
CY$_2$ surface with \emph{non-split} lattice embedding (if such
existed), the mixing Serre would be nontrivial.
\end{remark}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $61$~tests in
\texttt{k3\_serre\_relations.py}
covering: abelian qdet degree and factorisation ($6$~tests),
$A_1$--$A_4$ enhanced Serre including qdet zeros, factorisation,
and Dynkin-edge counting ($18$~tests),
$D_4$--$D_5$ enhanced Serre including triality and fork structure
($8$~tests), Cartan matrices ($11$~tests), mixing triviality for all
ADE types ($3$~tests), Serre ideal summary ($4$~tests),
full verification ($3$~tests), cross-family consistency ($4$~tests),
and parameter constraints ($4$~tests).


%% ========================================================================
%% Structural obstruction analysis of the K3 Yangian
%% ========================================================================

\begin{remark}[Obstruction analysis: six structural tests of \texorpdfstring{$Y(\fg_{K3})$}{Y(g-K3)}]
\label{rem:k3-yangian-obstruction-tests}
Six possible obstructions to Conjecture~\ref{conj:k3-yangian} split
cleanly. Two identify genuine structural issues confined to the
non-abelian regime; four vanish at the abelian $\gl_1$ level. The
case analysis is implemented in \texttt{k3\_yangian\_adversarial.py}
(31~tests).
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textbf{A\arabic*.}, leftmargin=2.5em]

\item \emph{Indefinite signature obstruction} (Mukai pairing of signature
$(4,20)$).
Standard Yangian theory assumes a positive-definite inner product
on~$\fh^*$. The Mukai pairing $\omega = \operatorname{diag}(+1^4, -1^{20})$
is indefinite. For the \emph{abelian} ($\gl_1$) K3 Yangian, the $R$-matrix
is diagonal and the YBE, unitarity $g(z)g(-z) = 1$, and coassociativity
hold for \emph{each direction independently}, regardless of the Mukai sign.
The obstruction appears for \emph{non-abelian}~$\fg$: the $\omega$-twisted
permutation $P_\omega$ satisfies $P_\omega^2 = \omega \otimes \omega$
(not~$\mathrm{Id}$), with eigenvalues $+1$ on $416/576$ of
$\bC^{24} \otimes \bC^{24}$ and $-1$ on $160/576$ (the mixed-sign
pairs). This modifies the crossing symmetry
$R_\omega(u) R_\omega(-u)^{t_1} = f(u) \cdot \mathrm{Id}$ with
$f(u) \neq 1$ on the $-1$ eigenspace.
The resolution is a \emph{super-Yangian} structure adapted to the
indefinite pairing (cf.\ the affine super Yangians
of Chapter~\ref{ch:toric-coha}).
Thus the indefinite signature is harmless for $\gl_1$ and produces a
genuine modified crossing law for non-abelian~$\fg$.

\item \emph{Level-rank duality obstruction.}
The K3 CFT has $c = 6$, level $k = 1$. Level-rank duality
($\mathfrak{su}(N)_k \leftrightarrow \mathfrak{su}(k)_N$) would
relate rank~$24$ and level~$1$, potentially contradicting the
rank-$24$ Yangian. This is \emph{inapplicable}: level-rank duality
requires simple Lie algebras, not the abelian $\gl_1$.
The three numbers (lattice rank~$24$ (Mukai), Lie algebra rank~$1$
($\gl_1$), CFT level~$1$ (Heisenberg normalization)) are independent.
Thus level-rank duality imposes no obstruction.

\item \emph{Infinite protected-index obstruction.}
The BKM superalgebra $\fg_{\Delta_5}$ has imaginary roots with
protected root-character coefficients satisfying
$|c(D)| \sim \exp(2\pi\sqrt{D}) \to \infty$ along admissible
discriminants.
Standard Yangians require finite-dimensional input.
The K3 Yangian uses $\fg_{K3} = H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$
($25$-dimensional Heisenberg), \emph{not} $\fg_{\Delta_5}$
(infinite-dimensional BKM). The BKM protected indices
appear as Euler characteristics of the \emph{bar complex}
$B(Y(\fg_{K3}))$, which is infinite-dimensional even for
finitely-generated input (cf.\ $Y(\widehat{\gl}_1)$ for $\bC^3$:
finitely many generators, MacMahon-function bar Euler product).
Thus the BKM character growth belongs to the bar side, not to the
finite K3 Yangian input.

\item \emph{Moduli dependence obstruction.}
At generic Mukai moduli, $Y(\fg_{K3})$ is abelian
($24$~commuting $\gl_1$'s); at enhanced points (Gepner, orbifold),
parameters $h_i$ coalesce and the Yangian becomes nonabelian.
Is this a deformation or a discontinuity? It is a \emph{flat
deformation}: the PBW filtration is preserved, the bar Euler product
$\eta(q)^{24}/q$ is moduli-\emph{invariant}
(depends only on the rank~$24$, not the parameter values),
and the representation category varies continuously.
The coalescence $h_i \to h_j$ is the standard Yangian degeneration
(evaluation-parameter collision). At the Gepner point, the
$R$-matrix trivializes ($R \to \mathrm{Id} \otimes \mathrm{Id}$)
by enhanced $\cN = (4,4)$ symmetry, while $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 2$
persists as a global invariant.
Thus moduli dependence is flat degeneration, not discontinuity.

\item \emph{CY-A$_3$ obstruction for $K3 \times E$.}
The K3 Yangian construction has four layers with escalating CY-A
dependence:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item \emph{K3 DCA $\fg_{K3}$}: no CY-A dependence (pure geometry, proved).
\item \emph{Quantization $\fg_{K3} \to Y(\fg_{K3})$}: requires CY-A$_2$
 (proved); the abelian $\gl_1$ case is a product of $24$ rank-$1$
 Yangians, each well-defined independently.
\item \emph{Coproduct from $K3 \times E$ factorization}: requires CY-A$_3$
 (proved in the infinity-categorical framework, Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}; chain-level data conditional).
\item \emph{Bar Euler $=$ BKM denominator}: requires CY-A$_3$ $+$
 Vol~I anchor (CY-A$_3$ proved; Vol~I anchor conditional).
\end{enumerate}
Layers (i)--(ii) close at abelian~$\gl_1$.
Layer~(iii) requires chain-level framing data (CY-A$_3$ is proved
in the infinity-categorical framework, but explicit chain-level
construction for non-formal algebras remains open).
Layer~(iv) requires CY-A$_3$ (proved in the $(\infty,1)$-categorical
lane; chain-level conjectural) \emph{plus} the Vol~I Borcherds-lift
bridge (conditional).
The abelian part is accessible now; the nonabelian
factorization-homology identifications and the bar Euler--BKM bridge
require chain-level data and the Vol~I anchor.

\item \emph{Coassociativity obstruction at rank~$24$.}
The $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ matrix Lax analysis
(\texttt{sl2\_matrix\_lax\_engine.py})
showed that trace-coassociativity holds while entry-wise coassociativity
fails for non-commuting tensor factors. At rank~$24$ with the
Mukai pairing, the indefinite signature introduces no additional
obstruction: for $\gl_1$, the transfer matrix
$T_{K3}(u) = \prod_{i=1}^{24}(u - \phi_i)$ is a scalar polynomial,
so trace-coassociativity \emph{equals} entry-wise coassociativity
(both reduce to associativity of scalar multiplication).
The Mukai pairing enters the \emph{commutation relations}
$[\phi_{i,m}, \phi_{j,n}] = \omega^{ij} m \delta_{m+n,0}$,
not the product structure of~$T(u)$.
For non-abelian~$\fg$, entry-wise coassociativity fails (as for
$\mathfrak{sl}_2$), but trace-coassociativity holds by associativity
of matrix multiplication.
Numerical verification at rank~$24$: both iterated-coproduct paths
give identical values at test points $(u, z, w) = (37, 11/3, 7/5)$
.
Thus coassociativity imposes no additional obstruction at the abelian
rank-$24$ level.
\end{enumerate}
\emph{Overall assessment}: the K3 Yangian conjecture for $\gl_1$ at
generic moduli is internally consistent. Two genuine issues are
identified: (A1)~the $\omega$-twisted crossing symmetry for
non-abelian~$\fg$ requires super-Yangian or orthosymplectic
adaptation; (A5)~the deeper identifications (coproduct as
factorization, bar Euler as BKM denominator) require chain-level
framing data (for non-formal algebras) and the Vol~I Borcherds-lift
bridge, respectively. CY-A$_3$ itself is proved (inf-cat,
Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}).
Neither issue breaks the abelian construction.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[K3 Yangian conjecture scoping]
\label{rem:k3-yangian-scoping}
The obstruction analysis (Remark~\ref{rem:k3-yangian-obstruction-tests}) establishes
that the K3 Yangian conjecture (Conjecture~\ref{conj:k3-yangian}) has
\emph{four mathematical layers}. Results that invoke
layer~(iii) (the coproduct interpretation via $K3 \times E$
factorization) require chain-level framing data beyond the
infinity-categorical CY-A$_3$ proof. Results that invoke
layer~(iv) (the bar Euler--BKM identification) require CY-A$_3$
(proved in the $(\infty,1)$-categorical lane; chain-level conjectural)
plus the Vol~I Borcherds-lift bridge (conditional);
such results must state those two hypotheses explicitly.
Results that invoke only layers~(i)--(ii) (the classical DCA $\fg_{K3}$
and the abelian quantization) require only CY-A$_2$ (proved) plus
the open quantization step.
\end{remark}


%% ========================================================================
%% The E_8 x E_8 maximal enhancement
%% ========================================================================

\subsection{The \texorpdfstring{$E_8 \times E_8$}{E-8 x E-8} maximal enhancement}
\label{subsec:k3-e8e8-maximal}

At the $E_8 \times E_8$ maximal enhancement point on K3 moduli, the Mukai lattice decomposes as
\begin{equation}\label{eq:k3-e8e8-decomposition}
 \widetilde{\Lambda}
 \;=\;
 E_8(-1) \;\oplus\; E_8(-1) \;\oplus\; U^4,
\end{equation}
where $E_8(-1)^2$ occupies $16$ of the $20$ negative Mukai directions and the orthogonal complement $U^4$ has signature~$(4,4)$. This is the \emph{largest} ADE enhancement on K3: the root lattice $E_8 \oplus E_8$ is the unique rank-$16$ even unimodular sublattice that embeds into the negative part of~$\widetilde{\Lambda}$.

\begin{conjecture}[\texorpdfstring{$E_8 \times E_8$}{E-8 x E-8} Yangian]
\label{conj:k3-e8e8-yangian}
At the $E_8 \times E_8$ enhancement point, the conjectural K3 Yangian~$Y(\fg_{K3})$ contains the subalgebra
\[
 Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{e}}_8)_1
 \;\otimes\;
 Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{e}}_8)_1
 \;\otimes\;
 Y(\mathfrak{h}_8),
\]
where $Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{e}}_8)_1$ denotes the Yangian of the affine Kac--Moody algebra $\widehat{\mathfrak{e}}_8$ at level~$1$, and $Y(\mathfrak{h}_8)$ is an abelian rank-$8$ Heisenberg Yangian from the $U^4$ complement.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Classical attribution]
\label{rem:k3-e8e8-classical}
The lattice decomposition $\widetilde{\Lambda} = E_8(-1) \oplus E_8(-1)
\oplus U^4$ and the central-charge identity
$c(\widehat{\mathfrak{e}}_8, k=1) = 8$ are classical Kac--Moody
root-space decomposition: Kac--Wakimoto \cite{KacWakimoto1988} established
the modular-invariance structure of affine-algebra characters, and the
level-$1$ $\widehat{\mathfrak{e}}_8$ character as the $E_8$ theta function
divided by $\eta^8$ is Frenkel--Kac--Segal lattice construction (1980).
The Freudenthal--de Vries strange formula
$\dim(\fg) = \mathrm{rank}(\fg) \cdot (1 + h^\vee)$ appears in
Freudenthal (1954) and de Vries (1969). The $E_8 \times E_8$
enhancement as the unique rank-$16$ even unimodular sublattice
of the Mukai lattice is due to Nikulin (1980). The identification ties
this classical lattice data to the $E_8 \times E_8$ Yangian subalgebra
of the conjectural K3 Yangian and the attendant $R$-matrix block
decomposition: a recasting of Kac--Wakimoto level-$1$ structure in the
$E_1$-chiral / bar-cobar framework. The level-$1$ Lax operator acting on the adjoint
$248$-dimensional representation is the classical minimal-dimension
$E_8$ fact; its interpretation as an RTT matrix of size $61{,}504$ is
the classical FRT formalism applied at the enhancement point.
Conjecture~\ref{conj:k3-e8e8-yangian} itself remains conjectural: the
containment claim requires a K3 Yangian whose construction is open.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{Central charge accounting.}
Each $\widehat{\mathfrak{e}}_8$ factor at level~$1$ contributes
\[
 c(\widehat{\mathfrak{e}}_8, k\!=\!1)
 \;=\;
 \frac{\dim(\mathfrak{e}_8)}{1 + h^\vee(\mathfrak{e}_8)}
 \;=\;
 \frac{248}{31}
 \;=\; 8,
\]
by the Freudenthal--de~Vries formula $\dim(\fg) = \mathrm{rank}(\fg) \cdot (1 + h^\vee)$ for simply-laced~$\fg$. The Heisenberg complement contributes $c = 8$ ($8$~free bosons). The total is $c = 8 + 8 + 8 = 24$, matching the Mukai rank and the generic-point central charge.

\paragraph{The adjoint is the smallest representation.}
$E_8$ is \emph{unique} among simple Lie algebras: its adjoint representation of dimension~$248$ is simultaneously its smallest representation. Consequently, the level-$1$ Lax operator $L(u)$ of $Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{e}}_8)_1$ acts on~$\bC^{248}$ (the adjoint), producing a $248 \times 248$ matrix. For every other simple Lie algebra, the Lax at level~$1$ acts on a fundamental representation of dimension strictly less than~$\dim(\fg)$. This means the Yang $R$-matrix entering the RTT relation $R_{12}(u-v)\,L_1(u)\,L_2(v) = L_2(v)\,L_1(u)\,R_{12}(u-v)$ is a $248^2 \times 248^2 = 61{,}504 \times 61{,}504$ matrix: the largest among all ADE Yangians at level~$1$.

\paragraph{Structure function.}
The degree-$(24, 24)$ K3 structure function factors as
\begin{equation}\label{eq:k3-e8e8-structure-fn}
 g_{K3}(z)
 \;=\;
 g_{E_8}^{(1)}(z) \cdot g_{E_8}^{(2)}(z) \cdot g_{U^4}(z),
\end{equation}
where $g_{E_8}^{(k)}(z) = \prod_{i=1}^{8} (z - h_i^{(k)})/(z + h_i^{(k)})$ has degree~$(8,8)$ and $g_{U^4}(z) = \prod_{j=1}^{8}(z - h_j^{(\perp)})/(z + h_j^{(\perp)})$ has degree~$(8,8)$. The total degree is $(8 + 8 + 8,\, 8 + 8 + 8) = (24, 24)$, counting one factor per Mukai lattice direction.

\begin{remark}[Degree clarification]
\label{rem:k3-e8e8-degree}
The structure function degree is $(24, 24)$ (the Mukai rank), \emph{not} $(500, 500)$. The dimension $\dim(\mathfrak{e}_8 \oplus \mathfrak{e}_8) = 496$ enters through the $R$-matrix block size, not through the structure function degree. Each $h_i$ parameter corresponds to a Mukai lattice direction, and there are $24$ such directions regardless of the enhancement type.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{Serre ideal.}
By Mukai lattice orthogonality (Conjecture~\ref{conj:k3-serre-enhanced}), the Serre ideal decomposes as
\begin{equation}\label{eq:k3-e8e8-serre}
 I_{\mathrm{Serre}}
 \;=\;
 I_{E_8}^{(1)} \;\oplus\; I_{E_8}^{(2)} \;\oplus\; I_{\mathrm{ab}},
\end{equation}
with \emph{no mixing} between the two $E_8$ factors or between $E_8$ and the abelian complement. The mechanism is $\omega^{ab} = 0$ for cross-sector pairs, which forces $g_{\mathrm{mix}}(z) = 1$ (Remark~\ref{rem:k3-serre-mixing-mechanism}). Each $I_{E_8}^{(k)}$ contributes $7$ nontrivial quantum Serre relations (one per edge of the $E_8$ Dynkin diagram), giving $14$ total nontrivial Serre relations. The abelian ideal $I_{\mathrm{ab}}$ imposes $\binom{8}{2} = 28$ commutativity relations on the $U^4$ complement.

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{$R$}{R}-matrix block structure.}
The charge-$1$ $R$-matrix decomposes into blocks:
\[
 R_{K3}(z) \;=\;
 \begin{pmatrix}
 R_{E_8}^{(1)}(z) & 0 & 0 \\
 0 & R_{E_8}^{(2)}(z) & 0 \\
 0 & 0 & R_{U^4}(z)
 \end{pmatrix},
\]
where $R_{E_8}^{(k)}(z)$ is a $248 \times 248$ block and $R_{U^4}(z)$ is $8 \times 8$ diagonal. The off-diagonal blocks vanish by the same Mukai orthogonality that kills the mixing Serre. For the abelian ($\gl_1$) case, all blocks are diagonal; for the non-abelian enhancement, the $E_8$ blocks acquire off-diagonal entries from the $\mathfrak{e}_8$ structure constants.

\paragraph{Connection to the heterotic string.}
The $E_8 \times E_8$ heterotic string (Gross--Harvey--Martinec--Rohm, 1985) compactified on K3 yields precisely this maximal enhancement. In the M-theory lift (Ho\v{r}ava--Witten, 1996), the two $E_8$ factors reside on the two boundaries of the interval $S^1/\bZ_2$. The level-$1$ Kac--Moody currents $\widehat{\mathfrak{e}}_8 \oplus \widehat{\mathfrak{e}}_8$ in the worldsheet CFT correspond to the Yangian generators at the $E_8 \times E_8$ enhancement point. This identification requires CY-A$_2$ (proved at $d = 2$) but \emph{not} CY-A$_3$.

\paragraph{The Mukai and Leech lattices.}
The Mukai lattice $\widetilde{\Lambda}$ and the Leech lattice $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Leech}}$ both have rank~$24$ but \emph{different signatures}: $(4, 20)$ versus $(0, 24)$. One cannot obtain a definite lattice by removing directions from an indefinite one. The connection is instead through Mathieu moonshine: the K3 elliptic genus exhibits $M_{24}$~symmetry, and $M_{24} < \mathrm{Co}_0 = \mathrm{Aut}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{Leech}})$. Whether this reflects a deeper structural relationship is open.

\paragraph{Shadow class.}
At the $E_8 \times E_8$ point, the $\widehat{\mathfrak{e}}_8 \oplus \widehat{\mathfrak{e}}_8$ subalgebra is class~$L$ (shadow depth~$3$: $S_3 \neq 0$ from the Sugawara term, $S_4 = 0$ at level~$1$), while the $U^4$ complement is class~$G$ (Heisenberg). The full sigma model remains class~$M$ at all K3 moduli points. The value $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 2$ is independent of the enhancement type.

\smallskip\noindent
\textit{Verification}: $92$~tests in \texttt{k3\_yangian\_e8e8.py} covering Mukai decomposition ($9$~tests), central charge accounting ($5$~tests), $E_8$ root system data ($8$~tests), Yangian parameters and CY$_2$ constraint ($6$~tests), structure function factorisation, unitarity, and degree analysis ($11$~tests), Serre ideal decomposition and orthogonality ($12$~tests), $R$-matrix block structure ($4$~tests), $E_8$ Lax operator ($4$~tests), heterotic string connection ($6$~tests), Leech lattice analysis ($3$~tests), shadow class ($6$~tests), enhancement comparison landscape ($5$~tests), constants consistency ($11$~tests), and full end-to-end verification ($2$~tests).


%% ========================================================================
%% Heterotic/type II duality and the K3 Yangian
%% ========================================================================

\subsection{Heterotic/type~II duality and the K3 Yangian}
\label{subsec:k3-heterotic-typeII}

The $E_8\times E_8$ heterotic string on K3 is dual to type~IIA on K3
(Hull--Townsend 1994; Witten 1995), placing the Yangian parameters
$(h_1,\ldots,h_{24})$ subject to $\sum h_i=0$ on the Narain moduli space
\begin{equation}\label{eq:narain-moduli}
 \mathcal{N}_{4,20}
 \;=\;
 O(4,20;\bZ)\big\backslash O(4,20;\bR)\big/\bigl(O(4)\times O(20)\bigr),
\end{equation}
with Narain lattice $\Gamma^{4,20}\simeq\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}$. The
duality
\begin{equation}\label{eq:het-iia-coupling}
 g_s^{\mathrm{het}} \;\longleftrightarrow\; t^{\mathrm{IIA}} = 1/g_s^{\mathrm{het}}
\end{equation}
exchanges heterotic coupling with the type~IIA K\"ahler modulus; the
remaining $79$ moduli are identified. The structure function
$g_{K3}(z) = \prod_{i=1}^{24}(z-h_i)/(z+h_i)$ is duality-invariant, and
in the weak-coupling limit $g_s^{\mathrm{het}}\to 0$ one has
$Y(\fg_{K3})\to U(\fg_{K3})$ with $\hbar\sim g_s^{\mathrm{het}}$. For the
abelian Heisenberg branch (class~$G$), the structure function is
moduli-independent and the tree-level description is exact; the bar
Euler product $\eta(q)^{24}$ is deformation-invariant at all orders.

\begin{conjecture}[D-brane/BKM dictionary and Fricke involution]
\label{conj:k3-dbrane-bkm}
Type~IIA D-branes with Mukai vector $v=(r,C,n)\in\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}$
and BKM discriminant $D=rn-C^2/2$ provide the protected
imaginary-root coefficients of $\fg_{\Delta_5}$: at $D=-1$ the Weyl
vector polar orbit has total multiplicity $2$ with coefficient
$c(-1)=1$ on each $l=\pm1$ monomial (D4/anti-D0 with $r=1$,
$C^2=0$, $n=-1$); at $D=0$ lightlike roots have protected coefficient
$c(0)=10$ fixing $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}=5$; at $D=3$ the first
fermionic sector has protected coefficient $c(3)=-64$ (D4--D2--D0 on
a $(-4)$-curve).
Heterotically these are NS5-brane and worldsheet instantons, with BPS
mass $M_{\mathrm{BPS}}^2\sim r^2 t^2 + |C^2| + n^2/t^2$ realising the
strong/weak self-duality; the Borcherds denominator encodes both additive
(Saito--Kurokawa) and multiplicative (Borcherds) expansions. The Fricke
involution $W_N\colon\tau\mapsto -1/(N\tau)$ acts on the moduli of
Yangians through
\begin{equation}\label{eq:fricke-eta}
 \eta\bigl(-1/(N\tau)\bigr)^{24} \;=\; (N\tau/i)^{12}\cdot\eta(N\tau)^{24},
\end{equation}
fixing $\tau_*=i/\sqrt{N}$; at $N=1$, $W_1=S$ with $g_s^{\mathrm{het}}=1$
(strong = weak). For the abelian class-$G$ Yangian the Fricke action on
$(h_1,\ldots,h_{24})$ is trivial; $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}=2$ is
Fricke-preserved for all $N$. The dictionary relies on CY-A$_2$ and the
Vol~I Borcherds lift.
\end{conjecture}

The Narain-moduli and Niemeier-enhancement geography is developed in
\cite{VolI},
Section~\ref{sec:k3-moduli-niemeier-landscape}.


%% ========================================================================
%% The Conway group, the Leech lattice, and the K3 Yangian
%% ========================================================================

\subsection{The Conway group, the Leech lattice, and the K3 Yangian}
\label{subsec:conway-leech-k3}

The Leech lattice $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Leech}}$ is the unique rootless
even unimodular rank-$24$ lattice, Niemeier $\#1$ in the Niemeier 1973
classification, with $\mathrm{Co}_0=\mathrm{Aut}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{Leech}})$
of order $2^{22}\cdot 3^9\cdot 5^4\cdot 7^2\cdot 11\cdot 13\cdot 23$.
The Mukai lattice $\widetilde\Lambda = U^3\oplus E_8(-1)^2$ and
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{Leech}}$ share rank $24$ but signatures $(4,20)$
versus $(0,24)$; no lattice isometry exists, but the common rank
arises from $24 = \dim H^*(S,\bC) = b_2(S)+2 = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)$
and the embedding $\Lambda_{K3}\oplus U\hookrightarrow N$ into a
Niemeier lattice. Dimension $24$ is the unique rigidity point of the
finite Niemeier classification (Niemeier 1973, Conway--Sloane 1999).
At the Leech enhancement point, the Niemeier lattice is rootless: no
enhanced Kac--Moody subalgebra, all $24$ Yangian parameters $h_i$ free
modulo $\sum h_i = 0$, $\mathrm{Co}_0 < O(24,\bZ)$ acting by orthogonal
transformations, and $V_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{Leech}}}$ carrying $c=24$,
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(V_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{Leech}}})=24$
of class~$G$ at depth~$2$ (K3 sigma model retains $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}=2$).
The $23$ deep holes of $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Leech}}$ are in bijection with
the $23$ other Niemeier lattices and give $23$ enhancement directions;
the class-$\mathcal S$ $\mathcal T[A_{N-1},\Sigma_{0,24}]$ Siegel-weight
ladder $k_N^{\mathrm{honest}}=N+3$ (Vol~I
\cite{VolI},
\cite{VolI}) lands on the Niemeier root systems
via $h(A_{N-1})=N$, with Conway moonshine at $N=24$.

$M_{24}<\mathrm{Co}_0$ stabilises a frame ($48$ vectors, $24$ antipodal
pairs; Curtis 1976), index $[\mathrm{Co}_0:M_{24}] = 33{,}965{,}714{,}432{,}000$.
The $M_{24}$ action on the K3 elliptic genus
(Conjecture~\ref{conj:mathieu-moonshine-yangian}) sees the permutation
sector $24|_{M_{24}} = 1+23$; $\mathrm{Co}_0$ also acts by
orthogonal reflections mixing the $24$ Mukai directions and transitively
on the $196{,}560$ norm-$4$ vectors of $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Leech}}$ (point
stabiliser $\mathrm{Co}_2$). The sporadic tower $M_{24}\subset
\mathrm{Co}_0\subset$(via $V^\natural$) $\mathbb{M}$ terminates at the
Monster VOA with $c=24$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(V^\natural)=12$;
the K3 Yangian has no direct action on $V^\natural$, the link going
through $\mathrm{Co}_1<\mathbb{M}$. The free-field character
$1/\eta(\tau)^{24} = q^{-1}(1 + 24q + 324q^2 + \cdots)$ matches
$J(\tau) + 24 = q^{-1}(1 + 24q + 196884q^2 + \cdots)$ at $q^0$ and
diverges at $q^1$ by $196884 - 324 = 196560$ (Leech kissing number);
McKay $196884 = 196883 + 1$ connects this to the smallest faithful
Monster representation.

\begin{conjecture}[Conway moonshine through the K3 Yangian]
\label{conj:conway-moonshine-yangian}
At the Leech enhancement point, $\mathrm{Co}_0$ acts on
$\Rep(Y(\fg_{K3}(\mathrm{Leech})))$ by orthogonal transformations of
the $24$ Yangian parameters; the twined partition functions
\[
Z_g(\tau) \;=\; \Tr_{\Rep(Y(\fg_{K3}(\mathrm{Leech})))}
\bigl(g\cdot q^{L_0 - c/24}\bigr), \qquad g\in\mathrm{Co}_0,
\]
are genus-zero Hauptmoduls (Conway moonshine). In particular,
$Z_1(\tau) = J(\tau)+24$ and $Z_{-I}(\tau) = J(\tau)+24$.
\end{conjecture}

The umbral BKM census attached to the $23$ non-Leech Niemeier lattices
is developed in
\cite{VolI},
Section~\ref{sec:k3ca-umbral-bkm-census}; the Monster/BKM chapter
framework is in
\cite{VolI}.

\begin{remark}[Niemeier enhancement directions are not compact Yangian hosts]
\label{rem:k3yang-niemeier-not-hosts}
The \(23\) non-Leech Niemeier lattices give enhancement directions and
umbral shadows for the K3 Yangian parameter space. They do not give
compact K3 Hodge-parity Yangian globalisations. The data verified in
the Niemeier engines are: rank \(24\), root-system decomposition,
frame-shape eta products, Coxeter-centred parameters satisfying
\(\sum_i h_i=0\), and mock-modular polar coefficient
\(-\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(K3)=-2\). These are necessary consistency
checks for a Niemeier specialisation of the abelian branch. They do
not construct the non-Kac \(Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4\mid20))\)
reflection equation, a global \(R\)-matrix on compact K3 moduli, or a
Hall--Drinfeld double.

Thus a statement of the form ``the Niemeier sibling \(N\) produces a
compact K3 Hodge-parity Yangian'' is stronger than the available
witnesses. The established content is the Coxeter/umbral
specialisation of the rank-\(24\) parameter set. The compact Hall
globalisation of the Hodge-parity Yangian remains the conjectural
content of Conjecture~\ref{conj:k3-super-yangian}.
\end{remark}


%% ========================================================================
%% The K3 Yangian attached to the Mukai form: so(4,20) trichotomy
%% ========================================================================

\subsection{The K3 Yangian and the \texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak{so}(4,20)$}{so(4,20)} vs \texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak{osp}(4 \mid 20)$}{osp(4|20)} trichotomy}
\label{subsec:k3-super-yangian}

The Mukai form on $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}} \otimes_\Z \bR$ is a
symmetric indefinite bilinear form of signature $(4, 20)$. Its
symmetry group is the indefinite orthogonal group $\mathrm{O}(4, 20)$
and its preserving Lie algebra is $\mathfrak{so}(4, 20)$ of type
$D_{12}$. Three form-preserving Lie-(super)algebras present themselves
as candidates for the K3 Yangian envelope, and only one of them is
selected by the restriction of the Mukai pairing to graded subspaces:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
\item $\mathfrak{so}(4, 20)$ -- the ungraded orthogonal Lie algebra of
 type $D_{12}$ preserving the Mukai form. This is the classical
 limit of the K$3$ Yangian on the ungraded Mukai lattice.
\item $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$ -- the programme-specific
 \emph{Hodge-parity $\Z/2$-super-extension}, symmetric on both the
 even part $V_+ = \C^4$ and the odd part $V_- = \C^{20}$. This is
 \emph{not} a Kac simple Lie superalgebra: Kac's classification
 \cite{Kac1977} requires the defining form to be symplectic on the
 odd part.
\item $\mathfrak{osp}(4 \mid 20)$ -- the Kac orthosymplectic simple
 Lie superalgebra, whose defining form is symmetric on $\C^4$ and
 \emph{symplectic} on $\C^{20}$. The Mukai pairing is symmetric on
 both parts, so $\mathfrak{osp}(4 \mid 20)$ is explicitly \emph{not}
 the Mukai-preserving algebra.
\end{enumerate}

The obstruction analysis (Remark~\ref{rem:k3-yangian-obstruction-tests},
Test~A1) tracks the Mukai-Hodge involution $\theta$ acting on
$V = \widetilde H^*(K3, \C) = V_+ \oplus V_-$, $\dim V_+ = 4$,
$\dim V_- = 20$, with $V_+ = H^0 \oplus H^4 \oplus (H^{2,0} \oplus
H^{0,2})$ the K\"ahler-plus-unit/top sector and $V_- = H^{1,1}_{\mathrm{prim}}$.
The Mukai pairing restricts to a symmetric \emph{positive-definite} form on
$V_+$ and to a symmetric form of signature $(0, 20)$ on $V_-$; both
restrictions are symmetric, neither is symplectic. This is exactly
the content of the trichotomy above.

\begin{remark}[Stage-\texorpdfstring{$2$}{2} scope: two distinct Yangian envelopes]
\label{rem:k3y-stage-super-yangian-scope}
Two envelopes are on offer at the stage-$2$ $\SpCh$-shadow of the
canonical stage-$1$ functor. The first is the \emph{ungraded} orthogonal
Yangian $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$ of Molev--Ragoucy
\cite{MolevRagoucy2008}, attached to the Mukai form without any parity
grading. The second is the programme-specific Hodge-parity
$\Z/2$-super-extension $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$, obtained by
refining the Gerstenhaber bracket on $\HH^\bullet(D^b\Coh(K3))$ through
the Hodge-parity $\Z/2$-grading of
\eqref{eq:k3yang-hodge-parity-grading}: the defining bilinear form
remains symmetric on both $V_+$ and $V_-$, distinguishing this
construction from Kac's orthosymplectic series (where the form on the
odd part is symplectic).
\[
 Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))
 \;\stackrel{?}{=}\;
 \SpCh_{\Sigma_1, C}\!\bigl(\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}, \mathrm{super}}_2(D^b\Coh(K3))\bigr).
\]
The super-enhancement $\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}, \mathrm{super}}_2$ is not
constructed in the present volume; both its existence and its
$\SpCh$-specialisation to $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$ remain
conjectural. Kac $\mathfrak{osp}(4 \mid 20)$ is explicitly excluded
from both stages: the Mukai pairing is symmetric on the Hodge-odd
$20$-dimensional sector, and Kac's series requires a symplectic form
there.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Mukai form-preserving Yangian envelopes at rank \texorpdfstring{$(4,20)$}{(4,20)}]
\label{conj:osp-yangian-mukai}
Let $\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ be the Mukai form on
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}} \otimes_\Z \C$, symmetric of signature
$(4, 20)$. Two Yangian envelopes are attached to $\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}$.

\textup{(I) Ungraded orthogonal Yangian.} The Molev--Ragoucy extended
orthogonal Yangian $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$ is defined by the
$\mathfrak{so}$-type reflection-equation RTT presentation
\[
 R_{12}(u - v)\, T_1(u)\, K_2(u + v)\, R_{21}(u + v)\, T_2(v)
 \;=\;
 T_2(v)\, R_{12}(u + v)\, K_1(u + v)\, T_1(u)\, R_{21}(u - v),
\]
with rational $\mathfrak{so}$ $R$-matrix of Zamolodchikov--Zamolodchikov
and $K(u)$ a Sklyanin--Molev--Ragoucy orthogonal reflection matrix
encoding the indefinite signature. This is an ungraded associative
algebra; existence is a theorem \cite{MolevRagoucy2008}.

\textup{(II) Hodge-parity $\Z/2$-theta-fixed envelope.} The
programme-specific $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$ is the
$\theta$-fixed Hodge-parity refinement of the ambient super-Yangian
$Y(\mathfrak{gl}(4 \mid 20))$, attached to the Mukai--Hodge
polarisation $V = V_+ \oplus V_-$, $\dim V_+ = 4$,
$\dim V_- = 20$, and defined by:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item Underlying $\Z/2$-graded vector space $V = V_+ \oplus \Pi V_-$,
 with the defining bilinear form $(\cdot, \cdot)_\omega$ \emph{symmetric
 on both} $V_+$ and $V_-$ (this is the feature that distinguishes the
 Hodge-parity extension from Kac $\mathfrak{osp}(4 \mid 20)$, whose
 form on the odd part is symplectic).
\item $\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20)$ is the $\Z/2$-graded Lie algebra of
 endomorphisms preserving $(\cdot, \cdot)_\omega$ in the graded sense;
 its even part is $\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20)_{\bar 0} = \mathfrak{so}(4)
 \oplus \mathfrak{so}(20)$ of dimension
 $\binom{4}{2} + \binom{20}{2} = 6 + 190 = 196$.
\item $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$ is presented by the
 $\theta$-fixed reflection-equation subalgebra
 $Y_\theta^{(4 \mid 20)} = Y(\mathfrak{gl}(4 \mid 20))^\theta$ of
 Definition~\ref{def:r10-Y-theta-ch}; its classical limit is
 $U(\mathfrak{so}(4,20)[u])$ by
 Theorem~\ref{thm:r10-classical-limit-ch}.
\end{enumerate}
Envelope \textup{(I)} is unconditional by Molev--Ragoucy. Envelope
\textup{(II)} is unconditional as the displayed $\theta$-fixed
associative/current-level algebra. What is not claimed here is the
separate identification of either envelope with the completed
Hall--Drinfeld double of $\CoHA(K3\times E)$, nor the existence of a
super-$\PhiFA_2$ functor whose stage-$2$ specialisation is envelope
\textup{(II)}. Those are exactly the Hall-pairing, coproduct,
completion, centre, and associator compatibilities isolated by
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3e-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[Mukai reflection matrix and $R$-matrix consistency at rank $(4,20)$]
\label{prop:mukai-K-matrix-and-YBE}
Let $V = V_+ \oplus V_- = \C^4 \oplus \C^{20}$ with the symmetric
Mukai form $(\cdot,\cdot)_\omega$ of
Theorem~\ref{conj:osp-yangian-mukai}. Let
\[
 R^{\so}(u) \;=\; \mathrm{Id} \;+\; \frac{\hbar}{u}\, P
 \;-\; \frac{\hbar}{u + \hbar\, \kappa_{\so}/2}\, Q,
 \qquad
 \kappa_{\so} \;=\; N - 2 \;=\; 22,
\]
be the rational $\mathfrak{so}(N)$ $R$-matrix at $N = 4 + 20 = 24$
\textup{(}Zamolodchikov--Zamolodchikov; Reshetikhin\textup{)}, with
$P$ the ungraded permutation, $Q$ the $\mathfrak{so}$-invariant
trace-like projector, $P^2 = \mathrm{Id}$, $P Q = Q P = Q$,
$Q^2 = N\, Q = 24\, Q$. Set
\[
 K_{\mathrm{Muk}}(u) \;=\; \operatorname{diag}(\mathrm{Id}_{V_+},\, -\,\mathrm{Id}_{V_-})
 \;+\; \frac{\hbar}{u}\,\Pi_{+-}
 \;+\; O(\hbar^2),
\]
where $\Pi_{+-}$ is the $(\mathfrak{so}(4) \oplus \mathfrak{so}(20))$-invariant
projector onto the mixed-block sector $V_+ \otimes V_-$. Then
\textup{(i)} $R^{\so}(u)$ satisfies the ungraded Yang--Baxter equation
on $V^{\otimes 3}$ at $N = 24$; \textup{(ii)} $K_{\mathrm{Muk}}(u)$
satisfies Sklyanin's reflection equation against $R^{\so}$ for the
symmetric pair $\mathfrak{so}(4, 20) \supset \mathfrak{so}(4) \oplus
\mathfrak{so}(20)$; \textup{(iii)} the reflection-equation RTT
presentation of envelope~(I) in
Theorem~\ref{conj:osp-yangian-mukai} is a consistent system of
quadratic relations, and the associative algebra
$Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$ of
Definition~\ref{def:osp-super-yangian-K3} exists.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\textup{(i)} The rational $\mathfrak{so}(N)$ YBE is classical
\cite{Reshetikhin1985, MolevRagoucy2008}: the proof uses only
$P^2 = \mathrm{Id}$, $P Q = Q P = Q$, and the trace normalisation
$Q^2 = N\, Q$. At $N = 24$ the shift is $\kappa_{\so} = 22$; partial
fractions in the spectral parameters reduce YBE to scalar identities
that use only these three projector relations.
\textup{(ii)} At $\hbar = 0$, $K_{\mathrm{Muk}}(0) = \mathrm{diag}(I_4, -I_{20})$
is the Mukai--Hodge involution; its $(+1)$-eigenspace is $V_+$
(K\"ahler-plus-unit/top, dimension $4$), its $(-1)$-eigenspace is
$V_-$ (primary $(1, 1)$-sector, dimension $20$). This involution
commutes with $\mathfrak{so}(4) \oplus \mathfrak{so}(20)$ and
therefore normalises the $\mathfrak{so}(24)$ $R$-matrix after
reduction to the symmetric pair. The first-order correction
$\Pi_{+-}/u$ is the unique $(\mathfrak{so}(4) \oplus
\mathfrak{so}(20))$-invariant completion, forced by the
Molev--Ragoucy orthogonal-reflection-equation algebra
\cite{MolevRagoucy2008}; higher orders in $\hbar$ are determined
recursively by the reflection equation as a linear system in the
coefficients of $K(u)$.
\textup{(iii)} With $R^{\so}$ satisfying YBE and $K_{\mathrm{Muk}}$
satisfying the reflection equation, the Sklyanin argument exhibits
the reflection-equation RTT relations as a consistent system of
quadratic relations; the resulting associative algebra
$Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$ is filtered, with associated graded
$\mathcal U(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20)[u])$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Scope: finite envelopes are constructed; the Hall double is the remaining completion]
\label{rem:osp-scope-upgrade}
Proposition~\ref{prop:mukai-K-matrix-and-YBE} establishes envelope~(I)
of Theorem~\ref{conj:osp-yangian-mukai} at the associative-algebra
level: $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$ exists unconditionally. The
same theorem constructs envelope~(II) as the $\theta$-fixed
Hodge-parity algebra $Y_\theta^{(4\mid20)}$. What remains conjectural
is the identification of either finite-rank envelope with the
cohomological Hall double of $K3\times E$ as a completed
quasi-Hopf object (Conjecture~\ref{conj:k3-super-yangian}). The
existence of the ungraded algebra, the existence of the Hodge-parity
current algebra, and the Hall--Drinfeld completion are three separate
statements.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Compact Hall globalisation remains conjectural]
\label{rem:k3y-hodge-parity-globalisation-obstruction}
The compact K3 Hall globalisation cannot be obtained from the finite
Mukai envelope alone. The ungraded orthogonal Yangian
\(Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4,20))\) is supplied by the
Molev--Ragoucy orthogonal RTT/reflection-equation formalism after
choosing the Mukai form. The Hodge-parity object is different: it is
a non-Kac \(\mathbb Z/2\)-graded theta-fixed extension whose defining
form is symmetric on both Hodge-parity pieces. Kac
\(\mathfrak{osp}(4\mid20)\) does not apply, because its odd form is
symplectic.

The finite chain-level checks settle the \(416/160\) block count,
the Mukai involution, the theta-fixed presentation, and the
small-rank \(Y(\mathfrak{gl}(m\mid n))\) super-YBE sign grammar.
They do not verify the compact Hall pairing, PBW flatness over the
Borcherds cone, coproduct comparison, centre control, or
\v{C}ech--Ran descent over compact K3. Those data are the
chain-level witnesses required before the finite Hodge-parity algebra
can be promoted to a completed Hall--Drinfeld object.
\end{remark}

\begin{definition}[Orthogonal K$3$ Yangian \texorpdfstring{$Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$}{Y-so(4,20)} and Hodge-parity theta-fixed extension]
\label{def:osp-super-yangian-K3}
Fix the Mukai form $\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ of signature $(4, 20)$ on
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}} \otimes_\Z \C$. The K\"ahler-plus-unit/top
polarisation $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}} \otimes \C = V_+ \oplus V_-$
with $\dim V_+ = 4$, $\dim V_- = 20$ is preserved by the Hodge
involution $\theta$ of
Subsection~\ref{subsec:k3-super-yangian}.

\emph{Ungraded envelope.} The orthogonal K$3$ Yangian
$Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$ is the associative algebra over
$\C[[\hbar]]$ generated by $\{t_{ij}^{(r)}\}_{r \geq 0}$ assembled
into the formal Lax series
\[
 T(u) \;=\; \sum_{r \geq 0} t^{(r)}\, u^{-r}
 \;\in\; \End(V)[[u^{-1}, \hbar]], \qquad t^{(0)} \;=\; \mathrm{Id}_V,
\]
subject to the Molev--Ragoucy~\cite{MolevRagoucy2008}
reflection-equation RTT relation
\[
 R_{12}(u - v)\, T_1(u)\, K_2(u + v)\, R_{21}(u + v)\, T_2(v)
 \;=\;
 T_2(v)\, R_{12}(u + v)\, K_1(u + v)\, T_1(u)\, R_{21}(u - v),
\]
with rational Zamolodchikov $\mathfrak{so}$ $R$-matrix
\[
 R^{\so}(u) \;=\; \mathrm{Id}
 \;+\; \frac{\hbar}{u}\, P
 \;-\; \frac{\hbar}{u + \hbar\, \kappa_{\so}/2}\, Q,
 \qquad
 \kappa_{\so} \;=\; N - 2 \;=\; 22,
\]
where $P$ is the ungraded permutation, $Q$ the
$\mathfrak{so}(24)$-invariant trace-like projector, and $K(u)$ a
Sklyanin--Molev--Ragoucy orthogonal reflection matrix; at
$K(u) = \mathrm{Id}$ the relation reduces to ungraded RTT. The
Molev--Ragoucy Sklyanin determinant $\operatorname{sdet}^{\so}_q T(u)$
generates the centre of $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$.

\emph{Hodge-parity $\Z/2$-theta-fixed extension.} The programme-specific
$Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$ is the $\Z/2$-graded
$\theta$-fixed refinement $Y_\theta^{(4\mid20)}$ of
$Y(\mathfrak{gl}(4 \mid 20))$ in which $V_-$ carries odd parity
under the Hodge involution $\theta$, and the defining bilinear form
remains \emph{symmetric on both} $V_+$ and $V_-$. Its classical
limit is the $\Z/2$-graded Lie algebra $\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20)$
with even part $\mathfrak{so}(4) \oplus \mathfrak{so}(20)$ of
dimension $\binom{4}{2} + \binom{20}{2} = 6 + 190 = 196$ and odd
part $V_+ \otimes V_-$ of dimension $80$; total (super-)dimension
$276$. Kac's $\mathfrak{osp}(4 \mid 20)$, whose even part is
$\mathfrak{so}(4) \oplus \mathfrak{sp}(20)$ of dimension
$6 + 210 = 216$, is a \emph{different} Lie superalgebra: it requires
a symplectic form on the $20$-dimensional odd part, which the Mukai
pairing does not carry.

The signature-type discipline is: $\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ is
symmetric on both Hodge-graded parts of $V$; its preserving Lie
algebra is $\mathfrak{so}(4, 20)$ (Kac type $D_{12}$); a
$\Z/2$-graded refinement through Hodge parity is programme-specific
and non-Kac; Kac $\mathfrak{osp}(4 \mid 20)$ is \emph{not} the
envelope. All structural properties of envelope~(I) (Lax series,
reflection equation, Sklyanin determinant, centre) are inherited
\emph{verbatim} from the rank-$N$ construction of
Molev--Ragoucy~\cite{MolevRagoucy2008} at $N = 24$; envelope~(II)
is the programme-specific $\Z/2$-graded theta-fixed extension of
Theorem~\ref{conj:osp-yangian-mukai}. Its identification with a
completed Hall--Drinfeld double remains the separate comparison
problem of Theorem~\ref{thm:k3e-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion}.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Signature-type discipline: orthogonal, not orthosymplectic]
\label{rem:gl-to-osp-correction}
The Mukai form $\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ on $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ is
symmetric indefinite of signature $(4, 20)$, symmetric on both the
Hodge-even part $V_+$ and the Hodge-odd part $V_-$. Its preserving
Lie algebra is therefore $\mathfrak{so}(4, 20)$, of Kac type
$D_{12}$. A Kac simple Lie superalgebra of orthosymplectic type
$\mathfrak{osp}(m \mid n)$ requires the defining form to be symmetric
on the $m$-dimensional even part and \emph{symplectic} on the
$n$-dimensional odd part; since the Mukai form is symmetric on both
parts, $\mathfrak{osp}(4 \mid 20)$ is neither the stabiliser nor
the envelope. The programme's Hodge-parity $\Z/2$-super-extension
$Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$ is a distinct, non-Kac
theta-fixed finite current algebra whose graded form is symmetric on
both parts; its compact Hall--Drinfeld realisation is the conjectural
refinement. The small-rank RTT verifications of
\texttt{k3\_super\_yangian.py} at
$\gl(1 \mid 1)$ and $\gl(2 \mid 1)$ retain value as $\Z/2$-graded
warm-ups but do not constitute a proof at the $(4, 20)$ orthogonal
rank by themselves.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[K3 Yangian at ADE enhancement: ungraded and $\Z/2$-graded envelopes]
\label{conj:k3-super-yangian}
At an ADE enhancement point, the K$3$ stable-envelope/Yangian branch
(Remark~\ref{rem:k3-yangian-nomenclature-scope}) is governed by one
of the two form-preserving envelopes of
Theorem~\ref{conj:osp-yangian-mukai}: the ungraded orthogonal
Yangian $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$, or the Hodge-parity
$\Z/2$-super-extension $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$ expected
as the stage-$2$ shadow of the yet-to-be-constructed super-$\PhiFA_2$
(Remark~\ref{rem:k3y-stage-super-yangian-scope}). A compact Hall
realisation would be an additional comparison with the K3$\times E$
Hall positive half after the oriented compact CoHA, negative half,
Hopf pairing, coproduct, centre, and completion have been constructed;
it is not the Borcherds--Kac--Moody object and not a consequence of
the finite envelope. The Hodge-parity
splitting is
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}} \otimes \C = V_+ \oplus V_-$ induced by
 the Hodge involution $\theta$;
\item $V_+ = \C^4$ spanned by $H^0 \oplus H^4 \oplus (H^{2,0} \oplus
 H^{0,2})_\R$, carrying the positive part of the Mukai form
 (signature $(4, 0)$ on $V_+$);
\item $V_- = \C^{20}$ the primary $(1, 1)$-sector $H^{1,1}_{\mathrm{prim}}
 (\mathrm{K3}, \R)$, carrying the negative part of the Mukai form
 (signature $(0, 20)$ on $V_-$).
\end{enumerate}
The rank $N = \dim V_+ + \dim V_- = 24$ is the total Mukai rank;
the signature $(4, 20)$ is preserved by $\mathfrak{so}(4, 20)$ in
envelope~(I) and by the $\Z/2$-graded $\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20)$
(symmetric on both parts) in envelope~(II). Kac
$\mathfrak{osp}(4 \mid 20)$ is explicitly excluded: its defining
form is symplectic on the $20$-dimensional odd part, which the
Mukai pairing is not.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{theorem}[Finite Mukai orthogonal closure and the Hall--double boundary]
\label{thm:k3-mukai-orthogonal-finite-closure}
Let
\[
 \Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}\otimes\C
 = V_+ \oplus V_-,
 \qquad
 \dim V_+ = 4,\quad \dim V_- = 20,
\]
be the Hodge-polarised Mukai lattice of a K3 surface, with symmetric
Mukai form of signature $(4,20)$. Then the nonabelian K3 residual
has the following finite-rank closure.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item The ungraded form-preserving envelope
 $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4,20))$ exists as the Molev--Ragoucy
 orthogonal reflection-equation Yangian at $N=24$, with classical
 limit $U(\mathfrak{so}(4,20)[u])$.
\item The Hodge-parity envelope
 $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4\mid20))$ exists as the theta-fixed algebra
 $Y_\theta^{(4\mid20)}=Y(\mathfrak{gl}(4\mid20))^\theta$ of
 Definition~\ref{def:r10-Y-theta-ch}. Its classical limit is again
 $U(\mathfrak{so}(4,20)[u])$, now displayed with
 Hodge-parity blocks
 \[
 \dim\mathfrak{so}(4\mid20)_{\bar 0}
 = \binom{4}{2}+\binom{20}{2}=196,
 \qquad
 \dim\mathfrak{so}(4\mid20)_{\bar 1}=4\cdot20=80,
 \]
 so total dimension $196+80=276=\binom{24}{2}$.
\item The Mukai supertrace is
 \[
 \operatorname{sdim}_{\mathrm{Muk}}(V)
 = \dim V_+-\dim V_-=4-20=-16,
 \]
 and the rank/signature data satisfy the Pythagorean identity
 \[
 24^2=(4+20)^2=(4-20)^2+4\cdot4\cdot20
 =(-16)^2+320.
 \]
 The $320$ term is the two-sided mixed-block size
 $2\dim(V_+\otimes V_-)$; the theta-fixed orthogonal constraint halves
 it to the $80$ odd generators of the Lie algebra, while the ambient
 $\mathfrak{gl}(4\mid20)$ warm-up retains the larger $160$ one-sided
 off-diagonal footprint.
\item Neither finite envelope is the K3$\times E$ BKM Hall--Drinfeld
 double. The finite branch has type-$D_{12}$ current limit and
 polynomial finite root system; the BKM branch has
 $\Delta_5$-regulated Borcherds cone, imaginary simple roots, and
 signed protected root-character coefficients governed by the K3
 elliptic-genus coefficients. A
 quasi-Hopf isomorphism with
 $D^\wedge_\hbar(\CoHA(K3\times E))$ is therefore not a consequence
 of \textup{(i)}--\textup{(iii)}; it is equivalent to the seven
 Hall--Drinfeld compatibilities of
 Theorem~\ref{thm:k3e-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion}.
\end{enumerate}
Thus the orthogonal/Hodge-parity residual is closed at finite
RTT/current level. The remaining obstruction is exactly the completed
Hall package: nondegenerate Hall pairing, PBW flatness over the
Borcherds cone, coproduct comparison, centre control, and compatible
Siegel--Borcherds associator topology.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The Mukai lattice identity
$\widetilde H(K3,\Z)\simeq U^{\oplus 4}\oplus E_8(-1)^{\oplus 2}
\simeq \mathrm{II}_{4,20}$ gives rank $24$ and signature $(4,20)$.
Item \textup{(i)} is Theorem~\ref{conj:osp-yangian-mukai} and
Proposition~\ref{prop:mukai-K-matrix-and-YBE}: the rational
$\mathfrak{so}(N)$ $R$-matrix at $N=24$ satisfies YBE, and the
Molev--Ragoucy reflection equation gives a filtered associative
algebra with associated graded $U(\mathfrak{so}(4,20)[u])$.
Item \textup{(ii)} is Definition~\ref{def:r10-Y-theta-ch} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:r10-classical-limit-ch}: the $\theta$-fixed
subalgebra exists inside the ambient super-Yangian and its
$\hbar=0$ fixed algebra is $\mathfrak{so}(4,20)[u]$. The dimension
count follows from
$\dim\mathfrak{so}(4)=6$, $\dim\mathfrak{so}(20)=190$, and
$\dim(V_+\otimes V_-)=80$.

Item \textup{(iii)} is arithmetic: expand
$(p+q)^2=(p-q)^2+4pq$ at $(p,q)=(4,20)$. The supertrace
$p-q=-16$ is the Mukai Hodge-parity trace; the mixed-block term
$4pq=320$ is the full two-sided off-diagonal block count in
$V\otimes V$. Passing from the ambient matrix algebra to the
theta-fixed orthogonal algebra identifies transpose-paired mixed
blocks, leaving $pq=80$ odd generators.

Item \textup{(iv)} is the rank/root/multiplicity separation. The
finite orthogonal branch has Cartan rank $12$ over $\C$ and finite
type $D_{12}$ root system after complexification. The BKM branch of
Chapter~\ref{ch:k3e-bkm} is controlled by the Borcherds
$F_3$ core and the K3 elliptic-genus coefficients in the
$\Delta_5$ denominator. These root data cannot be identified by a
finite RTT theorem. The only possible completed comparison is the
Hall--Drinfeld criterion
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3e-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion}, whose
conditions are precisely the missing Hopf-theoretic and topological
data.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Hodge origin of the $(4, 20)$ split]
\label{rem:k3-super-origin}
The signature $(4, 20)$ is the Hodge--Mukai signature of
$H^*(\mathrm{K3}, \R)$ under the cup product twisted by
$\sqrt{\mathrm{td}(\mathrm{K3})}$. Negative-norm directions in
$V_-$ contribute reflection signs in the Molev--Ragoucy orthogonal
reflection equation because $\langle e_i, e_i\rangle_{\mathrm{Muk}}
= -1$ flips the diagonal of $K_{\mathrm{Muk}}(0)$. The Hodge
involution $\theta$ is the datum that refines envelope~(I) into
envelope~(II): its $(\pm 1)$-eigenspace decomposition endows $V$
with a $\Z/2$-grading under which the Mukai pairing remains symmetric
on \emph{both} graded parts, distinguishing the programme's non-Kac
$\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20)$ from Kac's $\mathfrak{osp}(4 \mid 20)$
(whose form is symplectic on the odd part). No fermionic BPS
quantum number enters.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Envelope (I) is primary; envelope (II) is a Hodge-parity refinement; Kac $\mathfrak{osp}$ is excluded]
\label{rem:so-4-20-alternative}
Envelope~(I), $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$, is the
Molev--Ragoucy~\cite{MolevRagoucy2008} indefinite-orthogonal Yangian
of the real form $\mathfrak{so}(4, 20) \subset \mathfrak{so}(24, \C)$.
The programme's Hodge-parity $\Z/2$-super-extension
$Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$ of envelope~(II) is a
$\Z/2$-graded refinement of envelope~(I) supported by the Hodge
involution; it is a distinct algebra from (I) in the same way that
a $\Z/2$-graded Lie algebra is distinct from its underlying ungraded
Lie algebra, and it is \emph{not} Kac's orthosymplectic
$\mathfrak{osp}(4 \mid 20)$ (whose form is symplectic on the
$20$-dimensional odd part). Which envelope is selected at a given
ADE enhancement is determined by whether the $\mathcal{N} = (2, 2)$
worldsheet boundary algebra of K$3$ carries a non-trivial
$\Z/2$-grading compatible with the Hodge involution; see
Frontier~F26.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Scope of the Kac-$\osp$ RTT warm-up that follows]
\label{rem:k3-kac-osp-warmup-scope}
The paragraphs below carry out the Kac $\mathfrak{osp}(m \mid n)$
RTT warm-up of Arnaudon--Cramp\'e--Doikou--Frappat--Ragoucy~\cite{AcdfR2003}
as a structural benchmark against which the orthogonal envelope of
Proposition~\ref{prop:mukai-K-matrix-and-YBE} is contrasted. The
Mukai-preserving K$3$ Yangian itself is
$Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$ and its Hodge-parity $\Z/2$-super-extension
$Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$; Kac $\mathfrak{osp}(4 \mid 20)$
is not the envelope. The formulas that follow at the Kac-$\osp$ rank
$(m, n) = (4, 20)$ are recorded for completeness of the comparison and
do \emph{not} claim to present the Mukai-preserving algebra -- that role
belongs to the ungraded orthogonal RTT of
Definition~\ref{def:osp-super-yangian-K3}.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{Super-permutation warm-up at \texorpdfstring{$\gl(m|n)$}{gl(m|n)}.}
For the $\fgl(m|n)$-series, whose relation to the K3 problem is
indirect (the $\fgl$-series preserves no invariant form on
$\C^{m|n}$ beyond the supertrace, while $\osp(m|n)$ is defined
by such a form), the super-permutation $P_s$ on $\bC^{m|n}
\otimes \bC^{m|n}$ acts by
\[
 P_s(e_i \otimes e_j) \;=\; (-1)^{p(i)\, p(j)}\, e_j \otimes e_i,
\]
where $p(i) = 0$ for $i \leq m$ and $p(i) = 1$ for $i > m$. The
key property $P_s^2 = \mathrm{Id}$ (since $(-1)^{2\,p(i)\,p(j)}
= 1$) gives the Yang $R$-matrix
$R_s(u) = u \cdot \mathrm{Id} + \hbar \cdot P_s$
standard unitarity
$R_s(u)\, R_s(-u) = (u^2 - \hbar^2)\, \mathrm{Id}$.
This was verified symbolically at $\gl(1|1)$ and $\gl(2|1)$ in
\texttt{k3\_super\_yangian.py} (59~tests). These small-rank
$\fgl$-computations serve as a technical warm-up: they confirm
the graded tensor-product signs and the Kulish--Sklyanin
embedding, both of which are inherited by the $\osp$
reflection-equation construction. They do \emph{not} verify the
$(4, 20)$-rank orthosymplectic super-Yangian of
Theorem~\ref{conj:osp-yangian-mukai}.

\paragraph{Orthosymplectic \texorpdfstring{$R$}{R}-matrix and reflection equation.}
For the $\osp(m|n)$-series, the Kulish--Reshetikhin rational
$R$-matrix is
\[
 R^{\osp}(u) \;=\; \mathrm{Id}
 \;+\; \frac{\hbar}{u}\, P_s
 \;-\; \frac{\hbar}{u + (m - n - 2)\hbar / 2}\, Q,
\]
where $Q = \sum_{i,j} (-1)^{p(j)} e_{ij} \otimes e_{\bar j \bar i}$
is the trace-like projector onto the $\osp$-invariant line, and
$\bar i$ is the involution on indices induced by the orthogonal
form. The third term is the novelty of the orthogonal case:
$Q$ projects onto the trivial representation inside
$V \otimes V$, present for $\osp$ (because $V$ carries an
invariant form) but absent for $\fgl$ (which has no such form).
The $\osp$-Yangian is defined by the reflection equation of
Theorem~\ref{conj:osp-yangian-mukai}, with $K(u) =
\mathrm{Id}$ recovering super-RTT on the $\osp$-invariant
subspace.

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{$T$}{T}-matrix block structure and the \texorpdfstring{$\osp(4 \mid 20)$}{osp(4 mid 20)} dimension count.}
The $\osp(4 \mid 20)$ Lie super-algebra has even part
$\osp(4 \mid 20)_{\bar 0} = \mathfrak{so}(4) \oplus \mathfrak{sp}(20)$
of dimension $\binom{4}{2} + \binom{21}{2} = 6 + 210 = 216$,
using $\dim \mathfrak{sp}(2r) = r(2r+1) = \binom{2r+1}{2}$ at
$r = 10$. The odd part
$\osp(4 \mid 20)_{\bar 1} = \C^4 \otimes \C^{20}$ has graded
dimension $4 \cdot 20 = 80$, giving total super-dimension
$\dim \osp(4 \mid 20) = 216 + 80 = 296$ (even plus odd, counted
once). The comparison algebra $\fgl(4 \mid 20)$ has dimension
$(4 + 20)^2 = 576$, so the orthosymplectic constraint cuts
$576 - 296 = 280$ degrees of freedom. At the $T$-matrix level,
the $\osp(4 \mid 20)$ Lax operator has $80$~fermionic off-diagonal
entries (the $V_+ \otimes V_-$ block), in contrast to the $2 \cdot
4 \cdot 20 = 160$ of the $\fgl(4 \mid 20)$ $T$-matrix; the
orthosymplectic crossing constraint
$T(u)^{\mathrm{st}} = T(-u - \kappa_{\osp})$
(Kulish--Reshetikhin) identifies the $B$- and $C$-blocks,
halving the $\fgl$ off-diagonal count and matching the $80$
mixed-sign off-diagonal pairs of $P_\omega^2$ modulo the
reflection symmetry $e_i \leftrightarrow e_{\bar i}$.

\paragraph{Quantum Berezinian and centre.}
For $Y(\osp(m|n))$ the analogue of the Nazarov quantum Berezinian
is the Molev--Ragoucy reflection Berezinian
\[
 \operatorname{sdet}_q^{\osp} T(u)
 \;=\;
 \operatorname{Ber}_q T(u) \cdot \operatorname{Ber}_q T(-u - \kappa_{\osp}),
\]
symmetrised under the crossing shift $\kappa_{\osp} = (m - n - 2)\hbar/2$,
which at $(m, n) = (4, 20)$ gives $\kappa_{\osp} = -9\hbar$. In the
Kac-$\osp$ warm-up this would be the centre generator of $Y(\osp(4 \mid 20))$ and replaces the
$\fgl$-Berezinian as the super-analogue of the quantum
determinant.

\paragraph{\texorpdfstring{$A_1$}{A-1} enhancement: \texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak{sl}_2$}{sl-2} inside \texorpdfstring{$\osp(4 \mid 20)$}{osp(4 mid 20)}.}
At an $A_1$ enhancement point (nodal K3 with a single rational
$(-2)$-curve), the root vector sits in $V_- = \C^{20}$. The
$\mathfrak{sl}_2$ subalgebra is the one generated by an
$\mathfrak{sp}(2)$-triple inside $\mathfrak{sp}(20) \subset
\osp(4 \mid 20)_{\bar 0}$. The $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ RTT is therefore
the \emph{standard} (non-super) rational RTT. The super-structure
manifests in the mixing between the $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ root and
$V_+ = \C^4$: $8$~fermionic entries (half of the naive $\fgl$
count, via the orthosymplectic constraint) carry
anticommutation relations.

\paragraph{Crossing symmetry via \texorpdfstring{$\osp$}{osp} supertranspose.}
For $Y(\osp(m|n))$, the crossing relation is
\[
 R(u)^{\mathrm{st}_1}\, R(-u - (m-n-2)\hbar)^{\mathrm{st}_1}
 \;=\; f(u) \cdot \mathrm{Id},
\]
where $\mathrm{st}_1$ is the $\osp$-supertranspose combining
parity signs $(-1)^{(p(i)+p(j))p(j)}$ with the orthogonal-form
involution $i \leftrightarrow \bar i$. Verification at the
$\osp(1 \mid 2)$ and $\osp(2 \mid 2)$ levels (not implemented in
the existing \texttt{k3\_super\_yangian.py} module, which
handles $\fgl$) is a prerequisite for the $(4, 20)$-rank
conjecture.

\paragraph{ZTE obstruction persists.}
The Kac-$\osp$ warm-up super-Yang $R$-matrix does \emph{not} satisfy
the Zamolodchikov tetrahedron equation (ZTE), just as the
ordinary and $\fgl$-super Yang $R$-matrices fail ZTE
(Theorem~\ref{thm:zte-failure}). All three obstructions scale as
$O(\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^2)$ for $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \to 0$, with
different numerical prefactors. The super-structure modifies
but does not eliminate the ZTE obstruction: the resolution
requires genuinely 3D corrections beyond pairwise $R$-matrices,
regardless of whether one works in $\fgl$- or $\osp$-super-
Yangians. Numerical verification of the obstruction at
$\gl(2 \mid 1)$ (\texttt{k3\_super\_yangian.py}) illustrates the
scaling.

\begin{remark}[Summary: what the Kac-$\osp$ warm-up records and what it does not resolve]
\label{rem:k3-super-summary}
The Kac $\osp(4 \mid 20)$ warm-up computation (scope
Remark~\ref{rem:k3-kac-osp-warmup-scope}) records the following
structural features; the Mukai-preserving envelope is
$Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$ (ungraded) and its Hodge-parity
$\Z/2$-super-extension:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item \textbf{Resolves}: modified unitarity ($P_s^2 = \mathrm{Id}$),
 crossing symmetry (supertranspose), well-defined central element
 (quantum Berezinian).
\item \textbf{Does not resolve}: ZTE obstruction (persists at $O(\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^2)$,
 requires 3D corrections), CY-A$_3$ dependence (layers~(iii)--(iv)
 remain conditional).
\end{enumerate}
The Hodge-parity orthogonal comparison is constrained by the Mukai
signature: the Kac-$\osp(4\mid20)$ computation records a warm-up
block-count, but it is not the Mukai-preserving object. The actual
finite comparison algebra is the programme-specific theta-fixed
$Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$, symmetric on both Hodge-parity
pieces; the conjectural step is its compact Hall realisation. The
$\fgl(4 \mid 20)$ count of $(416,160)$, obtained by
ignoring the orthogonal constraint, is strictly larger and serves only
as the small-rank $\fgl$-analogue verified in
\texttt{k3\_super\_yangian.py}.
\end{remark}


\subsection{Explicit RTT presentation at rank \texorpdfstring{$(4, 20)$}{(4, 20)}: super-$R$-matrix, $\theta$-fixed Yangian, Yamane super-Serre}
\label{subsec:r10-explicit-rtt-4-20}

The trichotomy of Subsection~\ref{subsec:k3-super-yangian} names the
envelope~(II) $Y_\hbar(\fso(4 \mid 20))$ as the finite
programme-specific $\Z/2$-theta-fixed extension; the
Kulish--Sklyanin rank-$(1, 1)$ machinery of
Section~\ref{sec:stable-super-rtt-omega} verifies its super-YBE kernel.
The present subsection supplies the explicit construction between the two:
the super-$R$-matrix on $V = \C^{4 \mid 20}$, the rank-$(4, 20)$ RTT
relation, the Chevalley involution $\theta$, the $\theta$-fixed subalgebra
$Y_\theta \subset Y(\fgl(4 \mid 20))$ with Molev's twisted-Yangian
reflection-equation presentation, the Yamane super-Serre relations at the
distinguished Dynkin $(A_3 \mid \mathrm{odd\ isotropic} \mid A_{19})$, and
the Drinfeld-new Gauss decomposition. The classical limit of $Y_\theta$
is the current algebra of $\fso(4, 20)$, identifying this construction with
envelope~(I) of Theorem~\ref{conj:osp-yangian-mukai} at the Lie level.

\paragraph{Notational collision.}
The Gow 2007~\cite{Gow2007} and Molev 2007~\cite{Molev2007} label
$Y_{\fosp}(4 \mid 20)$ denotes the $\theta$-fixed twisted subalgebra
$Y(\fgl(4 \mid 20))^\theta$. This is \emph{not} the Drinfeld Yangian of
Kac's simple Lie superalgebra $\fosp(4 \mid 20)$ of Arnaudon--Cramp\'e--
Doikou--Frappat--Ragoucy~\cite{AcdfR2003}, whose defining form is
symplectic on the odd part. The two algebras are distinct, and the Mukai
pairing's symmetry on both Hodge-graded parts selects the $\theta$-fixed
construction below.

\paragraph{The super-$R$-matrix on $V = \C^{4 \mid 20}$.}
Fix $V = V_+ \oplus V_-$, $\dim V_+ = 4$, $\dim V_- = 20$, with ordered
basis $\{e_1, \ldots, e_{24}\}$ and parity $|e_i| = \bar 0$ for $i \leq 4$,
$|e_i| = \bar 1$ for $i \geq 5$. The Mukai symmetric form takes diagonal
shape $\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}} = \mathrm{diag}(I_4, -I_{20})$. The
\emph{graded permutation} $P_s \in \End(V \otimes V)$ acts by
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:r10-graded-permutation-ch}
 P_s(e_i \otimes e_j)
 \;=\;
 (-1)^{|e_i|\,|e_j|}\, e_j \otimes e_i.
\end{equation}
The \emph{Mukai trace projector} $Q \in \End(V \otimes V)$ acts by
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:r10-Q-projector-ch}
 Q(e_i \otimes e_j)
 \;=\;
 (\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}})_{ij}\, \sum_k (-1)^{|e_k|}
 (\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}})^{-1}_{kk}\, e_k \otimes e_k.
\end{equation}

\begin{proposition}[Operator algebra of $(P_s, Q)$ at rank $(4, 20)$]
\label{prop:r10-Ps-Q-algebra-ch}
The operators $P_s, Q$ satisfy
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\arabic*)}]
\item $P_s^2 = \Id$;
\item $P_s Q = Q P_s = +Q$ on $V_+ \otimes V_+$ and on
 $V_- \otimes V_-$, and $Q \equiv 0$ on the mixed-parity blocks
 $V_+ \otimes V_-$, $V_- \otimes V_+$;
\item $Q^2 = \str(\Id_V)\, Q = -16\, Q$ as a scalar multiple on the
 supports of $Q$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(1) is immediate from
$P_s^2(e_i \otimes e_j) = (-1)^{|e_i|\,|e_j|} (-1)^{|e_j|\,|e_i|}\,
e_i \otimes e_j = e_i \otimes e_j$.
(2) follows from diagonality of $\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}$: the
contraction $(\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}})_{ij} = 0$ unless $i = j$ (in
particular $|e_i| = |e_j|$), so $Q$ is supported on same-parity
tensor products, where $P_s$ acts as $+\Id$.
(3) follows from
$Q(e_k \otimes e_k) = (\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}})_{kk}\,
(\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}})^{-1}_{kk} \cdot \sum_\ell (-1)^{|e_\ell|}
(\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}})^{-1}_{\ell\ell}\, e_\ell \otimes e_\ell$;
summing over $k$ weighted by $(-1)^{|e_k|} (\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}})_{kk}$
produces $\str(\Id_V) = 4 - 20 = -16$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Rank-$(4, 20)$ Mukai super-$R$-matrix]
\label{def:r10-R-matrix-ch}
Set the \emph{Mukai crossing shift}
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:r10-kappa-Muk-ch}
 \kappa_{\mathrm{Muk}}
 \;=\;
 \str(\Id_V) - 2
 \;=\;
 -18.
\end{equation}
The \emph{rank-$(4, 20)$ Mukai super-$R$-matrix} is
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:r10-R-matrix-ch}
 R^{(4 \mid 20)}(u)
 \;=\;
 \Id
 \;+\; \frac{\hbar}{u}\, P_s
 \;-\; \frac{\hbar}{u + \hbar\, \kappa_{\mathrm{Muk}}/2}\, Q
 \;\in\; \End(V \otimes V)(u)[[\hbar]].
\end{equation}
As a $576 \times 576$ matrix with entries in $\Q(u, \hbar)$, it is a
rational function of $u$ with simple poles at $u = 0$ and $u = -9\hbar$;
its rational evaluation at the Mukai-Heegner wall (the limit
$u \to -9\hbar$) produces the trace-projector residue $-\hbar Q$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Super Yang--Baxter equation at rank $(4, 20)$]
\label{thm:r10-super-YBE-ch}
Assume either an exact Molev--Ragoucy theorem matching the Mukai
supertrace shift and parity convention, or a symbolic rank-$(4,20)$
verification of the displayed $R$-matrix.
The rank-$(4, 20)$ Mukai super-$R$-matrix~\eqref{eq:r10-R-matrix-ch}
satisfies the super-YBE on $V^{\otimes 3}$:
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:r10-super-YBE-ch}
 R^{(4 \mid 20)}_{12}(u - v)\,
 R^{(4 \mid 20)}_{13}(u)\,
 R^{(4 \mid 20)}_{23}(v)
 \;=\;
 R^{(4 \mid 20)}_{23}(v)\,
 R^{(4 \mid 20)}_{13}(u)\,
 R^{(4 \mid 20)}_{12}(u - v),
\end{equation}
as a polynomial identity of total $\hbar$-degree $3$ with
rational-function coefficients in $(u, v)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The triple product in~\eqref{eq:r10-super-YBE-ch} is polynomial of
degree $\leq 3$ in $\hbar$. Collecting coefficients of
$\hbar^0, \hbar^1, \hbar^2, \hbar^3$ and using
Proposition~\ref{prop:r10-Ps-Q-algebra-ch} plus the three
trace-projector braid identities
\begin{align}
 \label{eq:r10-PQ-braid-1}
 P_s^{(ij)}\, Q^{(jk)} \;&=\; Q^{(ik)}\, P_s^{(jk)}, \\
 \label{eq:r10-PQ-braid-2}
 Q^{(ij)}\, P_s^{(jk)} \;&=\; P_s^{(ik)}\, Q^{(jk)}, \\
 \label{eq:r10-PQ-braid-3}
 Q^{(ij)}\, Q^{(jk)} \;&=\; \str(\Id_V)\, Q^{(jk)}
 \;=\; -16\, Q^{(jk)},
\end{align}
each order collapses to a scalar identity in rational functions of
$(u, v)$. At $\hbar^0$ both sides equal $\Id$; at $\hbar^1$ both
sides equal $P_s^{(12)}/(u-v) + P_s^{(13)}/u + P_s^{(23)}/v
- Q^{(12)}/(u-v) - Q^{(13)}/u - Q^{(23)}/v$ up to the Mukai crossing
shift (the $Q$-denominators become $u - v - 9\hbar, u - 9\hbar,
v - 9\hbar$ after the crossing correction, and the $\hbar^1$
coefficients reduce to the stated common sum). At $\hbar^2$ the
cross-products $P_s^{(ij)} P_s^{(k\ell)}$, $P_s^{(ij)} Q^{(k\ell)}$,
$Q^{(ij)} Q^{(k\ell)}$ organise into three pieces that cancel via
the super-braid identities (Theorem~\ref{thm:super-ybe-ks}'s
argument applied to $P_s$) and the trace-projector
identities~\eqref{eq:r10-PQ-braid-1}--\eqref{eq:r10-PQ-braid-3}. At
$\hbar^3$ the single $(P_s)^3$ term equals
$P_s^{(12)} P_s^{(13)} P_s^{(23)}/((u-v) u v)$ on the LHS and
$P_s^{(23)} P_s^{(13)} P_s^{(12)}/(v u (u-v))$ on the RHS, equal by
the super-braid relation. The $Q$-deformed $\hbar^3$ terms collapse
by the triple trace-projector identity combined with partial
fractions.

The identity is the rank-$24$ specialisation of the Molev--Ragoucy
extended super-$\fso$ YBE~\cite{MolevRagoucy2008} with parity signs
of~\eqref{eq:r10-graded-permutation-ch}. The remaining direct proof is
the symbolic verification on the $24^3 = 13{,}824$ basis triples in
\texttt{compute/lib/k3\_super\_yangian\_rank\_4\_20.py}.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{The rank-$(4, 20)$ Lax series and RTT relation.}
The \emph{Lax series} of the rank-$(4, 20)$ super-Yangian is
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:r10-lax-series-ch}
 T(u)
 \;=\;
 \Id \;+\; \sum_{r \geq 1} t^{(r)}\, u^{-r},
 \qquad
 t^{(r)} \;=\; \sum_{i, j = 1}^{24} t^{(r)}_{ij}\, e_{ij}
 \;\in\; \End(V) \otimes Y^{(4 \mid 20)}_\hbar,
\end{equation}
with parity $|t^{(r)}_{ij}| = |e_i| + |e_j| \pmod 2$; the generators
$t^{(r)}_{ij}$ are fermionic when $|e_i| \neq |e_j|$ (off-diagonal
mixed-parity) and bosonic otherwise.

\begin{definition}[Rank-$(4, 20)$ RTT relation]
\label{def:r10-RTT-ch}
The \emph{rank-$(4, 20)$ RTT relation} in $\End(V)^{\otimes 2}
\otimes Y^{(4 \mid 20)}_\hbar[[u^{-1}, v^{-1}]]$ is
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:r10-RTT-ch}
 R^{(4 \mid 20)}(u - v)\, T_1(u)\, T_2(v)
 \;=\;
 T_2(v)\, T_1(u)\, R^{(4 \mid 20)}(u - v).
\end{equation}
Written entry-by-entry,
\begin{multline}
 \label{eq:r10-RTT-components-ch}
 [t_{ij}(u),\, t_{k\ell}(v)]_s
 \;=\;
 \frac{\hbar}{u - v}\,
 \Bigl(
 (-1)^{|e_i|(|e_k| + |e_\ell|) + |e_k||e_\ell|}\,
 t_{kj}(u)\, t_{i\ell}(v)
 \\[-0.5ex]
 {} -
 (-1)^{|e_\ell|(|e_i| + |e_j|) + |e_i||e_j|}\,
 t_{i\ell}(v)\, t_{kj}(u)
 \Bigr)
 \;-\;
 \frac{\hbar\, Q^{ab}_{ij}}{u - v + 9\hbar}\,
 \sum_{a, b} t_{a\ell}(u)\, t_{bk}(v),
\end{multline}
where $[\cdot, \cdot]_s$ is the graded commutator
$[x, y]_s = xy - (-1)^{|x||y|} yx$, and the $Q$-contribution is
supported on indices satisfying the Mukai diagonality
$(\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}})_{ij}(\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}})_{k\ell} \neq 0$.
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Block structure of the RTT relation]
\label{rem:r10-RTT-blocks-ch}
The RTT relation~\eqref{eq:r10-RTT-ch} decomposes along parity blocks
of $V \otimes V$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
\item $V_+ \otimes V_+$: the relation reduces to the $\fso(4)$
 Yangian RTT with crossing shift $\kappa_{\fso(4)} = 2$; the bosonic
 generators $t^{(r)}_{ij}$, $i, j \leq 4$, present the
 $Y_\hbar(\fso(4))$-block.
\item $V_- \otimes V_-$: the relation reduces to the $\fso(20)$
 Yangian RTT with a super-sign correction; the bosonic generators
 $t^{(r)}_{ij}$, $i, j \geq 5$, present the $Y_\hbar(\fso(20))$-block
 with negative-definite Mukai pairing.
\item $V_+ \otimes V_-$, $V_- \otimes V_+$: the Mukai trace projector
 $Q$ vanishes on mixed-parity blocks; the relation reduces to the
 pure $\fgl$-super RTT of Nazarov~\cite{Nazarov1991} with graded
 permutation only.
\end{enumerate}
The three-block decomposition realises the symmetric-pair
decomposition $\fso(4, 20) \supset \fso(4) \oplus \fso(20)$ at the
Yangian level.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{Chevalley involution and the $\theta$-fixed subalgebra.}
Define the super-transpose
$(e_{ij})^t = (-1)^{|e_i|\,|e_j| + |e_j|}\, e_{ji}$ on $\End(V)$.

\begin{definition}[Chevalley involution at rank $(4, 20)$]
\label{def:r10-theta-ch}
The \emph{Chevalley involution} $\theta$ on the ambient super-Yangian
$Y(\fgl(4 \mid 20))$ is the algebra automorphism determined on the
Lax series by
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:r10-theta-Lax-ch}
 \theta(T(u)) \;:=\; T(-u)^{t}.
\end{equation}
Component-wise,
$\theta(t^{(r)}_{ij}) = (-1)^r (-1)^{|e_i||e_j| + |e_j|}\, t^{(r)}_{ji}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[$\theta$-symmetry of the super-$R$-matrix]
\label{prop:r10-theta-R-symm-ch}
The rank-$(4, 20)$ super-$R$-matrix satisfies
$R^{(4 \mid 20)}(u)^{t_1 t_2} = R^{(4 \mid 20)}(u)$.
Hence the Chevalley involution preserves the RTT relation: applying
$\theta$ to~\eqref{eq:r10-RTT-ch} yields the same identity after the
substitution $(u, v) \mapsto (-u, -v)$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Direct computation on~\eqref{eq:r10-R-matrix-ch}:
$P_s^{t_1 t_2} = P_s$ because the graded permutation's
super-transpose in both factors recovers itself, and
$Q^{t_1 t_2} = Q$ because trace projectors are super-transpose
invariant. The RTT-compatibility is then the standard argument:
apply $\theta$ to both sides of~\eqref{eq:r10-RTT-ch}, use the
$\theta$-symmetry of $R^{(4 \mid 20)}$, and relabel the spectral
parameters.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Twisted Yangian $Y_\theta^{(4 \mid 20)}$]
\label{def:r10-Y-theta-ch}
The \emph{twisted Yangian at rank $(4, 20)$} is
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:r10-Y-theta-ch}
 Y_\theta^{(4 \mid 20)}
 \;:=\;
 Y(\fgl(4 \mid 20))^\theta
 \;=\;
 \bigl\{\, x \in Y(\fgl(4 \mid 20)) \,:\, \theta(x) = x\, \bigr\}.
\end{equation}
Equivalently, it is generated by the $\theta$-symmetric combinations
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:r10-s-generators-ch}
 s^{(r)}_{ij}
 \;:=\;
 \tfrac{1}{2}
 \bigl(\, t^{(r)}_{ij}
 \;+\; (-1)^r (-1)^{|e_i||e_j| + |e_j|}\, t^{(r)}_{ji} \,\bigr).
\end{equation}
The Lax series $S(u) = \tfrac{1}{2}(T(u) + T(-u)^t)$ satisfies the
Sklyanin reflection equation
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:r10-reflection-eq-ch}
 R^{(4 \mid 20)}(u - v)\, S_1(u)\, R^{(4 \mid 20)}(-u - v)\,
 S_2(v)
 \;=\;
 S_2(v)\, R^{(4 \mid 20)}(-u - v)\, S_1(u)\, R^{(4 \mid 20)}(u - v).
\end{equation}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Classical limit of $Y_\theta^{(4 \mid 20)}$]
\label{thm:r10-classical-limit-ch}
The $\hbar \to 0$ classical limit of $Y_\theta^{(4 \mid 20)}$ is
$U(\fso(4, 20)[u])$, the universal enveloping algebra of the
current algebra of $\fso(4, 20)$. In particular, the classical limit
preserves the Mukai symmetric form of signature $(4, 20)$ and does
not carry Kac's $\fosp(4 \mid 20)$-structure (whose defining form is
symplectic on the odd part).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The $\theta$-symmetric combinations $s^{(r)}_{ij}$ span, at $r = 1$
and $\hbar = 0$, the classical $\theta$-fixed subalgebra of
$\fgl(4 \mid 20)$ inside $U(\fgl(4 \mid 20)[u])$. The super-transpose
$\theta_0 = \theta|_{\hbar = 0}$ coincides, on the bosonic-bosonic
$V_+ \otimes V_+$ and fermionic-fermionic $V_- \otimes V_-$ blocks,
with the ordinary transpose weighted by the Mukai pairing; on these
blocks, the $\theta_0$-fixed subspaces are $\fso(V_+) = \fso(4)$ and
$\fso(V_-) = \fso(20)$ (preserving the symmetric forms). On the
mixed-parity blocks $V_+ \otimes V_-$, $V_- \otimes V_+$, $\theta_0$
acts as $(a, b) \mapsto (-b^T, -a^T)$ in the matrix-pair encoding;
the $\theta_0$-symmetric subspace is an $80$-dimensional module under
the diagonal $\fso(4) \oplus \fso(20)$. Total classical $\theta_0$-fixed
Lie-algebra dimension $6 + 190 + 80 = 276 = \binom{24}{2} =
\dim \fso(24)$; the signature is $(4, 20)$ after the Mukai-diagonal
form. Hence the classical limit is $\fso(4, 20)[u]$, identifying
$Y_\theta^{(4 \mid 20)}|_{\hbar = 0} = U(\fso(4, 20)[u])$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[$Y_\theta^{(4 \mid 20)}$ as envelope~(II) of Theorem~\ref{conj:osp-yangian-mukai}]
\label{rem:r10-Y-theta-is-envelope-II-ch}
Theorem~\ref{thm:r10-classical-limit-ch} identifies the $\theta$-fixed
construction with envelope~(II) at the Lie level: both have classical
limit $\fso(4, 20)$, both carry the symmetric Mukai pairing on the
defining representation, both exclude Kac's $\fosp(4 \mid 20)$. The
$\theta$-fixed construction furnishes the explicit envelope~(II):
the Gow/Molev twisted-Yangian route is the concrete presentation of
$Y_\hbar(\fso(4 \mid 20))$ consistent with the Hodge-parity
$\Z/2$-super-extension. The label $Y_{\fosp}(4|20)$ in the Gow 2007
/ Iohara--Koga 2001 / Molev 2007 literature is a notational collision
with Kac's orthosymplectic Yangian of ACDFR 2003; the two algebras
are distinct. The Mukai pairing's symmetry on both Hodge-graded parts
selects the $\theta$-fixed route.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{Super-Serre relations at the distinguished Dynkin.}
The ambient $\fgl(4 \mid 20)$ carries the distinguished Dynkin
diagram
\begin{center}
 $\underbrace{\bullet - \bullet - \bullet}_{A_3\ \mathrm{bosonic}} -
 \underbrace{\otimes}_{\alpha_4\ \mathrm{odd\ isotropic}} -
 \underbrace{\bullet - \bullet - \cdots - \bullet}_{A_{19}\ \mathrm{bosonic}}$
\end{center}
with $23$ simple roots, of which $22$ are bosonic (positions
$1, 2, 3$ on the $A_3$ side and $5, 6, \ldots, 23$ on the $A_{19}$
side) and $1$ is odd isotropic ($\alpha_4$).

\begin{theorem}[Yamane super-Serre relations at rank $(4, 20)$]
\label{thm:r10-super-Serre-ch}
At the distinguished Dynkin of the ambient $Y(\fgl(4 \mid 20))$, the
Chevalley generators $\{e_i, f_i, h_i\}_{i = 1, \ldots, 23}$ satisfy
three families of super-Serre relations:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(S\arabic*)}]
\item \emph{Bosonic Serre} at non-isotropic simple roots
 $\alpha_i, \alpha_j$, $i, j \neq 4$:
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:r10-bosonic-Serre-ch}
 (\ad e_i)^{1 - a_{ij}}(e_j) \;=\; 0,
 \qquad
 (\ad f_i)^{1 - a_{ij}}(f_j) \;=\; 0.
\end{equation}
\item \emph{Isotropic Serre} at the odd isotropic simple root
 $\alpha_4$:
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:r10-isotropic-Serre-ch}
 e_4^2 \;=\; 0, \qquad f_4^2 \;=\; 0.
\end{equation}
\item \emph{Mixed cubic Serre} at the $\alpha_3 - \alpha_4$ and
 $\alpha_4 - \alpha_5$ boundaries:
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:r10-mixed-Serre-ch}
 [e_4, [e_3, [e_4, e_5]]] \;=\; 0,
 \qquad
 [f_4, [f_3, [f_4, f_5]]] \;=\; 0.
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
The total count of super-Serre relations per sign is
$22 + 1 + 2 = 25$ (bosonic $+$ isotropic $+$ mixed cubic). The
restriction to $Y_\theta^{(4 \mid 20)}$ descends on the
$\theta$-symmetric generators $s^{(r)}_{ij}$ and preserves the
count.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The super-Serre presentation of $Y(\fgl(m \mid n))$ at the
distinguished Dynkin is the Yamane~\cite{Yamane1999}
Theorem~1 result, itself deriving from
