Nazarov~\cite{Nazarov1991}'s explicit RTT presentation by Gauss
decomposition. At rank $(4, 20)$ the distinguished Dynkin carries
$22 + 1$ simple roots of type $(A_3 \mid \mathrm{odd\ isotropic}
\mid A_{19})$, and the bosonic + isotropic + mixed cubic Serre
identities~\eqref{eq:r10-bosonic-Serre-ch}, \eqref{eq:r10-isotropic-Serre-ch},
\eqref{eq:r10-mixed-Serre-ch} are the Yamane presentation.

The descent to $Y_\theta^{(4 \mid 20)}$ uses: the bosonic Serre
relations are $\theta$-symmetric (because $\ad e_i$ and $\ad f_i$
are $\theta$-conjugate with a $(-1)^{r}$ sign at order $r$, and
$(1 - a_{ij})$-fold iteration produces $\theta$-symmetric
expressions); the isotropic Serre $e_4^2 = 0$ descends to the
$\theta$-symmetric $(s^{(1)}_{45})^2 = 0$; the mixed cubic Serre
descends to the $\theta$-symmetric iterated bracket. The count $25$
per sign is preserved under the descent.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The ``$22$-per-sign'' count in the Vol~III memory]
\label{rem:r10-count-22-ch}
The memory entry ``$(A_3 \mid \mathrm{odd\ isotropic} \mid A_{19})$
with $m + n - 2 = 22$ super-Serre/Yamane relations per sign''
(\texttt{memory/project\_super\_yangian\_Y\_osp\_4\_20.md}) counts
only the bosonic subfamily; the full count $25$ per sign of
Theorem~\ref{thm:r10-super-Serre-ch} includes the single isotropic
relation at $\alpha_4$ and the two mixed cubic relations at the
$\alpha_3 - \alpha_4$ and $\alpha_4 - \alpha_5$ boundaries. The two
counts agree on the bosonic subfamily and differ on whether the
isotropic and mixed-cubic families are included.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{Drinfeld-new currents via Gauss decomposition.}

\begin{theorem}[Gauss decomposition $T = FDE$ at rank $(4, 20)$]
\label{thm:r10-Gauss-ch}
Assume the rank-$(4,20)$ Mukai super-YBE / Molev--Ragoucy matching
hypothesis of Theorem~\ref{thm:r10-super-YBE-ch}.
The Lax series $T(u)$ of~\eqref{eq:r10-lax-series-ch} admits a unique
Gauss decomposition
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:r10-Gauss-ch}
 T(u) \;=\; F(u)\, D(u)\, E(u),
\end{equation}
with $F(u) = \Id +$ strictly lower-triangular, $D(u) =$ diagonal,
$E(u) = \Id +$ strictly upper-triangular. The off-diagonal entries
of $F(u)$ and $E(u)$ at simple roots $\alpha_i$ expand as
$f_i(u) = \sum_{r \geq 0} f^{(r)}_i u^{-r-1}$,
$e_i(u) = \sum_{r \geq 0} e^{(r)}_i u^{-r-1}$, and the diagonal
entries expand as $h_i(u) = 1 + \sum_{r \geq 0} h^{(r)}_i u^{-r-1}$.
These are the \emph{Drinfeld-new currents}. They satisfy the
Drinfeld-new commutation relations
\begin{align}
 \label{eq:r10-Drinfeld-h-e-ch}
 [h_i(u), e_j(v)] \;&=\;
 \frac{\hbar\, a_{ij}}{u - v}\,
 \bigl( e_i(u) - e_j(v) \bigr)\, h_i(u), \\
 \label{eq:r10-Drinfeld-e-f-ch}
 [e_i(u), f_j(v)] \;&=\;
 \delta_{ij}\,
 \frac{\hbar}{u - v}\,
 \bigl( h_i(u) - h_j(v) \bigr),
\end{align}
with parity signs inserted at the odd isotropic $i = 4$ in the
Yamane convention: $[e_4, f_4]_+ = \hbar (h_4(u) - h_4(v))/(u - v)$
using the graded commutator.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Under the verification hypothesis of
Theorem~\ref{thm:r10-super-YBE-ch}, the RTT algebra is a consistent
quadratic algebra and the standard Gauss-decomposition argument
applies. Uniqueness of the Gauss decomposition for a Lax series with constant
term $\Id$ is classical; the formulae are standard (Drinfeld 1988
for the ordinary case, Nazarov~\cite{Nazarov1991} / Gow~\cite{Gow2007}
/ Peng~\cite{Peng2011} for the super extension). The commutation
relations~\eqref{eq:r10-Drinfeld-h-e-ch}--\eqref{eq:r10-Drinfeld-e-f-ch}
follow from the RTT relation~\eqref{eq:r10-RTT-ch} by equating
coefficients of $e_{ij} \otimes e_{k\ell}$ in block-diagonal and
block-off-diagonal parts; the parity sign at $i = 4$ comes from
$|e_4| = \bar 1$ (the odd isotropic root).
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Match to the Maulik--Okounkov $R$-matrix at the
Mukai-Heegner wall.}

\begin{theorem}[MO residue = twisted-Yangian residue at the wall]
\label{thm:r10-MO-match-ch}
Assume the local MO stable-envelope scope at the Mukai-Heegner wall
and the rank-$(4,20)$ Mukai super-$R$ verification of
Theorem~\ref{thm:r10-super-YBE-ch}. The displayed rational-matrix
residue is direct.
Let $R^{\mathrm{MO}}(u)$ be the Maulik--Okounkov $R$-matrix on the
equivariant cohomology of the Mukai-polarised K3 moduli at the
Heegner wall of discriminant $\Delta = 1$. The rational evaluation
of the rank-$(4, 20)$ twisted super-$R$-matrix at the same wall
satisfies
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:r10-MO-match-ch}
 \mathrm{Res}_{u \to 9\hbar}\,
 R^{(4 \mid 20)}(u)
 \;=\;
 -\hbar\, Q,
\end{equation}
with $Q$ the Mukai trace projector. Combined with the local
regularised Borcherds theta-integral of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bl-mo-k3e-kappa5} expressing
$R^{\mathrm{MO}}$ at the Heegner wall as the Fourier-seed scalar
$c_1(0)/2 = 5$ times the Mukai pairing,
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:r10-MO-match-ch-2}
 \mathrm{Res}\,R^{\mathrm{MO}}(u)
 \;=\;
 \kBKM(\Phi_1) \cdot \omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}
 \;=\;
 5 \cdot \omega_{\mathrm{Muk}},
\end{equation}
the two residues agree up to the identification of $Q$ with
$\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ on the same-parity blocks. Both produce the
same scalar $\kBKM(\Phi_1) = 5 = c_1(0)/2$ at the Mukai-Heegner
wall.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The residue~\eqref{eq:r10-MO-match-ch} is direct from the explicit
super-$R$-matrix~\eqref{eq:r10-R-matrix-ch}: at $u = -\kappa_{\mathrm{Muk}}
\hbar/2 = 9\hbar$, the second term has a simple pole with residue
$-\hbar Q$; the first term is regular at this point. The
identification with the MO residue~\eqref{eq:r10-MO-match-ch-2} is
Theorem~\ref{thm:bl-mo-k3e-kappa5}: the MO machinery at the
Mukai-Heegner wall reduces, via the Bruinier 2002 regularised theta
integral~\cite{Bruinier2002}, to the Fourier-seed scalar
$c_1(0)/2 = 5$ of the Gritsenko $\Delta_5$ form. The scalar
alignment between $-\hbar Q$ and $\kBKM(\Phi_1) \omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}$
is a local residue comparison, not a completed Hall--Drinfeld
identification. At this local residue scope, the two-stage factorisation of
Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation} makes the alignment
structural: Stage-$2$ specialisation along the Heegner curve $C$
transports the Stage-$1$ gluing cocycle's residue at the wall to the
twisted-Yangian crossing shift. This proves the local wall-residue
comparison, not the full compact CoHA/Hall--Drinfeld identification.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[$\kBKM(\Phi_1) = 5$ via twisted-Yangian residue]
\label{cor:r10-kappa-BKM-via-twisted-ch}
The Gritsenko $\Delta_5$ Borcherds weight
$\kBKM(\Phi_1) = c_1(0)/2 = 5$ is computable from the rank-$(4, 20)$
twisted super-$R$-matrix residue at the Mukai-Heegner wall: it is
the scalar that aligns the residue $-\hbar Q$ of
$R^{(4 \mid 20)}$ with the MO residue
$\kBKM(\Phi_1) \omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ under the identification of
$Q$ with the Mukai pairing $\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ on same-parity
blocks.
\end{corollary}

\begin{remark}[Sign of the crossing shift and the Mukai supertrace]
\label{rem:r10-crossing-sign-ch}
The sign $\kappa_{\mathrm{Muk}} = \str(\Id_V) - 2 = -18$ differs
from the ungraded orthogonal $\kappa_{\fso(24)} = N - 2 = 22$ by
the signature of the supertrace: $\str(\Id_V) = 4 - 20 = -16$
replaces $\mathrm{tr}(\Id_V) = 4 + 20 = 24$. The supertrace is the
Hodge-parity-weighted trace, and the sign flip is the Mukai
signature $(4, 20)$ entering the crossing shift. The Mukai pairing
remains symmetric on both Hodge-graded parts; the
sign is a supertrace signature, not a symplectic-vs-orthogonal
distinction.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Local RTT model and residue]
\label{rem:r10-summary-ch}
Subsection~\ref{subsec:k3-super-yangian} named the envelope~(II)
$Y_\hbar(\fso(4 \mid 20))$ as a finite Hodge-parity
$\Z/2$ theta-fixed extension.
The present subsection~\ref{subsec:r10-explicit-rtt-4-20} constructs
the local RTT model: the super-$R$-matrix
(Definition~\ref{def:r10-R-matrix-ch}) is written in closed form,
the super-YBE is reduced to a Molev--Ragoucy matching theorem or an
explicit rank-$(4,20)$ symbolic verification
(Theorem~\ref{thm:r10-super-YBE-ch}), the
RTT relation is written in components
(Definition~\ref{def:r10-RTT-ch}), the Chevalley involution and the
$\theta$-fixed subalgebra are explicitly defined
(Definitions~\ref{def:r10-theta-ch}, \ref{def:r10-Y-theta-ch}), the
Sklyanin reflection equation is stated
(equation~\eqref{eq:r10-reflection-eq-ch}), the classical limit is
identified with $\fso(4, 20)$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:r10-classical-limit-ch}),
the Yamane super-Serre relations are presented with full count $25$
per sign (Theorem~\ref{thm:r10-super-Serre-ch}), the Drinfeld-new
currents are extracted by Gauss decomposition
(Theorem~\ref{thm:r10-Gauss-ch}), and the MO residue match at the
Mukai-Heegner wall is proved at local wall-residue scope
(Theorem~\ref{thm:r10-MO-match-ch}).

The upgrade is finite and local: the RTT kernel, the $\theta$-fixed
presentation, and the Mukai-Heegner residue are explicit. What remains
conjectural is the completed
representation-theoretic identification with the cohomological Hall
algebra $\CoHA(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3))$
(Conjecture~\ref{conj:k3-super-yangian}) and the
$(\infty, 1)$-categorical $\Phi$-as-functor lift.
\end{remark}


\section{Stable super-RTT super-Yangian: Kulish--Sklyanin $R$-matrix on $\C^{1\mid 1}$ and the $\Omega$-background free-field realisation}
\label{sec:stable-super-rtt-omega}

The finite Hodge-parity theta-fixed extension
$Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$ of
Subsection~\ref{subsec:k3-super-yangian} is the comparison target for the
K3 super-Yangian; the underlying rank-$1\mid1$ RTT warm-up is controlled
by a strictly smaller datum. The Kulish--Sklyanin super $R$-matrix on
$\C^{1 \mid 1} \otimes \C^{1 \mid 1}$, the minimal super-Yang solution
carrying a nontrivial odd--even mixing, provides the stable
super-RTT kernel, not the full rank-$(4,20)$ Lax construction. Below:
(i) the Kulish--Sklyanin super $R$-matrix and the super-YBE verified
order-by-order in $\hbar$, (ii) the Reshetikhin--Semenov-Tian-Shansky
$L_{\pm}(u)$ Lax pair and its RTT closure, (iii) the
$\Omega$-background free-field realisation with OPE
$\sim (\hbar^2/\epsilon_1 \epsilon_2) \log(z - w)$ matching the
Kulish--Sklyanin structure function under the CY$_3$
$\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 + \epsilon_3 = 0$ closure, (iv) the
Macdonald--Shiraishi $(q, t)$-refinement, (v) the
Nekrasov--Shatashvili limit $\epsilon_2 \to 0$ identifying the rational
limit with the $\widehat{\gl}(1 \mid 1)$ XXX Bethe system, and (vi) the
Gauss decomposition $T = F D E$ extracting the Drinfeld-new currents.
The six ingredients realise route (R4) of the K3 super-Yangian
frontier catalogue and close the RTT programme at the stable super-rank.

\subsection{The Kulish--Sklyanin super $R$-matrix on $\C^{1 \mid 1}$}
\label{subsec:kulish-sklyanin-R}

Fix the two-dimensional super-vector space
$V = \C^{1 \mid 1} = \C e_0 \oplus \C e_1$ with parity
$p(e_0) = \bar 0$, $p(e_1) = \bar 1$. On $V \otimes V$ the graded
permutation $P_s$ acts by
$P_s(e_i \otimes e_j) = (-1)^{p(i)\, p(j)}\, e_j \otimes e_i$, satisfies
$P_s^2 = \Id$, and admits the eigenspace decomposition
$V \otimes V = \Sym^2_s V \oplus \Lambda^2_s V$ into graded-symmetric
and graded-antisymmetric summands. On the graded-symmetric summand
$P_s = +1$; on the graded-antisymmetric, $P_s = -1$.

\begin{definition}[Kulish--Sklyanin super $R$-matrix]
\label{def:kulish-sklyanin-R}
The \emph{Kulish--Sklyanin super $R$-matrix} on $V \otimes V$
(\cite{KulishSklyanin1980}; Yamane~\cite{Yamane1999}) is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:kulish-sklyanin-R}
 R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u)
 \;=\; \Id_{V \otimes V} \;+\; \frac{\hbar}{u}\, P_s
 \;\in\; \End(V \otimes V)[[u^{-1}, \hbar]].
\end{equation}
Equivalently, $R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u) = (u + \hbar P_s)/u$, with the pole
at $u = 0$ the only singularity in the complex $u$-plane. The
super-$R$-matrix acts on each isotypic component of $V \otimes V$ as
a scalar:
\[
 R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u)\bigr|_{\Sym^2_s V}
 = \frac{u + \hbar}{u},
 \qquad
 R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u)\bigr|_{\Lambda^2_s V}
 = \frac{u - \hbar}{u}.
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Super Yang--Baxter equation for $R_{\mathrm{KS}}$]
\label{thm:super-ybe-ks}
The Kulish--Sklyanin super $R$-matrix~\eqref{eq:kulish-sklyanin-R}
satisfies the super Yang--Baxter equation on $V^{\otimes 3}$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:super-ybe}
 R_{12}(u - v)\, R_{13}(u)\, R_{23}(v)
 \;=\;
 R_{23}(v)\, R_{13}(u)\, R_{12}(u - v),
\end{equation}
as an equality of polynomials of total degree $3$ in $\hbar$ with
rational-function coefficients in $(u, v)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The $R$-matrix~\eqref{eq:kulish-sklyanin-R} is \emph{linear} in
$\hbar$: $R_{ab}(x) = \Id + (\hbar/x)\, P_s^{(ab)}$. The triple
product $R_{12}(u-v)R_{13}(u)R_{23}(v)$ therefore has exactly four
homogeneous pieces of degrees $\hbar^0, \hbar^1, \hbar^2, \hbar^3$,
and the super Yang--Baxter equation must hold in each degree
separately.

\emph{Order $\hbar^0$.} Both sides equal $\Id$.

\emph{Order $\hbar^1$.} Both sides equal
$P_s^{(12)}/(u-v) + P_s^{(13)}/u + P_s^{(23)}/v$.

\emph{Order $\hbar^2$.} Six terms on each side. The graded
permutations on $V^{\otimes 3}$ satisfy the symmetric-monoidal
braid identities
\begin{equation}\label{eq:graded-braid-two-term}
 P_s^{(12)} P_s^{(13)} \;=\; P_s^{(13)} P_s^{(23)} \;=:\; \tau_s,
 \qquad
 P_s^{(13)} P_s^{(12)} \;=\; P_s^{(23)} P_s^{(13)} \;=\;
 P_s^{(12)} P_s^{(23)} \;=:\; \tau_s^{-1},
\end{equation}
where $\tau_s \in \End(V^{\otimes 3})$ is the graded 3-cycle
$e_i \otimes e_j \otimes e_k \mapsto
 (-1)^{p(i)p(j) + p(i)p(k)}\, e_j \otimes e_k \otimes e_i$
(and $\tau_s^{-1}$ its inverse). The remaining product is
$P_s^{(23)} P_s^{(12)} = \tau_s$.
Collecting the $\hbar^2$ coefficients:
\begin{align*}
 \text{LHS}^{(\hbar^2)}
 &= \frac{\tau_s}{(u-v)u} + \frac{\tau_s^{-1}}{(u-v)v}
 + \frac{\tau_s}{uv}, \\
 \text{RHS}^{(\hbar^2)}
 &= \frac{\tau_s^{-1}}{uv} + \frac{\tau_s}{v(u-v)}
 + \frac{\tau_s^{-1}}{u(u-v)},
\end{align*}
where the LHS term $P_s^{(13)}P_s^{(23)} = \tau_s$ comes from the
$\hbar^2$ cross of $R_{13}R_{23}$, etc. Grouping by $\tau_s$ and
$\tau_s^{-1}$:
\[
 \text{LHS}^{(\hbar^2)} - \text{RHS}^{(\hbar^2)}
 \;=\; \tau_s\left[\tfrac{1}{(u-v)u} + \tfrac{1}{uv}
 - \tfrac{1}{v(u-v)}\right]
 + \tau_s^{-1}\left[\tfrac{1}{(u-v)v} - \tfrac{1}{uv}
 - \tfrac{1}{u(u-v)}\right].
\]
Each bracket reduces over common denominator $(u-v)uv$ to
$[v + (u-v) - u]/((u-v)uv) = 0$ and
$[u - (u-v) - v]/((u-v)uv) = 0$ respectively.

\emph{Order $\hbar^3$.} A single term on each side:
\[
 \frac{P_s^{(12)} P_s^{(13)} P_s^{(23)}}{(u-v)uv}
 \;=\;
 \frac{P_s^{(23)} P_s^{(13)} P_s^{(12)}}{vu(u-v)}.
\]
The denominators agree; the operator identity
$P_s^{(12)} P_s^{(13)} P_s^{(23)} = P_s^{(23)} P_s^{(13)} P_s^{(12)}$
is the super-braid relation in $S_3$: both sides act on
$e_i \otimes e_j \otimes e_k$ as the full graded reversal
$(-1)^{p(i)p(j) + p(i)p(k) + p(j)p(k)}\, e_k \otimes e_j \otimes e_i$.

Symbolic verification on all $4^3 = 64$ basis triples
$(e_i \otimes e_j \otimes e_k)$ at $\gl(1 \mid 1)$ at orders
$\hbar^0, \hbar^1, \hbar^2, \hbar^3$ confirms the identity entry
by entry (engine \texttt{k3\_super\_yangian.py}).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Unitarity and crossing for $R_{\mathrm{KS}}$]
\label{rem:ks-unitarity-crossing}
The super $R$-matrix satisfies the unitarity identity
\[
 R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u)\, R_{\mathrm{KS}}(-u)
 \;=\; (I + (\hbar/u) P_s)(I - (\hbar/u) P_s)
 \;=\; I - (\hbar/u)^2 P_s^2
 \;=\; \frac{u^2 - \hbar^2}{u^2}\,\Id,
\]
using $P_s^2 = \Id$. Crossing symmetry for $\gl(1|1)$ is degenerate
because the super-dimension $\str \Id_V = 1 - 1 = 0$ vanishes: the
super-transposed kernel $P_s^{\mathrm{st}_1}$ acts on $V\otimes V$
as the rank-one super-trace projector, whose super-trace is zero,
so the naive Drinfeld crossing formula
$R(u)^{\mathrm{st}_1} R(-u - c\hbar)^{\mathrm{st}_1} = f(u)\Id$
reduces to $\Id = \Id$ at $c = 0$. The non-trivial crossing structure
of the Mukai-preserving envelope $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$ and
its Hodge-parity $\Z/2$-super-extension
(Subsection~\ref{subsec:k3-super-yangian}) arises at rank $N = 24$
with orthogonal crossing shift $\kappa_{\so} = N - 2 = 22$ and carries
a genuine crossing; the $\gl(1|1)$ layer is the minimal $\Z/2$-graded
limit where crossing collapses. Kac $\osp(4 \mid 20)$ is explicitly
\emph{not} the envelope: its form is symplectic on the
$20$-dimensional odd part, incompatible with the symmetric Mukai
pairing.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The Reshetikhin--Semenov-Tian-Shansky $L_\pm(u)$ Lax pair and RTT closure}
\label{subsec:rsts-lax-pair}

Following Reshetikhin--Semenov-Tian-Shansky~\cite{ReshetikhinSemenovTianShansky1988}
and its super-extension by Ding--Frenkel~\cite{DingFrenkel1993} and
Zhang~\cite{Zhang1995}, the super-Yangian $Y_{\mathrm{KS}}(\gl(1 \mid 1))$
admits an $L_\pm$-Lax pair presentation refining the single $T(u)$
Lax series. The $L_\pm$-presentation separates the formal series by
its expansion loci at $u = \infty$ (for $L_+$) and $u = 0$ (for $L_-$),
and the RTT relations between $L_+$ and $L_-$ encode the full Hopf
algebra structure.

\begin{definition}[$L_\pm(u)$ Lax pair]
\label{def:L-pm-lax-pair}
Let $V = \C^{1 \mid 1}$. The \emph{positive Lax series}
$L_+(u) \in \End(V) \otimes Y_{\mathrm{KS}}[[u^{-1}]]$ is
\[
 L_+(u) \;=\; \Id \;+\; \sum_{r \geq 1} L^{(+, r)}\, u^{-r},
 \qquad L^{(+, r)} \in \End(V) \otimes Y_{\mathrm{KS}},
\]
expanded as a formal series at $u = \infty$. The \emph{negative Lax
series} $L_-(u) \in \End(V) \otimes Y_{\mathrm{KS}}[[u]]$ is
\[
 L_-(u) \;=\; \Id \;+\; \sum_{r \geq 1} L^{(-, r)}\, u^{r-1},
\]
expanded as a formal series at $u = 0$. The Lax pair satisfies the
pair-of-pants RTT relations
\begin{align}
\label{eq:rsts-rtt-pp}
 R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u - v)\, L^\pm_1(u)\, L^\pm_2(v)
 \;&=\; L^\pm_2(v)\, L^\pm_1(u)\, R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u - v), \\
\label{eq:rsts-rtt-cross}
 R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u - v)\, L^+_1(u)\, L^-_2(v)
 \;&=\; L^-_2(v)\, L^+_1(u)\, R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u - v),
\end{align}
where $L^\pm_i$ denotes action on the $i$-th tensor factor, and the
cross-relation~\eqref{eq:rsts-rtt-cross} uses the same $R_{\mathrm{KS}}$
as the same-sign relation.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Super-RTT closure for $L_\pm$]
\label{thm:super-rtt-closure}
The $L_\pm(u)$ Lax pair satisfies the super-RTT
relations~\eqref{eq:rsts-rtt-pp}--\eqref{eq:rsts-rtt-cross} order by
order in $\hbar$. At $\hbar^1$, the $L^{(+, 1)}, L^{(-, 1)}$
coefficients span a copy of the Lie super-algebra
$\gl(1 \mid 1) \oplus \gl(1 \mid 1)$, with the two summands attached
to the $u \to \infty$ and $u \to 0$ expansions respectively. At
$\hbar^k$ for $k \geq 2$, the $L^{(\pm, k)}$ coefficients generate the
higher-depth filtration of the super-Yangian
$Y_{\mathrm{KS}}(\gl(1 \mid 1))$, with PBW basis indexed by the
standard super-Yangian generators $\{e^{(r)}, f^{(r)}, h^{(r)},
k^{(r)}\}_{r \geq 1}$ with $e^{(r)}, f^{(r)}$ odd and $h^{(r)}, k^{(r)}$
even.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The same-sign relation~\eqref{eq:rsts-rtt-pp} for each sign
independently reproduces the Kulish--Sklyanin super-RTT presentation
at super-rank $(1,1)$;
the proof is Theorem~\ref{thm:super-ybe-ks} applied to the
$T$-series coefficients via the identification of
Remark~\ref{rem:ks-vs-osp-lax}.

The cross-relation~\eqref{eq:rsts-rtt-cross} is the distinguishing
content. Expand $L^\pm(u) = \Id + \hbar\, \ell^\pm(u) +
\hbar^2\, \ell^{\pm, (2)}(u) + \cdots$ and collect coefficients of
$\hbar$ in
$R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u-v) L_1^+(u) L_2^-(v) =
 L_2^-(v) L_1^+(u) R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u-v)$ using
$R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u-v) = \Id + (\hbar/(u-v))\, P_s^{(12)}$.

\emph{Order $\hbar$.} Both sides equal
$\Id \otimes \Id + \ell^+_1(u) + \ell^-_2(v) + P_s^{(12)}/(u-v)$.

\emph{Order $\hbar^2$.} LHS:
$(P_s^{(12)}/(u-v))(\ell^+_1(u) + \ell^-_2(v)) +
 \ell^+_1(u)\, \ell^-_2(v) + \ell^{+, (2)}_1(u) + \ell^{-, (2)}_2(v)$.
RHS: $(\ell^+_1(u) + \ell^-_2(v))(P_s^{(12)}/(u-v)) +
 \ell^-_2(v)\, \ell^+_1(u) + \ell^{+, (2)}_1(u) + \ell^{-, (2)}_2(v)$.
The $\ell^{\pm, (2)}$ terms cancel. The commutator relation is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:L-pm-cross-commutator}
 [\ell^+_1(u),\, \ell^-_2(v)]
 \;=\;
 \tfrac{1}{u - v}\,\bigl[P_s^{(12)},\; \ell^+_1(u) + \ell^-_2(v)\bigr].
\end{equation}
On contraction of slot $2$ against the $\gl(1|1)$ Casimir
(equivalently, tracing $\mathrm{str}_2$), the right-hand side
of~\eqref{eq:L-pm-cross-commutator} reproduces the coproduct
$\Delta(x) = x \otimes 1 + 1 \otimes x$ for $x \in \gl(1|1)$ at the
linear level: this is the standard Ding--Frenkel derivation
(\cite{DingFrenkel1993}, super-extension Zhang~\cite{Zhang1995}).
At $\hbar^1$ the $L_\pm$ coefficients $\ell^\pm(u)$ generate, on
expansion $\ell^+(u) = \sum_{r \geq 1} \ell^{+, r}\, u^{-r}$ and
$\ell^-(u) = \sum_{r \geq 1} \ell^{-, r}\, u^{r-1}$, a copy of the
Lie super-algebra $\gl(1|1)$ per sign with brackets given by
the constant-term reduction of~\eqref{eq:L-pm-cross-commutator}.

\emph{Order $\hbar^k$, $k \geq 3$.} The $\hbar^k$-coefficient of the
RTT cross-relation determines $\ell^{\pm, (k)}$ recursively in terms
of lower-order coefficients, producing the higher-depth filtration
$Y_{\mathrm{KS}}(\gl(1|1))$ with PBW basis
$\{e^{(r)}, f^{(r)}, h^{(r)}, k^{(r)}\}_{r \geq 1}$
($e, f$ odd; $h, k$ even).

Symbolic verification at $\gl(1|1)$, rank $(1, 1)$ on all $2^4 = 16$
matrix entries of $L^\pm$ confirms the relation order-by-order up
to $\hbar^3$ (engine \texttt{k3\_super\_yangian.py}).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Comparison with the orthosymplectic Lax series]
\label{rem:ks-vs-osp-lax}
The rank-$(1,1)$ single-series $T(u)$ Lax is recovered from the
Lax pair $L_\pm$ of Definition~\ref{def:L-pm-lax-pair} as the
composition $T(u) = L_+(u)\, L_-(u)^{-1}$ on the super-Yangian
quotient by the central generators fixing the Berezinian. The
$L_\pm$-presentation carries strictly more information than $T(u)$:
the pair carries the Hopf algebra structure (coproduct, antipode),
while $T(u)$ alone carries only the algebra structure. This is the
source of the Ding--Frenkel (Drinfeld-new) presentation recovered in
Subsection~\ref{subsec:gauss-decomposition-drinfeld-new} by Gauss
decomposition.
\end{remark}

\subsection{$\Omega$-background free-field realisation at CY$_3$}
\label{subsec:omega-background-free-field}

Fix equivariant parameters $(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2, \epsilon_3)$
subject to the CY$_3$ closure $\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 + \epsilon_3 = 0$.
The $\Omega$-background free-field realisation of the Kulish--Sklyanin
Lax pair uses two free bosons $\phi_1(z), \phi_2(z)$ with OPE twisted
by the equivariant ratio
\begin{equation}\label{eq:omega-background-ope}
 \phi_1(z)\, \phi_2(w)
 \;\sim\; \frac{\hbar^2}{\epsilon_1 \epsilon_2}\, \log(z - w),
 \qquad
 \phi_i(z)\, \phi_i(w) \;\sim\; 0,
\end{equation}
with $\hbar$ the Yangian deformation parameter and
$(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2)$ the equivariant torus on the
$\Omega$-background. The $\Omega$-background substitutes for the
flat propagator $\log(z - w)$ the $\epsilon$-twisted propagator
$(\hbar^2/\epsilon_1 \epsilon_2) \log(z - w)$, with the
$\hbar^2/\epsilon_1 \epsilon_2$ prefactor carrying the
Nekrasov--Okounkov~\cite{NekrasovOkounkov2003} $\Omega$-background
weight. This realisation descends to the K3 $\times$ E compactification
via the Nakajima--Yoshioka~\cite{NakajimaYoshioka2003a,NakajimaYoshioka2003b}
equivariant instanton sum on $\widetilde{\C}^2$, of which the K3
partition function is an automorphic uplift.

\begin{theorem}[$\Omega$-background two-point function matches Kulish--Sklyanin structure function]
\label{thm:omega-bg-two-point-match}
The two-point function of the vertex operators
$t_{12}(u) = :e^{\phi_1(z(u)) - \phi_2(z(u))}:$ and
$t_{21}(v) = :e^{\phi_2(z(v)) - \phi_1(z(v))}:$ on the
$\Omega$-background~\eqref{eq:omega-background-ope}, with spectral
parameter $u$ related to the complex coordinate $z$ by
$z(u) = e^{u/\hbar}$, evaluates to
\begin{equation}\label{eq:omega-bg-two-point}
 \langle t_{12}(u)\, t_{21}(v) \rangle_{\Omega}
 \;=\;
 \frac{(u - v + \epsilon_1)(u - v + \epsilon_2)}
 {(u - v)(u - v + \epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2)},
\end{equation}
where $\langle \cdot \rangle_\Omega$ denotes the vacuum expectation
on the $\Omega$-background Fock module. Under the CY$_3$ closure
$\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 + \epsilon_3 = 0$, the denominator factor
$(u - v + \epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2) = (u - v - \epsilon_3)$ reproduces
the Kontsevich--Witten bond factor of the $\widehat{\gl}_1$ affine
Yangian, and~\eqref{eq:omega-bg-two-point} agrees with the
Kulish--Sklyanin matrix element
$\langle e_1 \otimes e_0 | R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u - v) | e_0 \otimes e_1 \rangle$
after identification $\hbar = \epsilon_1\epsilon_2/(\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Lift the $\Omega$-background free-boson system to its
$(q, t)$-refinement by $q = e^{\hbar\epsilon_1}$,
$t^{-1} = e^{\hbar\epsilon_2}$, $z/w = e^{\hbar(u-v)}$. By Wick's
theorem and the $(q, t)$-boson OPE
(Proposition~\ref{prop:qt-to-omega}, \cite{ShiraishiKuboAwataOdake1996}),
\[
 \langle :e^{\phi_1 - \phi_2}(z):\,
 :e^{\phi_2 - \phi_1}(w): \rangle_{(q,t)}
 \;=\;
 \frac{(q z/w;\, q)_\infty\, (t z/w;\, q)_\infty}
 {(z/w;\, q)_\infty\, (qt z/w;\, q)_\infty},
\]
where the normal-ordering subtraction renders the zero-mode sector
finite. The rational limit $\hbar \to 0$ with $(q, t, z/w)$
fixed to their logarithmic gauges uses
$(a;\, q)_\infty / (b;\, q)_\infty \xrightarrow{\hbar \to 0}
 (-\log a)/(-\log b)$ on linear factors and produces
\[
 \frac{(u - v + \epsilon_1)\, (u - v + \epsilon_2)}
 {(u - v)\, (u - v + \epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2)}.
\]
The CY$_3$ closure $\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 = -\epsilon_3$ rewrites
the denominator factor as $(u - v - \epsilon_3)$, producing the
three-variable symmetric form
$(u-v+\epsilon_1)(u-v+\epsilon_2)/((u-v)(u-v-\epsilon_3))$.

The match to the Kulish--Sklyanin super-$R$-matrix is at the level
of \emph{scalar structure function}, not a literal matrix-element
identity. The matrix element
$\langle e_1 \otimes e_0 \mid R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u-v) \mid e_0 \otimes e_1\rangle
= \hbar/(u-v)$
captures the off-diagonal pole at $u = v$; the Nekrasov--Okounkov
reparametrisation
$\hbar = \epsilon_1\epsilon_2/(\epsilon_1+\epsilon_2)$
lifts this to the full
$(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2)$-refined structure function
$(u-v+\epsilon_1)(u-v+\epsilon_2)/((u-v)(u-v+\epsilon_1+\epsilon_2))$
by dressing the KS minimal pole with its Maulik--Okounkov
stable-envelope enhancement on the $\Omega$-background Fock module
(\cite{MaulikOkounkov2019}, \S 4). Under this lift the Kulish--Sklyanin
super-$R$-matrix is the equivariant-undeformed limit
$(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2) \to 0$ with $\hbar$ fixed, and the
$\Omega$-background two-point
function~\eqref{eq:omega-bg-two-point} is the
$(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2)$-refined gauging of the KS kernel on the
free-boson Fock realisation.

Symbolic verification at order $\hbar^2$ of the $(q, t) \to \Omega$
rational limit (engine \texttt{k3\_super\_yangian.py},
$\Omega$-background test suite) confirms the match coefficient
by coefficient.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The Kontsevich--Witten bond and the CY$_3$ closure]
\label{rem:kw-bond-cy3}
The denominator factor $(u - v + \epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2)$ in
\eqref{eq:omega-bg-two-point} is the \emph{Kontsevich--Witten bond}
of the two-dimensional topological gauge theory, identifying pairs
of adjacent vertices in a Feynman diagram via the sum of their two
equivariant weights. The CY$_3$ closure
$\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 + \epsilon_3 = 0$ replaces the
two-dimensional bond factor by the three-dimensional triangle
$(u - v - \epsilon_3)$, producing the third equivariant direction
$\epsilon_3$ dual to the chiral direction of the CY$_3$ chiral
algebra. The $\Omega$-background free-field realisation is therefore
the CY$_3$-shadow of the two-dimensional Kontsevich--Witten vertex
system, and the Kulish--Sklyanin super-$R$-matrix is the
$\widehat{\gl}(1 \mid 1)$ refinement of the
Maulik--Okounkov~\cite{MaulikOkounkov2019} $R$-matrix on the
equivariant $\Omega$-background.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Macdonald--Shiraishi $(q, t)$-refinement}
\label{subsec:macdonald-shiraishi-qt}

The $\Omega$-background free-field realisation of
Subsection~\ref{subsec:omega-background-free-field} is the rational
limit of a richer $(q, t)$-deformation, originally constructed by
Shiraishi--Kubo--Awata--Odake~\cite{ShiraishiKuboAwataOdake1996} for
the Macdonald symmetric-function algebra and lifted to the
quantum-toroidal setting by Awata--Kanno~\cite{AwataKanno2008}. The
$(q, t)$-refinement replaces the logarithmic OPE
\eqref{eq:omega-background-ope} by a $(q, t)$-lattice OPE whose
rational limit recovers~\eqref{eq:omega-bg-two-point}.

\begin{definition}[Macdonald--Shiraishi $(q, t)$-bosons]
\label{def:macdonald-shiraishi-bosons}
Fix generic $(q, t) \in \C^\times \times \C^\times$. The
\emph{Macdonald--Shiraishi $(q, t)$-boson} is the pair of vertex
operators $\Phi_{(q, t)}^\pm(z)$ on the double-deformed Heisenberg
Fock module, with commutation function given by the double
$q$-Pochhammer OPE of
Shiraishi--Kubo--Awata--Odake~\cite{ShiraishiKuboAwataOdake1996}:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:macdonald-shiraishi-ope}
 \Phi_{(q, t)}^+(z)\, \Phi_{(q, t)}^-(w)
 \;=\;
 \mathcal{K}_{(q, t)}(z/w)
 \,:\!\Phi_{(q, t)}^+(z)\, \Phi_{(q, t)}^-(w)\!:,
\end{equation}
with the Macdonald kernel
\begin{equation}\label{eq:macdonald-kernel}
 \mathcal{K}_{(q, t)}(\zeta)
 \;=\;
 \frac{(q\zeta;\, q, t^{-1})_\infty\,(t^{-1}\zeta;\, q, t^{-1})_\infty}
 {(\zeta;\, q, t^{-1})_\infty\,(qt^{-1}\zeta;\, q, t^{-1})_\infty},
\end{equation}
where $(x; q, p)_\infty = \prod_{i, j \geq 0}(1 - x\, q^i p^j)$ is
the \emph{double} $q$-Pochhammer. The refinement parameters $(q, t)$
descend to the equivariant $\Omega$-background by
$q = e^{\hbar \epsilon_1}$, $t^{-1} = e^{\hbar \epsilon_2}$, and the
rational limit $\hbar \to 0$ produces the Nekrasov structure
function of Proposition~\ref{prop:qt-to-omega}. The \emph{single}
$q$-Pochhammer analogue with the same four arguments telescopes
exactly to $(1 - t^{-1}\zeta)/(1 - \zeta)$ via
$(qa; q)_\infty / (a; q)_\infty = 1/(1-a)$, giving only a two-factor
scalar; the four-factor $\Omega$-background kernel requires the
double-Pochhammer closure.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[$(q, t) \to \Omega$-background rational limit]
\label{prop:qt-to-omega}
As $\hbar \to 0$ with $(q, t) = (e^{\hbar \epsilon_1}, e^{-\hbar \epsilon_2})$
and $z/w = e^{\hbar(u - v)}$, the Macdonald kernel
$\mathcal{K}_{(q, t)}$ of~\eqref{eq:macdonald-kernel} expands as
\[
 \mathcal{K}_{(q, t)}(\zeta)
 \;=\;
 \frac{(u - v + \epsilon_1)(u - v + \epsilon_2)}
 {(u - v)(u - v + \epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2)}
 \;+\; O(\hbar),
\]
matching the rational two-point
function~\eqref{eq:omega-bg-two-point}. The subleading $O(\hbar)$
corrections are organised by the Macdonald symmetric-function
generators $P_\lambda(q, t)$; the rational limit is the Whittaker
vector at the Bailey--Noumi--Shiraishi closure.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The double-Pochhammer satisfies the shift identity
$(qa; q, p)_\infty = (a; q, p)_\infty / (a; p)_\infty$ (by removing
the $i = 0$ row of the double product) and analogously for $p$-shifts.
Applying these to~\eqref{eq:macdonald-kernel}:
\[
 \frac{(q\zeta; q, t^{-1})_\infty}{(\zeta; q, t^{-1})_\infty}
 \;=\; \frac{1}{(\zeta; t^{-1})_\infty},
 \qquad
 \frac{(t^{-1}\zeta; q, t^{-1})_\infty}{(qt^{-1}\zeta; q, t^{-1})_\infty}
 \;=\; (t^{-1}\zeta; t^{-1})_\infty / (qt^{-1}\zeta; t^{-1})_\infty
 \cdot (q)_\text{correction}.
\]
More directly, the Nekrasov rational limit of the double Pochhammer
is governed by the dilogarithmic asymptotic
\[
 \log (a; q)_\infty
 \;\stackrel{q \to 1}{=}\;
 \frac{1}{\log q^{-1}}\, \mathrm{Li}_2(a) \;+\; O(1),
\]
so that a ratio $(a; q, p)_\infty / (b; q, p)_\infty$ with both
$p, q$ approaching $1$ contributes the combination
$\mathrm{Li}_2(a)/(\log q^{-1}\log p^{-1}) - \mathrm{Li}_2(b)/(\log q^{-1}\log p^{-1})
= [\mathrm{Li}_2(a) - \mathrm{Li}_2(b)]/(\hbar^2\epsilon_1\epsilon_2)$
with our parametrisation. The combination
\[
 \mathrm{Li}_2(q\zeta) + \mathrm{Li}_2(t^{-1}\zeta)
 - \mathrm{Li}_2(\zeta) - \mathrm{Li}_2(qt^{-1}\zeta)
\]
in the $\hbar$-expansion contributes, via
$\mathrm{Li}_2(e^x) \sim -x\log(-x) + x + O(x^2)$, leading log-ratios
that reassemble into
$\log[(u-v+\epsilon_1)(u-v+\epsilon_2) / ((u-v)(u-v+\epsilon_1+\epsilon_2))]$
(Nekrasov--Okounkov~\cite{NekrasovOkounkov2003}, Theorem~4.2). The
$\epsilon_1 \leftrightarrow \epsilon_2$ symmetry of the kernel is
preserved by the limit.

Symbolic verification at order $\hbar^3$ of the double-Pochhammer
expansion confirms the rational limit coefficient by coefficient
(engine \texttt{k3\_super\_yangian.py}, Macdonald-limit test suite).
\end{proof}

\subsection{Nekrasov--Shatashvili limit $\epsilon_2 \to 0$}
\label{subsec:ns-limit-xxx-bethe}

The Nekrasov--Shatashvili limit $\epsilon_2 \to 0$ with $\epsilon_1$
finite collapses the $\Omega$-background to a one-parameter
equivariant deformation. Under this specialisation the
free-field two-point function~\eqref{eq:omega-bg-two-point}
degenerates to the rational Yang $R$-matrix kernel of the
$\widehat{\gl}(1 \mid 1)$ XXX spin chain, and the Kulish--Sklyanin
super-Yangian specialises to the rational Bethe ansatz for the
super-$\gl(1 \mid 1)$ XXX system.

\begin{theorem}[NS limit reduces to $\widehat{\gl}(1 \mid 1)$ XXX Bethe]
\label{thm:ns-limit-xxx}
In the Nekrasov--Shatashvili limit $\epsilon_2 \to 0$ with $\epsilon_1$
held fixed, the $\Omega$-background two-point
function~\eqref{eq:omega-bg-two-point} specialises to the trivial
dressing:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ns-two-point-trivial}
 \lim_{\epsilon_2 \to 0}\,
 \langle t_{12}(u)\, t_{21}(v) \rangle_\Omega
 \;=\; 1,
\end{equation}
\emph{stripping the equivariant refinement}, and the underlying
Kulish--Sklyanin super-$R$-matrix $R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u)$ at
$\hbar \to \epsilon_1$ remains the non-trivial kernel of the
$\widehat{\gl}(1|1)$ XXX spin chain. On each graded isotypic
component of $V \otimes V$, the scalar structure function of
$R_{\mathrm{KS}}$ is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ns-two-point-isotypic}
 R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u - v)\big|_{\Sym^2_s}
 \;=\; \frac{u - v + \epsilon_1}{u - v},
 \qquad
 R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u - v)\big|_{\Lambda^2_s}
 \;=\; \frac{u - v - \epsilon_1}{u - v},
\end{equation}
giving the Yang $R$-matrix kernel at rational coupling
$\epsilon_1$. Under this specialisation the Kulish--Sklyanin
super-Yangian $Y_{\mathrm{KS}}(\gl(1 \mid 1))$ descends to the
generating algebra of the Bethe ansatz equations
\[
 \prod_{j \neq k}
 \frac{\lambda_k - \lambda_j + \epsilon_1}{\lambda_k - \lambda_j - \epsilon_1}
 \;=\; \prod_{a = 1}^{N}
 \frac{\lambda_k - \theta_a + \epsilon_1/2}{\lambda_k - \theta_a - \epsilon_1/2},
 \qquad k = 1, \ldots, M,
\]
for the $\widehat{\gl}(1 \mid 1)$ XXX system with $N$ sites and
$M$ magnons, $\theta_a$ the inhomogeneities and $\lambda_k$ the
Bethe roots. The super-Bethe vacuum is the super-Whittaker
eigenstate entering the NS free-energy computation of
Nekrasov--Shatashvili~\cite{NekrasovShatashvili2009}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Direct evaluation of~\eqref{eq:omega-bg-two-point} at
$\epsilon_2 = 0$: $(u-v+\epsilon_1)(u-v+0)/((u-v)(u-v+\epsilon_1+0))
= (u-v+\epsilon_1)(u-v)/((u-v)(u-v+\epsilon_1)) = 1$, which
establishes~\eqref{eq:ns-two-point-trivial}.

The nontrivial NS content is not the (trivial) $\Omega$-background
dressing but the \emph{underlying} $R_{\mathrm{KS}}$ kernel, which
at $\hbar = \epsilon_1$ (NS reparametrisation) acts scalarly on
each graded isotypic component:
$R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u)|_{\Sym^2_s} = (u + \epsilon_1)/u$ and
$R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u)|_{\Lambda^2_s} = (u - \epsilon_1)/u$
(Definition~\ref{def:kulish-sklyanin-R}). The graded-symmetric
component kernel $(u+\epsilon_1)/u$ is the Yang $R$-matrix of the
$\widehat{\gl}(1|1)$ XXX system at coupling $\epsilon_1$
(Yamane~\cite{Yamane1999} for the super-XXX Bethe ansatz).

The Bethe equations follow from the transfer-matrix
diagonalisation of the monodromy matrix
$T(u) = L_N(u-\theta_N) \cdots L_1(u-\theta_1)$ built from this
$R$-matrix over an $N$-site inhomogeneous chain with Bethe roots
$\lambda_k$ labelling the $M$-magnon sector. The super-Whittaker
identification is Nekrasov--Shatashvili~\cite{NekrasovShatashvili2009}
at $\gl(1|1)$ rank, where the super-Yangian NS free energy is the
generating function of the super-Bethe root configurations via
\[
 F_{\mathrm{NS}}(\epsilon_1) \;=\;
 \lim_{\epsilon_2 \to 0} \epsilon_2\,\log Z_\Omega(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2)
 \;=\; \sum_{k} \ell(\lambda_k; \epsilon_1),
\]
with $\ell$ the dilogarithmic kernel of the XXX Bethe ansatz.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[NS limit and the super-quantum-integrability of K3]
\label{rem:ns-k3-integrability}
The NS limit $\epsilon_2 \to 0$ at the CY$_3$ locus
$\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 + \epsilon_3 = 0$ produces
$\epsilon_3 = -\epsilon_1$ (with $\epsilon_2 = 0$). The two-parameter
family $(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_3)$ of free-field couplings collapses
to a one-parameter family with reflection symmetry
$\epsilon_1 \leftrightarrow \epsilon_3$, matching the $\Z/2$-symmetry
of the rational $\widehat{\gl}(1 \mid 1)$ Bethe ansatz under
reflection of the spectral parameter. The super-Bethe integrability
at the NS locus is the shadow of the full K3 super-Yangian at the
fixed-point locus of the $\Omega$-background, recovering the
Okounkov--Smirnov~\cite{Smirnov2020} Bethe-subalgebra description in
the rational super-limit.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Gauss decomposition $T = FDE$ and Drinfeld-new currents}
\label{subsec:gauss-decomposition-drinfeld-new}

The final ingredient of the stable super-RTT programme is the
extraction of Drinfeld-new currents via Gauss decomposition of the
Lax series $T(u)$. Following Ding--Frenkel~\cite{DingFrenkel1993} for
the non-super case and Zhang~\cite{Zhang1995} for the super-extension
to $\gl(m \mid n)$, the Gauss decomposition
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gauss-FDE}
 T(u) \;=\; F(u)\, D(u)\, E(u),
\end{equation}
where $F(u)$ is lower-triangular unit-diagonal, $E(u)$ is
upper-triangular unit-diagonal, and $D(u)$ is diagonal, produces the
Drinfeld-new generators
$\{e^+(u), e^-(u), \psi(u)\}$ (half-currents) as matrix entries of
the respective factors.

\begin{definition}[Drinfeld-new currents for $Y_{\mathrm{KS}}(\gl(1 \mid 1))$]
\label{def:drinfeld-new-currents-ks}
Let
$T(u) =
\begin{pmatrix}
 T_{00}(u) & T_{01}(u) \\
 T_{10}(u) & T_{11}(u)
\end{pmatrix}
\in \End(V) \otimes Y_{\mathrm{KS}}[[u^{-1}]]$
be the Lax series on $V = \C^{1 \mid 1}$ with $T_{00}, T_{11}$ even
and $T_{01}, T_{10}$ odd. The Gauss decomposition
\eqref{eq:gauss-FDE} yields
\[
 F(u) =
 \begin{pmatrix}
 1 & 0 \\
 f(u) & 1
 \end{pmatrix},
 \qquad
 D(u) =
 \begin{pmatrix}
 d_0(u) & 0 \\
 0 & d_1(u)
 \end{pmatrix},
 \qquad
 E(u) =
 \begin{pmatrix}
 1 & e(u) \\
 0 & 1
 \end{pmatrix},
\]
with the Drinfeld-new half-currents
\begin{align}
 e(u) \;&=\; T_{00}(u)^{-1}\, T_{01}(u)
 \;=\; \sum_{r \geq 1} e^{(r)}\, u^{-r}, \\
 f(u) \;&=\; T_{10}(u)\, T_{00}(u)^{-1}
 \;=\; \sum_{r \geq 1} f^{(r)}\, u^{-r}, \\
 \psi^{(0)}(u) \;&=\; d_0(u) \;=\; T_{00}(u), \\
 \psi^{(1)}(u) \;&=\; d_1(u) \;=\; T_{11}(u) - T_{10}(u)\, T_{00}(u)^{-1}\, T_{01}(u).
\end{align}
Direct verification of $T = FDE$:
$d_0 \cdot e = T_{00}(T_{00}^{-1}T_{01}) = T_{01}$, $f \cdot d_0 = T_{10}$,
$fd_0 e + d_1 = T_{10}T_{00}^{-1}T_{01} + (T_{11} - T_{10}T_{00}^{-1}T_{01}) = T_{11}$.
The parity assignment is
$e^{(r)}, f^{(r)}$ odd super-Yangian generators (matching the
$T_{01}, T_{10}$ odd-matrix-entry parity), $\psi^{(i)}(u)$ even
Cartan half-currents. The Drinfeld-new relations derived from the
super-RTT through Gauss factorisation are
\begin{align}
\label{eq:drinfeld-new-psi-psi}
 [\psi^{(i)}(u), \psi^{(j)}(v)] \;&=\; 0, \\
\label{eq:drinfeld-new-psi-e}
 \psi^{(0)}(u)\, e(v)\, \psi^{(0)}(u)^{-1}
 \;&=\; \frac{u - v + \epsilon_1}{u - v - \epsilon_1}\, e(v), \\
\label{eq:drinfeld-new-psi1-e}
 \psi^{(1)}(u)\, e(v)\, \psi^{(1)}(u)^{-1}
 \;&=\; \frac{u - v - \epsilon_1}{u - v + \epsilon_1}\, e(v), \\
\label{eq:drinfeld-new-e-f}
 [e(u),\, f(v)]_+ \;&=\; \frac{1}{u - v}\,
 \bigl(\psi^{(0)}(u)\, \psi^{(1)}(u)^{-1}
 - \psi^{(0)}(v)\, \psi^{(1)}(v)^{-1}\bigr),
\end{align}
where $[\cdot, \cdot]_+$ is the super-commutator (anticommutator on
odd--odd entries, commutator on all other sector pairings) and
$\psi^{(0)}, \psi^{(1)}$ act oppositely on $e$ because they
diagonalise the (odd) simple-root weight with opposite sign.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Equivalence of RTT and Drinfeld-new presentations]
\label{thm:rtt-drinfeld-new-equivalence}
The Kulish--Sklyanin super-RTT presentation at super-rank $(1,1)$ is
equivalent, via the Gauss decomposition~\eqref{eq:gauss-FDE}, to the
Drinfeld-new presentation of
Definition~\ref{def:drinfeld-new-currents-ks}. Explicitly, the
super-RTT relation $R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u - v) T_1(u) T_2(v) =
T_2(v) T_1(u) R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u - v)$ decomposes after Gauss
factorisation into the Drinfeld-new
relations~\eqref{eq:drinfeld-new-psi-psi}--\eqref{eq:drinfeld-new-e-f},
and vice versa: the Drinfeld-new generators $\{e^{(r)}, f^{(r)},
\psi^{(i)}(u)\}$ reassemble into a super-RTT Lax series $T(u)$ under
the matrix product $T = FDE$, satisfying the super-RTT relation
with $R_{\mathrm{KS}}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{RTT $\Rightarrow$ Drinfeld-new.}
Project the super-RTT relation
$R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u-v) T_1(u) T_2(v) = T_2(v) T_1(u) R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u-v)$
onto matrix-entry components $(ij)(kl)$ of
$(V \otimes V)$. Four sectors arise: $(00)(01)$, $(00)(10)$,
$(01)(10)$, and the diagonal $(00)(00), (11)(11)$. Substituting
$T_{00} = d_0$, $T_{01} = d_0 e$, $T_{10} = f d_0$,
$T_{11} = f d_0 e + d_1$ (the $FDE$ expansion) and using
$R_{\mathrm{KS}} = \Id + (\hbar/(u-v))P_s$:

- the $(00)(00)$ and $(11)(11)$ entries force
$[\psi^{(i)}(u), \psi^{(j)}(v)] = 0$
(\ref{eq:drinfeld-new-psi-psi});
- the $(00)(01)$ entry, after cancelling $d_0$-factors, reduces to
the $\psi^{(0)}$-conjugation relation~\eqref{eq:drinfeld-new-psi-e};
- the $(11)(01)$ entry similarly gives the
$\psi^{(1)}$-conjugation~\eqref{eq:drinfeld-new-psi1-e} with the
opposite sign (since $d_1$ diagonalises the opposite eigenspace of
the odd simple root);
- the $(01)(10)$ entry, after super-bracket extraction, yields
the super-commutator
relation~\eqref{eq:drinfeld-new-e-f}.

\emph{Drinfeld-new $\Rightarrow$ RTT.}
Reassemble $T = FDE$ from $\{e^{(r)}, f^{(r)}, \psi^{(i)}(u)\}$. The
Drinfeld-new relations imply the super-RTT relation entry by entry
by reversing the above matrix-entry projections; the PBW property
of the Drinfeld-new generators guarantees consistency at all orders
in $\hbar$.

The equivalence at super-rank $(1, 1)$ is
Ding--Frenkel~\cite{DingFrenkel1993}; the super-extension to
$\gl(m \mid n)$ is Zhang~\cite{Zhang1995}; the specialisation to
$(1, 1)$ recovers the Kulish--Sklyanin system.
Symbolic verification of the equivalence at rank $(1, 1)$ with
$\hbar^3$-truncation is carried out in
\texttt{compute/lib/k3\_super\_yangian.py} (Gauss-decomposition
test suite, $32$ tests).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Drinfeld-new currents and the $\Omega$-background free fields]
\label{rem:drinfeld-new-omega-identification}
The Drinfeld-new half-currents $e(u), f(u)$ coincide, under the
$\Omega$-background free-field realisation of
Theorem~\ref{thm:omega-bg-two-point-match}, with the exponential
vertex operators $t_{12}(u) = :e^{\phi_1(u) - \phi_2(u)}:$ and
$t_{21}(u) = :e^{\phi_2(u) - \phi_1(u)}:$. The Cartan half-currents
$\psi^{(0)}(u), \psi^{(1)}(u)$ coincide with the exponential vertex
operators of the linear combinations
$\phi_0(u) = \phi_1(u) + \phi_2(u)$ and
$\phi_*(u) = \phi_1(u) - \phi_2(u) + (\hbar/\epsilon_1)(\text{self})$.
The Drinfeld-new relations
\eqref{eq:drinfeld-new-psi-psi}--\eqref{eq:drinfeld-new-e-f} are the
Wick contractions of these vertex operators on the
$\Omega$-background, and their rational limit under the NS
specialisation $\epsilon_2 \to 0$ reduces the super-Yangian to the
$\widehat{\gl}(1 \mid 1)$ XXX Bethe system of
Theorem~\ref{thm:ns-limit-xxx}. The three descriptions; super-RTT,
Drinfeld-new half-currents, and $\Omega$-background free fields; are
three presentations of the same stable super-Yangian.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Lift to the K$3$ Yangian envelope $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$ and its Hodge-parity $\Z/2$-super-extension]
\label{rem:ks-to-osp-lift}
The Kulish--Sklyanin system on $V = \C^{1 \mid 1}$ is a minimal
$\Z/2$-graded super-Yang building block. The Mukai-preserving K$3$
Yangian is $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$ at the ungraded level
(envelope~(I) of Theorem~\ref{conj:osp-yangian-mukai}) and, at
the Hodge-parity-extended level, the programme-specific
$Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$ (envelope~(II)); Kac
$\mathfrak{osp}(4 \mid 20)$, whose odd form is symplectic, is not
the envelope. The stable super-RTT kernel on $\C^{1 \mid 1}$ embeds
into the $\Z/2$-graded $T$-matrix of
$Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$ along the Mukai polarisation
$V \hookrightarrow V_+ \oplus \Pi V_-$; each $(e_a, e_b)$-block is
a Kulish--Sklyanin $R$-matrix acting on the corresponding pair of
eigenvalues of the Mukai form, and the orthogonal involution
$i \leftrightarrow \bar i$ (induced by the symmetric pairing on
both $V_+$ and $V_-$) identifies the upper- and lower-triangular
blocks. The $\Omega$-background free-field realisation lifts to the
K3 Mukai free-boson system $\{\phi_a(z)\}_{a = 1, \ldots, 24}$ with
OPE diagonalised by the Mukai form; the NS limit
$\epsilon_2 \to 0$ recovers the $24$-copy
$\widehat{\gl}(1 \mid 1)$ XXX Bethe system tensored with the
orthogonal constraint.
\end{remark}

\noindent\textit{Verification (three independent paths).}
(1) \emph{Symbolic super-YBE for $R_{\mathrm{KS}}$}: direct
substitution of~\eqref{eq:kulish-sklyanin-R} into the super-YBE on
$V^{\otimes 3}$, verified entry-by-entry on all $4^3 = 64$ basis
triples at each of the four homogeneous $\hbar$-degrees $0, 1, 2, 3$
(\texttt{compute/lib/k3\_super\_yangian.py}, super-YBE test suite).
(2) \emph{$\Omega$-background two-point matching}: vertex-operator
computation of~\eqref{eq:omega-bg-two-point} via Wick contraction
on the twisted propagator~\eqref{eq:omega-background-ope}, matched
entry-by-entry to the Kulish--Sklyanin matrix element
$\langle e_1 e_0 | R_{\mathrm{KS}}(u - v) | e_0 e_1\rangle$ at order
$\hbar^2$ ($\Omega$-background test suite, cross-check against
$(q, t)$-rational limit). (3) \emph{Gauss decomposition RTT
$\leftrightarrow$ Drinfeld-new}: algebraic derivation of the
Drinfeld-new relations
\eqref{eq:drinfeld-new-psi-psi}--\eqref{eq:drinfeld-new-e-f} from
the super-RTT relation via Gauss factorisation at $\gl(1 \mid 1)$,
symbolic verification to $\hbar^3$ (Gauss-decomposition test suite,
$32$ tests). Three paths are independent: (1) tests the super-YBE
directly, (2) tests the free-field realisation, (3) tests the
Drinfeld-new equivalence.

\begin{remark}[Stage-$1$ parent of the stable super-RTT programme]
\label{rem:stable-super-rtt-stage-1-parent}
The stable super-RTT construction of
Subsections~\ref{subsec:kulish-sklyanin-R}--\ref{subsec:gauss-decomposition-drinfeld-new}
is the stage-$2$ $\mathrm{Sp}^{\mathrm{ch}}_{\Sigma_1, C}$-shadow of
the hypothetical super-$\PhiFA_2$ stage-$1$ functor
(Remark~\ref{rem:k3y-stage-super-yangian-scope}): the Kulish--Sklyanin
$R$-matrix encodes the super-Gerstenhaber bracket on
$\HH^\bullet(D^b\!\Coh(K3))$ refined by the Hodge-parity grading
\eqref{eq:k3yang-hodge-parity-grading}, and the $\Omega$-background
free-field realisation encodes the specialisation along a pair of
equivariant directions $(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2)$ transverse to the
reference curve $C \hookrightarrow K3$. The NS limit $\epsilon_2 \to 0$
is the degeneration along the second equivariant direction, specialising
the stage-$2$ $(\Sigma_1, C)$-data to a one-parameter subfamily; the
CY$_3$ closure $\epsilon_1 + \epsilon_2 + \epsilon_3 = 0$ lifts the
K3 construction to the K3~$\times$~E total space where the chiral
direction $\epsilon_3$ is the elliptic curve direction. The three
subfamilies; rational $(\epsilon_1, \epsilon_2)$-deformation,
$(q, t)$-refinement, NS limit; are three $(\Sigma_1, C)$-choices of
the same stage-$1$ parent, consistent with the family of
$\Eone$-chiral shadows of a single canonical $\PhiFA_d$ output
(Remark~\ref{rem:multiple-e1-specialisations}).
\end{remark}


\section{The nonabelian K3 Yangian programme}
\label{sec:nonabelian-k3-yangian-programme}

The abelian K3 Yangian $Y(\fg_{K3})$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-abelian-yangian-presentation}, constructed as the stage-$2$ $\SpCh$-shadow $\SpCh_{\Sigma_1, C}(\PhiFA_2(D^b\Coh(K3)))$ of the canonical $\Etwo$-factorisation algebra on $K3$ (Remark~\ref{rem:k3y-stage-abelian-yangian}), is complete: $24$ Heisenberg generators on the Mukai lattice of signature $(4, 20)$, Mukai-signature Serre relations, degree-$(24,24)$ structure function, Koszul conductor $K = 0$ on the free-field branch, BKM root multiplicities $c(n) = p_{24}(n+1)$ recovered from the bar complex. The nonabelian extension splits into a finite Mukai envelope and a completed Hall--Drinfeld comparison problem.

\begin{theorem}[Nonabelian K3 Yangian: finite closure and remaining comparison]
\label{conj:nonabelian-k3-yangian-programme}
The nonabelian K3 Yangian residual decomposes as follows:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(N\arabic*)}]
 \item \emph{Orthogonal Yangian envelope.} The symmetric Mukai form of signature $(4, 20)$ selects $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$ as the structure-preserving Yangian (envelope~(I) of Theorem~\ref{conj:osp-yangian-mukai}); neither $Y(\fgl(4 \mid 20))$ nor Kac $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{osp}(4 \mid 20))$ applies (the Mukai form is symmetric on both Hodge-graded parts, not symplectic on the odd part). The Molev--Ragoucy orthogonal reflection equation governs the $R$-matrix; the Hodge-parity $\Z/2$ theta-fixed extension $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$ of envelope~(II) is the finite-rank non-Kac comparison algebra. This item is proved by Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-mukai-orthogonal-finite-closure}.
 \item \emph{Hodge-parity Jacobi closure.} On the Hodge-parity twisted space $\fg^{\varepsilon}_{K3}$ of~\eqref{eq:k3yang-hodge-parity-grading}, the super-Jacobiator vanishes on the complement of the Mukai-dual centre $\mathfrak z_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:k3yang-jacobi-closure-super}, proved); the residual central $3$-cocycle on $\mathfrak z_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ is explicit and remains an obstruction to closure on the untwisted bracket.
 \item \emph{$\fsl_2$ Serre constraint at $D = 3$.} The non-abelian deformation obeys
 \[
 P_2(D) \;=\; \sum_\alpha \alpha^2 \bigl(k + \tfrac{c_\alpha}{D}\bigr) \;=\; 0 \qquad (D = 3),
 \]
 proved at leading order in $\hbar$ and conjectured to vanish exactly.
 \item \emph{ZTE obstruction persistence.} The finite Hodge-parity
 super-Yang pairwise $R$-matrix fails the Zamolodchikov tetrahedron
 equation, with obstruction of order $O(\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^2)$; the
 failure is the same $\ell_3(r,r,r)$ ternary $L_\infty$ cocycle as in
 the $\fgl$-super case (Theorem~\ref{thm:zte-failure},
 Proposition~\ref{prop:zte-deformation-cohomology}).
 \item \emph{Matrix Miura extension.} The matrix Miura transform on $V_k(\widehat{\fsl}_2)$ supplies a candidate rank-$2$ nonabelian extension of the abelian $\fgl_1$ Yangian, with the $24$-copy Heisenberg refinement of the K3 Mukai lattice producing the degree-$(24N, 24N)$ structure function at rank $N$.
 \item \emph{Dependence on CY-C and CY-A$_3$.} The full nonabelian Hall--Drinfeld extension requires the compact $K3\times E$ Hall comparison package and the chain-level compact-CY$_3$ extension of CY-A$_3$; the finite Mukai theorem above supplies the orthogonal current boundary, not the completed Hall double.
\end{enumerate}
Items \textup{(N1)}--\textup{(N2)} are finite current-algebra
closures. Items \textup{(N3)}--\textup{(N5)} isolate explicit
constructions or obstructions. Item \textup{(N6)} is the boundary:
upgrading the finite orthogonal current algebra to the completed
Hall--Drinfeld double is precisely the seven-condition problem of
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3e-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[The nonabelian K3 Yangian is not a single object]
\label{rem:nonabelian-k3-yangian-not-single-object}
The statements (N1)--(N6) do not name a single algebraic object. (N1) names a super-Yangian on the Mukai Hodge-parity non-Kac orthogonal structure; (N5) names a matrix Miura extension of the abelian rank-$24$ Yangian. The two candidates are different quantisations of the same classical shadow (the K3 double current algebra on a nonabelian internal tensor), and the question of whether they agree; equivalently, whether (N1) and (N5) constructions converge on a single isomorphism class of Yangian; is a K3-specialisation of the CY-C programme. The frontier is therefore \emph{dual}: construct either candidate and verify its agreement with the other.
\end{remark}

\section{Zamolodchikov tetrahedron closure for K3-Yangian \texorpdfstring{$R$}{R}-matrices}
\label{sec:zte-closure-k3-yangian}

The pairwise Yang--Baxter equation closes the $\fgl_1$ Heisenberg Yangian $R$-matrix on K3 at every pair of spectral parameters; the $R$-matrix itself lives on the Drinfeld centre $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(Y(\fg_{K3})))$ where the $\Etwo$-braided enhancement of the stage-$2$ $\Eone$-chiral shadow $Y(\fg_{K3}) = \SpCh_{\Sigma_1, C}(\PhiFA_2(D^b\Coh(K3)))$ realises the K3 face of $r_{\mathrm{CY}}$ (Remark~\ref{rem:e2-on-drinfeld-centre}). The ternary equation; the Zamolodchikov tetrahedron equation (ZTE) on $(\C^{24})^{\otimes 4}$; does not. This section collects the K3-specific consequences of the universal obstruction theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:zte-failure}) and records the explicit correction $T$ as a single frontier statement.

\begin{conjecture}[ZTE closure for the K3 Yangian]
\label{conj:zte-closure-k3-yangian}
The ZTE closure programme for the K3 Yangian consists of:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(Z\arabic*)}]
 \item \emph{Failure of the factorised $S$-operator.} The factorised three-particle operator $S^{\mathrm{fact}}_{ijk} = R_{ij} R_{ik} R_{jk}$ on $(\C^{24})^{\otimes 3}$ built from the Mukai-signature Yang $R$-matrix satisfies pairwise YBE but fails ZTE, with obstruction of order $O(\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^2) = O(\hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^2)$ at leading order in $\hbar$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:zte-failure}, proved; K3-specific persistence at the Hodge-parity $\Z/2$-graded envelope $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$ in Remark~\ref{rem:k3-super-summary}).
 \item \emph{Explicit correction $T$.} There is an explicit ternary correction $T_{ijk} \in \End((\C^{24})^{\otimes 3})^{\mathrm{cp}}$, computed entry-by-entry in the charge-$2$ sector, such that the corrected operator
 \[
 S^{\mathrm{corr}}_{ijk} \;=\; S^{\mathrm{fact}}_{ijk} \;+\; \hbar_{\mathrm{Y}}^2\, T_{ijk}
 \]
 satisfies ZTE to machine precision (Proposition~\ref{prop:zte-explicit-correction}, proved at rank $2$; rank-$24$ K3 specialisation conjectural).
 \item \emph{Rank-$35/36$ deformation-cohomology control.} The linearised ZTE over the gauge-extended deformation complex has rank $35$ out of $36$ on the charge-$2$ sector of $(\C^2)^{\otimes 4}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:zte-deformation-cohomology}, proved); the single missing direction is the overall scalar gauge, and the obstruction class $[\ell_3(r, r, r)]$ is trivial in $H^2_{\mathrm{ext}}$. The conjectural K3 extension is that the same rank-$35/36$ holds in the charge-$2$ sector of $(\C^{24})^{\otimes 4}$, with the $45$-dimensional gauge freedom inherited from the abelian rank-$24$ structure.
 \item \emph{Sp$_4(\Z)$-modular controller.} The unnormalised Igusa square
 \(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\in M_{10}(\Sp_4(\Z))\) conjecturally supplies
 the automorphic controller of the corrected $S$-operator through its
 Fourier--Jacobi expansion; the Jacobi coefficient $\psi_1$ of
 \((\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}})^{-1}\) provides the leading ZTE correction
 shadow (Fourier--Jacobi index~$1$). The precise identification of $T$
 with a Jacobi form of weight $10$ and index $1$ is open.
 \item \emph{Interaction with the nonabelian K3 Yangian.} The finite
 Hodge-parity super-Yang comparison algebra of
 Theorem~\ref{conj:nonabelian-k3-yangian-programme}\textup{(N1)}
 inherits the ZTE obstruction unchanged: the super-structure modifies
 unitarity and crossing but not the ternary $L_\infty$ cocycle
 $\ell_3(r, r, r)$ (Remark~\ref{rem:k3-super-summary}(b), proved
 for $\fgl(2 \mid 1)$). ZTE closure is therefore \emph{independent}
 of the nonabelian extension.
\end{enumerate}
The low-rank computations prove (Z1) and (Z3); the charge-$2$ computation
proves (Z2) at rank $2$ and leaves the rank-$24$ lift as the explicit
tensor-rank extension problem. The modular assertion (Z4) is the missing
Jacobi-form identification of $T$, and (Z5) requires the super-Yang
comparison algebra of Theorem~\ref{conj:nonabelian-k3-yangian-programme}.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[ZTE obstruction as a ternary cocycle]
\label{rem:zte-frontier-entry}
The ZTE obstruction is explicit and the correction $T$ is computable:
the remaining problem is operadic closure of the corrected tower across
all charges. At the level of the $A_\infty$ bar complex, the obstruction
is the $\delta^{(3)}$-coefficient of the shadow tower; at the level of
$L_\infty$ deformation theory, it is the ternary bracket
$\ell_3(r, r, r)$; at the level of Siegel automorphic theory, it is the
Fourier--Jacobi index-$1$ coefficient of
\((\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}})^{-1}\). The three descriptions name the
same cocycle.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Jacobi closure for the non-abelian K3 Yangian}
\label{subsec:k3yang-jacobi-closure}

Conjecture~\ref{conj:k3-super-yangian} proposed the envelope pair
$Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$ (ungraded) and its Hodge-parity
$\Z/2$-super-extension $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20))$ (programme-specific,
non-Kac) as the Mukai-preserving K$3$ Yangian; the Molev--Ragoucy
orthogonal $R$-matrix of Proposition~\ref{prop:mukai-K-matrix-and-YBE}
settles the $R$-matrix side for envelope~(I). The classical limit
presents a sharper question: does the bracket~\eqref{eq:k3-dca-bracket} of
Definition~\ref{def:k3-double-current-algebra},
extended to include non-abelian internal tensors, satisfy the Jacobi
identity when $\fg$ is a \emph{non-abelian} simple Lie algebra? The
$\gl_1$ case is handled by Proposition~\ref{prop:k3-heisenberg}; the
non-abelian case introduces the antisymmetry obstruction on the pair
$(a, i) \leftrightarrow (b, j)$ that the Mukai symmetry does not
automatically absorb. Jacobi closes in the Hodge-parity-twisted
Lie super structure $\fg^{\varepsilon}_{K3}$; the residual
obstruction for the non-twisted classical algebra is an explicit
central $3$-cocycle.

\paragraph{Step 1: Kac \texorpdfstring{$\osp(4 \mid 20)$}{osp(4 mid 20)} and \texorpdfstring{$\fgl(4 \mid 20)$}{gl(4 mid 20)} fail to host the Mukai form.}
Kac's orthosymplectic super-algebra $\osp(4 \mid 20)$ carries on
its odd part $\osp(4 \mid 20)_{\bar 1} = V_+ \otimes V_-$
($V_+ = \C^4$, $V_- = \C^{20}$) a \emph{symplectic} invariant form
induced from the $\mathrm{Sp}(20)$-factor of the even part, whereas
the Mukai pairing on $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}} \otimes \C$ is
\emph{symmetric} throughout. The symmetric--symplectic mismatch on
the odd sector means the Mukai form cannot extend to an invariant
super-pairing on Kac $\osp(4 \mid 20)$ compatible with the super-Lie
bracket. Moving to $\fgl(4 \mid 20)$ replaces the symplectic odd
form by a graded trace form: $\fgl(m \mid n)$ carries a supertrace
but no invariant bilinear form on the defining representation
beyond the supertrace itself, and the supertrace vanishes on the
odd part $V_+ \otimes V_-$ under Berezinian grading. Neither
Kac $\osp(4 \mid 20)$ nor $\fgl(4 \mid 20)$ is therefore a home
for the non-abelian Mukai-Jacobi question. The correct envelope is
either $\mathfrak{so}(4, 20)$ (ungraded) or its Hodge-parity
$\Z/2$-super-extension $\mathfrak{so}(4 \mid 20)$
(programme-specific, symmetric on both graded parts, non-Kac).

\paragraph{Step 2: Hodge-parity \texorpdfstring{$\Z/2$}{Z/2}-grading on \texorpdfstring{$H^*(S, \C)$}{H-*(S, C)}.}
The K3 cohomology $H^*(S, \C) = H^0 \oplus H^2 \oplus H^4$ carries
a canonical $\Z/2$-grading by \emph{Hodge parity}
\begin{equation}\label{eq:k3yang-hodge-parity-grading}
 \varepsilon(\alpha_i) \;=\;
 \begin{cases}
 \bar 0 & \text{if } \alpha_i \in H^0 \oplus H^4,\\[2pt]
 \bar 1 & \text{if } \alpha_i \in H^2.
 \end{cases}
\end{equation}
The cup product is $\Z/2$-graded commutative under $\varepsilon$
because $H^2 \cup H^2 \subset H^4$ and because the classical sign
$(-1)^{p \cdot q}$ on cohomology degrees
$(p, q) \in \{0, 2, 4\}^2$ coincides with
$(-1)^{\varepsilon(\alpha)\, \varepsilon(\beta)}$ on reduction
mod $2$. The Mukai pairing is symmetric under both the topological
degree reversal $H^p \otimes H^{4 - p} \to H^4$ and under the Hodge
parity: $\langle H^0, H^4\rangle_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ lives in the
$(\bar 0, \bar 0)$-sector, $\langle H^2, H^2\rangle_{\mathrm{Muk}}$
in the $(\bar 1, \bar 1)$-sector, and the cross-terms vanish for
signature reasons.

\paragraph{Step 3: graded transpose Mukai form on the super-algebra.}
Equip the classical K3 double current algebra
$\fg_{K3} = (\fg \otimes H^*(S, \C)) \oplus \C \cdot \mathbf c$ with
the $\Z/2$-grading inherited
from~\eqref{eq:k3yang-hodge-parity-grading}:
$|J^a_i| = \varepsilon(\alpha_i)$. Define the twisted bracket
\begin{equation}\label{eq:k3yang-twisted-bracket}
 [J^a_i, J^b_j]_{\varepsilon}
 \;=\;
 f^{ab}{}_c\, \sum_k \mu^k_{ij}\, J^c_k
 \;+\;
 (T^a, T^b)_{\fg}\,
 \langle \alpha_i, \alpha_j\rangle_{\mathrm{Muk}}\,
 \mathbf c,
\end{equation}
required to satisfy the super-antisymmetry
$[J^a_i, J^b_j]_{\varepsilon}
 = -(-1)^{|J^a_i|\,|J^b_j|}\,[J^b_j, J^a_i]_{\varepsilon}$.
The body of~\eqref{eq:k3yang-twisted-bracket} is identical in form
to the abelian bracket~\eqref{eq:k3-dca-bracket}; the
super-antisymmetry is compatible because the cup-product structure
constants $\mu^k_{ij}$ are graded-symmetric
($\mu^k_{ij} = (-1)^{\varepsilon_i \varepsilon_j}\mu^k_{ji}$) and
the Mukai pairing is symmetric
($\langle \alpha_i, \alpha_j\rangle
 = \langle \alpha_j, \alpha_i\rangle$).
The non-abelian factor $f^{ab}{}_c$ is antisymmetric in $(a, b)$,
and the total sign on the swap $(a, i) \leftrightarrow (b, j)$ is
$-1 \cdot (-1)^{\varepsilon_i \varepsilon_j}$, matching the
super-antisymmetry requirement.

\begin{theorem}[Bracket-term closure and explicit central defect for
\texorpdfstring{$\fg^{\varepsilon}_{K3}$}{g-eps-K3}]
\label{thm:k3yang-jacobi-closure-super}
Let $\fg$ be a finite-dimensional simple Lie algebra with invariant
trace form $(\cdot, \cdot)_{\fg}$ and structure constants
$f^{ab}{}_c$. Let $\fg^{\varepsilon}_{K3}$ denote the $\Z/2$-graded
vector space $(\fg \otimes H^*(S, \C)) \oplus \C \cdot \mathbf c$
equipped with the Hodge-parity
grading~\eqref{eq:k3yang-hodge-parity-grading} and bracket
$[\cdot, \cdot]_{\varepsilon}$
of~\eqref{eq:k3yang-twisted-bracket}, with central element
$\mathbf c$ of parity $\bar 0$. Let $\mathcal S(X, Y, Z)$ denote the
super-Jacobiator
\begin{equation}\label{eq:k3yang-super-jacobi}
 \mathcal S(X, Y, Z)
 \;=\;
 (-1)^{|X||Z|}\,
 [X, [Y, Z]_{\varepsilon}]_{\varepsilon}
 + (-1)^{|Y||X|}\,
 [Y, [Z, X]_{\varepsilon}]_{\varepsilon}
 + (-1)^{|Z||Y|}\,
 [Z, [X, Y]_{\varepsilon}]_{\varepsilon}.
\end{equation}
Let $\mathfrak z_{\mathrm{Muk}} := (\fg \otimes \C[\mathrm{pt}])
\oplus \C \cdot \mathbf c \subset \fg^{\varepsilon}_{K3}$
denote the \emph{Mukai-dual centre}; the subspace formed by
generators valued in the top class $[\mathrm{pt}] \in H^4(S, \C)$,
together with the scalar centre. Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item The projection of $\mathcal S(X, Y, Z)$ onto the
 \emph{complement} of $\mathfrak z_{\mathrm{Muk}}$, namely onto
 $\fg \otimes (H^0 \oplus H^2)$, vanishes identically for all
 homogeneous triples.
\item The projection of $\mathcal S(X, Y, Z)$ onto the Mukai-dual
 centre $\mathfrak z_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ equals the explicit
 Pillai--Gorelik--Kac $3$-cocycle
 \begin{equation}\label{eq:k3yang-central-defect}
 \omega^{(3)}_{\mathrm{Muk}}(X, Y, Z)
 \;=\;
 \sum_{\mathrm{cyc}(X, Y, Z)}
 (-1)^{|X||Z|}\,
 \bigl(\,
 f^{bc}{}_d\,f^{ad}{}_e\,\mu^\ell_{jk}\,\mu^m_{i\ell}\,
 T^e \otimes \alpha_m
 \;+\;
 f^{bc}{}_d\,(T^a, T^d)_{\fg}\,\mu^\ell_{jk}\,
 \langle \alpha_i, \alpha_\ell\rangle_{\mathrm{Muk}}\,\mathbf c
 \bigr),
 \end{equation}
 valued in $\mathfrak z_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ (the first summand lands in
 $\fg \otimes [\mathrm{pt}]$; the second lands in
 $\C \cdot \mathbf c$); this cocycle represents the
 $[\omega^{(3)}] \in H^3(\fg \otimes H^*(S, \C);\, \C)$ of
 Proposition~\ref{prop:k3yang-pgk-3cocycle}.
\end{enumerate}
$\mathfrak z_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ is an abelian ideal of
$\fg^{\varepsilon}_{K3}$: the first summand commutes with
$\fg \otimes H^{\geq 2}$ (since $[\mathrm{pt}] \cup H^{\geq 2} = 0$)
and pairs with $\fg \otimes 1$ only into the $\mathbf c$-direction.
The Hodge-parity twist is therefore \emph{necessary but not
sufficient}: it enforces graded antisymmetry of $[\cdot, \cdot]_
{\varepsilon}$ but does not cure the PGK defect, which is absorbed
by the minimal central extension $\widehat{\fg}_{K3}$
of~\eqref{eq:k3yang-central-extension} enlarged to identify the
$\fg \otimes [\mathrm{pt}]$ sector with a second abelian centre.
The extended algebra $\widehat{\fg}_{K3}^{\,\varepsilon}$ satisfies
the super-Jacobi identity identically modulo the cocycle structure
constants encoded in $\omega^{(3)}_{\mathrm{Muk}}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Write $X = J^a_i$, $Y = J^b_j$, $Z = J^c_k$. The central element is
absorbed in the central term and contributes trivially to the
double bracket. Expanding
$[X, [Y, Z]_{\varepsilon}]_{\varepsilon}$
using~\eqref{eq:k3yang-twisted-bracket},
\begin{align*}
 [J^a_i,\; [J^b_j, J^c_k]_{\varepsilon}]_{\varepsilon}
 \;&=\; f^{bc}{}_d\, \mu^\ell_{jk}\,
 [J^a_i, J^d_\ell]_{\varepsilon} \\
 \;&=\; f^{bc}{}_d\, f^{ad}{}_e\, \mu^\ell_{jk}\, \mu^m_{i\ell}\, J^e_m
 \;+\; f^{bc}{}_d\,(T^a, T^d)_{\fg}\, \mu^\ell_{jk}\,
 \langle \alpha_i, \alpha_\ell\rangle\, \mathbf c,
\end{align*}
and the central contribution from the inner bracket vanishes under
the outer bracket (since $\mathbf c$ is central). The super-Jacobi
identity~\eqref{eq:k3yang-super-jacobi} decomposes into a bracket
term (the cyclic sum of
$f^{bc}{}_d f^{ad}{}_e \mu^\ell_{jk} \mu^m_{i\ell}$) and a central
term (the cyclic sum of
$f^{bc}{}_d (T^a, T^d)_{\fg} \mu^\ell_{jk}
 \langle \alpha_i, \alpha_\ell\rangle$).

\emph{Complement closure (claim (i)).} Expand the bracket-term
piece of $\mathcal S(X, Y, Z)$, valued a priori in $\fg \otimes
H^*(S, \C)$. It takes the form $\sum_{e, m} c_{e, m}\, T^e \otimes
\alpha_m$ with
$c_{e, m} = \sum_{\mathrm{cyc}} (-1)^{|X||Z|}
 f^{bc}{}_d f^{ad}{}_e \mu^\ell_{jk} \mu^m_{i\ell}$.
For $\alpha_m \neq [\mathrm{pt}]$ (i.e.\ $m$ corresponding to
$H^0 \oplus H^2$), the coefficient $c_{e, m}$ vanishes by the
combination of the $\fg$-Jacobi identity and cup-product
associativity. Explicitly: if $\alpha_m \in H^0 = \C \cdot 1$, then
$\mu^m_{i\ell} \neq 0$ requires both $\alpha_i = \alpha_\ell = 1$;
the cyclic sum then collapses to $f^{bc}{}_d f^{ad}{}_e \cdot
\mathrm{cyc}(a, b, c)$, which is zero by the Lie-Jacobi. If
$\alpha_m \in H^2$, then $\mu^m_{i\ell}$ forces one of
$\{\alpha_i, \alpha_\ell\} = 1$ (cup with identity), reducing the
relation again to the pure $\fg$-Jacobi. This establishes~(i).

\emph{Mukai-dual centre defect (claim (ii)).} The $\fg \otimes
[\mathrm{pt}]$-component of $\mathcal S(X, Y, Z)$ arises when
$\mu^m_{i\ell}$ with $\alpha_m = [\mathrm{pt}]$ produces the
nontrivial cup product $\alpha_i \cup \alpha_\ell = \omega_{i\ell}
\cdot [\mathrm{pt}]$ between two middle classes $\alpha_i, \alpha_\ell
\in H^2$. On such triples the cyclic sum of
$f^{bc}{}_d f^{ad}{}_e \mu^\ell_{jk} \mu^m_{i\ell}$ with
$\alpha_m = [\mathrm{pt}]$ does not vanish: the Mukai pairing
$\omega_{i\ell}$ is symmetric while the Lie-Jacobi cycle produces
an antisymmetrising combination, leaving a nonzero residue in
$\fg \otimes [\mathrm{pt}]$. Direct symbolic evaluation at $\fg =
\fsl_2$ and cohomology basis $\{1, e_1, \ldots, e_{22},
[\mathrm{pt}]\}$ (Step~9 below) enumerates exactly $69$ unordered
triples carrying a nonzero $\mathfrak z_{\mathrm{Muk}}$-component,
each with sl$_2$-label multiset $\{E, F, H\}$ and cohomology
multiset in the $23$-element family
$\{(1, 1, [\mathrm{pt}])\} \cup \{(1, \alpha_i, \alpha_i) : 1 \leq i \leq 22\}$.
Of these $69$, the $44$ triples with $\omega_{ii} \ne 0$ and surviving
$(E, F, H)$-cyclic antisymmetrisation (namely $22$ middle axes $\times\,2$
independent $(E, F, H)$-assignments per axis) carry a \emph{simultaneously}
nonzero $\fg \otimes [\mathrm{pt}]$-residue (the cup-associator
defect) and a nonzero $\C \cdot \mathbf c$-residue (the trace-form
defect); the remaining $25 = 3 + 22$ triples ($3$ U-plane
$(1, 1, [\mathrm{pt}])$-permutations and $22$ diagonal
$(1, \alpha_i, \alpha_i)$-permutations for the third $(E, F, H)$-cycle)
carry only the scalar-$\mathbf c$ residue. The two residues are
cohomologous as $3$-cocycle classes on $\fg \otimes H^*(S, \C)$
via the Mukai Poincar\'e pairing $[\mathrm{pt}] \leftrightarrow 1$,
so the total cohomology class $[\omega^{(3)}] \in H^3(\fg_{K3}; \C)$ has
dimension $23 = 1 \cdot (1 + 22) = \dim H^3(\fsl_2; \C) \cdot (\dim H^0 + \dim H^2)$,
and the $69$ defect triples are the $23$ cohomology classes each
presented via the $3$ independent $(E, F, H)$-permutations.
This establishes~(ii).

\emph{Absorption by extended centre.} The subspace $\mathfrak
z_{\mathrm{Muk}} = (\fg \otimes [\mathrm{pt}]) \oplus \C \cdot
\mathbf c$ is abelian in $\fg^{\varepsilon}_{K3}$: for $X \in \fg
\otimes [\mathrm{pt}]$ and $Y \in \fg \otimes H^{\geq 2}$, the
bracket vanishes because $[\mathrm{pt}] \cup \alpha = 0$ for
$\alpha \in H^{\geq 2}$ and $\langle [\mathrm{pt}], \alpha\rangle_
{\mathrm{Muk}} = 0$; for $X \in \fg \otimes [\mathrm{pt}]$ and
$Y \in \fg \otimes 1$, only the scalar-centre contribution
$(T^a, T^b)_{\fg} \cdot \mathbf c$ survives (since $[\mathrm{pt}]
\cup 1 = [\mathrm{pt}] \in \fg \otimes [\mathrm{pt}] \subset \mathfrak
z_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ and the pairing $\langle [\mathrm{pt}], 1\rangle_
{\mathrm{Muk}} = 1$ lands in the centre component). Thus $\mathfrak
z_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ is an abelian ideal, and the quotient
$\fg^{\varepsilon}_{K3} / \mathfrak z_{\mathrm{Muk}} = \fg \otimes
(H^0 \oplus H^2)$ is a well-defined Lie algebra (concretely, the
$\fg$-valued Heisenberg--Lie algebra on the $(3, 19)$-signature
Mukai odd lattice). The super-Jacobi identity on
$\widehat{\fg}_{K3}^{\,\varepsilon}$ (with $\mathfrak z_{\mathrm
{Muk}}$ declared abelian, bracket on complement as in
\eqref{eq:k3yang-twisted-bracket}, coupling between complement
and centre the PGK cocycle \eqref{eq:k3yang-central-defect})
holds by construction: the defect cocycle $\omega^{(3)}_{\mathrm
{Muk}}$ is absorbed as the structure constants for the pairing
between $\fg \otimes (H^0 \oplus H^2)$ and $\mathfrak z_{\mathrm
{Muk}}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Why the Hodge parity is forced]
\label{rem:k3yang-hodge-parity-forced}
The super-antisymmetry of the bracket on
$(\fg \otimes H^*(S, \C), [\cdot, \cdot])$ is compatible with only
\emph{one} $\Z/2$-grading on $H^*(S, \C)$ that interacts correctly
with cup-product graded commutativity: the Hodge parity of
\eqref{eq:k3yang-hodge-parity-grading}. The topological-degree
reduction mod $2$ of
$H^0 \oplus H^2 \oplus H^4 = H^{\mathrm{even}}$ is trivial
($\varepsilon \equiv \bar 0$); it does not generate a super-Lie
structure. The parity that distinguishes the middle cohomology
$H^2$, where the Mukai form of signature $(3, 19)$ lives (the
$+1$ from $H^0 \oplus H^4$ providing the remaining $(1, 1)$-piece),
is the only $\Z/2$-grading that both (a) makes $\cup$
graded-commutative and (b) separates the signature $(4, 20)$ into
a $(4, 20) - (1, 1) = (3, 19)$ odd part and a $(1, 1)$ even part.
This is the constraint that the orthosymplectic super-Yangian of
Conjecture~\ref{conj:k3-super-yangian} does not respect (it splits
$(4, 20)$ into $V_+ \otimes V_-$ using the K\"ahler polarisation,
not the Hodge polarisation).
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[Classical limit of the Mukai-twisted super-Yangian]
\label{cor:k3yang-twisted-classical-limit}
Assume $Y^{\varepsilon}_\hbar(\fg_{K3})$ is a Drinfeld-type
quantisation of $\fg^{\varepsilon}_{K3}$ with Mukai $r$-matrix
$r(u) = \hbar \cdot \Omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}/u$, where
$\Omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}
 = \sum_{i, j}
 (\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}^{-1})^{ij}\,
 (\alpha_i \otimes \alpha_j)$
is the Mukai Casimir on $H^*(S, \C)$. Its classical limit as
$\hbar \to 0$ is the Lie super-algebra $\fg^{\varepsilon}_{K3}$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3yang-jacobi-closure-super}, equipped with the
classical $r$-matrix structure
\[
 r_{\mathrm{cl}}
 \;=\; \sum_{a, i, j}
 T^a \otimes T^a \otimes
 (\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}^{-1})^{ij}\,
 \alpha_i \otimes \alpha_j.
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Expansion of the quasi-triangular structure
$R(u) = 1 + \hbar\, r(u) + O(\hbar^2)$ at first order in $\hbar$
yields the classical Yang--Baxter equation
$[r^{12}, r^{13}] + [r^{12}, r^{23}] + [r^{13}, r^{23}] = 0$, whose
$\fg$-factor is the classical $r$-matrix of $\fg$ and whose
cohomology-factor is the Mukai-dual Casimir
$\Omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}$. The super-Jacobi identity of
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3yang-jacobi-closure-super} is the infinitesimal
form of the cYBE on $\fg^{\varepsilon}_{K3}$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Step 4: the non-twisted classical \texorpdfstring{$\fg_{K3}$}{g-K3} Jacobi obstruction.}
If one declines the Hodge-parity twist and works with the plain
$\fg_{K3}$ (no super-structure), the super-Jacobi identity collapses
to the ordinary Jacobi identity and the sign
$(-1)^{\varepsilon_i \varepsilon_k}$ is replaced by $+1$. The
bracket term still vanishes by ordinary associativity of $\cup$
and the Lie-Jacobi of $\fg$. The central term, however, no longer
has a uniform sign structure: the cyclic sum becomes
\[
 C_{\mathrm{flat}} \;=\;
 \sum_{\mathrm{cyc}(a, b, c) \times (i, j, k)}
 f^{bc}{}_d\, (T^a, T^d)_{\fg}\,
 \mu^\ell_{jk}\,
 \langle \alpha_i, \alpha_\ell\rangle_{\mathrm{Muk}},
\]
which vanishes by the Lie-side ad-invariance as before. The
genuine obstruction arises at one level higher, in the
\emph{deformation} of $\fg_{K3}$ by the Yangian parameter $\hbar$:
the $\hbar^2$-level central term does not vanish in general and
measures the chiral Yangian non-triviality.

\begin{proposition}[Pillai--Gorelik--Kac central \texorpdfstring{$3$}{3}-cocycle on \texorpdfstring{$\fg_{K3}$}{g-K3}]
\label{prop:k3yang-pgk-3cocycle}
The $\hbar^2$-order obstruction to the Jacobi identity on
$\fg_{K3} \otimes \C[\hbar]/\hbar^3$, with $\hbar$-deformed bracket
$[\cdot, \cdot]_\hbar
 = [\cdot, \cdot] + \hbar\, \beta + \hbar^2\, \gamma$
and $\beta, \gamma$ the first two deformation classes, is the
cohomology class
\begin{equation}\label{eq:k3yang-pgk-3cocycle}
 \omega^{(3)} \;\in\; H^3\bigl(\fg_{K3};\, \C\bigr)
 \;=\; H^3(\fg; \C) \otimes
 \bigl(H^*(S, \C)^{\otimes 3}\bigr)_{\mathrm{Muk\text{-}sym}},
\end{equation}
where the right-hand factor is the Mukai-symmetric part of the
cohomology-triple. For $\fg$ simple of type $A_n$, $D_n$, or
$E_{6, 7, 8}$, the class $\omega^{(3)}$ is represented by the
explicit cocycle
\begin{equation}\label{eq:k3yang-pgk-cocycle-explicit}
 \omega^{(3)}(X, Y, Z)
 \;=\;
 \mathrm{tr}_{\fg}([X_0, Y_0]\, Z_0)
 \;\cdot\;
 \mathrm{Muk}^{(3)}(X_{\mathrm{coh}},
 Y_{\mathrm{coh}},
 Z_{\mathrm{coh}}),
\end{equation}
where $X = X_0 \otimes X_{\mathrm{coh}}$ etc.\ is the factor
decomposition and $\mathrm{Muk}^{(3)}$ is the Mukai cubic form on
$H^*(S, \C)^{\otimes 3}$ induced by
$\alpha \otimes \beta \otimes \gamma \mapsto
 \int_S \alpha \cup \beta \cup \gamma \cup \sqrt{\mathrm{td}(S)}$.
The class $\omega^{(3)}$ is nontrivial in $H^3$ whenever $\fg$ is
simple (so $H^3(\fg; \C) \neq 0$) and the Mukai cubic is nonzero
(which holds generically on $H^*(S, \C)$).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The deformation theory of Kac--Moody and double-current algebras
(Pillai 1978, Gorelik--Kac 2002, Gan--Ginzburg 2005) expresses
the Jacobi obstruction at $\hbar^2$ as the cup product of
$\beta \in H^2(\fg_{K3}; \fg_{K3})$ with itself in
$H^3(\fg_{K3}; \fg_{K3})$. For $\fg_{K3} = \fg \otimes R$ with
$R = H^*(S, \C)$, the Chevalley--Eilenberg cohomology factorises
by the Hochschild--Kostant--Rosenberg theorem on smooth affine
varieties (applied to the formal neighbourhood of the K3 surface
in its deformation theory):
$H^\bullet(\fg_{K3}; \C)
 \cong H^\bullet(\fg; \C) \otimes H^\bullet(R; \C)$,
and the obstruction lives in the degree-$3$ Mukai-symmetric piece.
The trace-pairing factor captures the Lie-side Jacobi identity
and the cohomology-cubic factor captures the associativity
failure of the $\hbar$-deformed Mukai form.

Explicit computation at $\fg = \fsl_2$ and
$H^*(S, \C) = \C\langle 1, e_1, \ldots, e_{22}, [\mathrm{pt}]\rangle$
with Mukai form
$\mathrm{diag}(+1, +1, \ldots, -1, \ldots, +1)$ (signature
$(4, 20)$, cup product
$e_i \cup e_j = \omega^{ij}\, [\mathrm{pt}]$,
$1 \cup [\mathrm{pt}] = [\mathrm{pt}]$) yields
\[
 \omega^{(3)}([E, F], H, e_i \otimes e_j \otimes 1)
 \;=\; 2\, \omega^{ij} \;\neq\; 0,
\]
confirming the non-triviality of the class. For $\fg$ of higher
rank the same calculation applies componentwise along each
$\fsl_2$-triple.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Step 5: central extension \texorpdfstring{$\widehat{\fg}_{K3}$}{widehatg-K3}.}
Proposition~\ref{prop:k3yang-pgk-3cocycle} identifies the
obstruction as a genuine cohomology class in $H^3$, not a gauge
artefact. The canonical resolution is to enlarge $\fg_{K3}$ by a
two-dimensional centre
$\C \cdot \mathbf c \oplus \C \cdot \mathbf c'$ with the second
generator $\mathbf c'$ absorbing the cocycle:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:k3yang-central-extension}
 \widehat{\fg}_{K3}
 \;=\;
 (\fg \otimes H^*(S, \C))
 \;\oplus\;
 \C \cdot \mathbf c
 \;\oplus\;
 \C \cdot \mathbf c',
\end{equation}
with bracket
\[
 [J^a_i,\; J^b_j]_{\widehat{}}
 \;=\;
 [J^a_i, J^b_j]
 \;+\;
 \omega^{(3)}(J^a_i, J^b_j;\, \cdot)\, \mathbf c',
\]
where the third argument of $\omega^{(3)}$ is contracted against a
canonical $\fg_{K3}$-invariant vector (the Mukai Casimir). The
extended algebra $\widehat{\fg}_{K3}$ satisfies Jacobi exactly; the
trade-off is that $\widehat{\fg}_{K3}$ is a \emph{genuine central
extension}, not a Lie algebra in the non-extended sense. In the
super-twisted presentation, $\widehat{\fg}_{K3}$ agrees with
$\fg^{\varepsilon}_{K3}$ up to the absorption of the second centre
into the grading-zero piece.

\paragraph{Step 6: the obstruction is not a gauge artefact.}
One might hope that the obstruction of
Proposition~\ref{prop:k3yang-pgk-3cocycle} can be killed by a
redefinition of the bracket
$\beta \mapsto \beta + [\alpha, \cdot]$ for some
$\alpha \in C^1(\fg_{K3}; \fg_{K3})$. The obstruction is the class
$[\omega^{(3)}] \in H^3$, and a cohomology class is gauge-invariant
by definition: no redefinition of the $\hbar$-expansion at order
$\hbar$ or $\hbar^2$ can kill a non-trivial class in $H^3$. The
$\fsl_2$-computation of the proof shows the class is non-trivial
whenever the Mukai cubic does not vanish, which holds generically.

\paragraph{Step 7: the trichotomy of resolutions.}
The non-abelian K3 Yangian problem thus admits three equivalent
resolutions:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item \emph{Hodge-parity super-twist} ($\fg^{\varepsilon}_{K3}$,
 Theorem~\ref{thm:k3yang-jacobi-closure-super}): Jacobi closes as
 a Lie super-algebra, paying the price of super-structure on the
 underlying algebra; the Mukai signature $(4, 20)$ splits as
 $(1, 1) \oplus (3, 19)$ with even and odd parts of appropriate
 signature.
\item \emph{Central extension} ($\widehat{\fg}_{K3}$,
 Proposition~\ref{prop:k3yang-pgk-3cocycle} together with Step~5):
 Jacobi closes as a Lie algebra (no super-structure), paying the
